Good call for the current stage. MongoDB-polled worker is the right MVP choice — no new infra, ops stays simple, and it can be swapped for BullMQ later when volume justifies Redis. Generating the full prompt now.

# FEATURE: Job Queue — MongoDB-Polled Background Worker
# Product: Internal / Backend Processing Pipeline
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Backend Worker
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: High

---

## Context and Decision Rationale

This worker uses a MongoDB-polled approach rather than BullMQ/Redis. Decision
rationale for the record:

| Criterion          | BullMQ (Redis)         | MongoDB Polled Worker   |
|--------------------|------------------------|-------------------------|
| New infra needed   | Yes (Redis)            | No                      |
| Implementation     | Medium                 | Low                     |
| Ops complexity     | Higher                 | Minimal                 |
| Job volume         | High throughput        | Low-medium (MVP fit)    |
| Retry logic        | Built-in               | Manual (built here)     |
| Priority queues    | Built-in               | Manual (built here)     |
| Migration path     | —                      | Extractable to BullMQ   |
| Current stage fit  | Over-engineered        | ✓ Correct               |

Migration note: the worker interface is deliberately abstracted so that the
polling implementation can be swapped for BullMQ with minimal changes to the
rest of the codebase when volume grows or Redis becomes available.

---

## Task Description

Build a MongoDB-polled background worker that claims, processes, and finalizes
jobs from a `jobs` collection. The worker runs as a long-lived process (separate
from the API server), polls for `QUEUED` jobs on a fixed interval, claims one
job at a time per worker instance using an atomic findOneAndUpdate, processes it,
and writes the result back to the job document.

- [ ] Create a `jobs` collection schema with a `status` lifecycle field:
      `QUEUED → CLAIMED → PROCESSING → COMPLETED | FAILED`
- [ ] Implement an atomic claim step using `findOneAndUpdate` with a
      `claimedAt` lease timestamp to prevent double-claiming across worker
      instances
- [ ] Implement a polling loop with configurable interval and graceful shutdown
      on `SIGTERM` / `SIGINT`
- [ ] Implement per-job retry logic: on failure, increment `attempts`; if
      `attempts < maxAttempts`, reset status to `QUEUED` with a
      `nextRetryAt` delay; if exhausted, set status to `FAILED`
- [ ] Implement a stale-claim recovery mechanism: a job that has been in
      `CLAIMED` or `PROCESSING` status for longer than a configurable
      `claimTimeoutMs` is automatically re-queued by the worker on next poll
- [ ] Expose a `registerProcessor(jobType, handlerFn)` interface so individual
      processing functions can be registered without touching the worker loop
- [ ] Write structured logs (JSON) at each lifecycle transition
- [ ] Add worker health-check output: log a heartbeat every N polls with queue
      depth counts by status

---

2. `src/services/jobService.ts` — `createJob` and `finalizeJob` already exist;
   `createJob` will need to set initial `status: "QUEUED"` at the end of the
   upload-complete flow (or a new `enqueueJob` call); confirm the current status
   value written there
3. `src/types/job.ts` — existing `ProjectStatus` and `JobStatus` enums; extend,
   do not duplicate
4. `src/app.ts` / `src/index.ts` — understand the existing entry point before
   creating a separate worker entry point; the worker must NOT be started inside
   the API server process
5. `package.json` — confirm whether a process manager (`pm2`, `ts-node`, `tsx`)
   is already in use so the worker start command matches the project pattern
6. `.env` / `.env.example` — understand existing env var patterns before adding
   new ones

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Extend the Job Schema

In `src/models/job.model.ts`, add the following fields if they are not already
present. Do not remove any existing fields.

```typescript
// Add to JobSchema:
jobType: { type: String, required: true, default: "CAPTURE_PROCESSING" },
status: {
  type: String,
  enum: ["QUEUED", "CLAIMED", "PROCESSING", "COMPLETED", "FAILED"],
  default: "QUEUED",
  index: true,   // critical — polling queries filter on this field
},
priority: { type: Number, default: 0 },       // higher = picked first
attempts: { type: Number, default: 0 },
maxAttempts: { type: Number, default: 3 },
lastError: { type: String, default: null },
claimedAt: { type: Date, default: null },
claimedBy: { type: String, default: null },   // worker instance ID
nextRetryAt: { type: Date, default: null },
startedAt: { type: Date, default: null },
completedAt: { type: Date, default: null },
result: { type: Schema.Types.Mixed, default: null },
```

Add a **compound index** for the polling query:

```typescript
JobSchema.index({ status: 1, priority: -1, nextRetryAt: 1, createdAt: 1 });
```

This index covers the exact query shape used in the claim step (Step 3) and
prevents full collection scans on every poll. Add it in the model file, not
inline in the worker.

### Step 2: Create `src/worker/workerTypes.ts`

Define the shared types for the worker abstraction:

```typescript
export type JobStatus =
  | "QUEUED"
  | "CLAIMED"
  | "PROCESSING"
  | "COMPLETED"
  | "FAILED";

export interface JobDocument {
  _id: string;
  jobType: string;
  status: JobStatus;
  priority: number;
  attempts: number;
  maxAttempts: number;
  projectId: string;
  payload: Record<string, unknown>;   // job-type-specific input data
  result: Record<string, unknown> | null;
  lastError: string | null;
  claimedAt: Date | null;
  claimedBy: string | null;
  nextRetryAt: Date | null;
  startedAt: Date | null;
  completedAt: Date | null;
  createdAt: Date;
}

export type JobProcessor<T = Record<string, unknown>> = (
  job: JobDocument & { payload: T }
) => Promise<Record<string, unknown>>;

export interface WorkerConfig {
  pollIntervalMs: number;       // how often to poll for new jobs
  claimTimeoutMs: number;       // how long before a claimed job is considered stale
  concurrency: number;          // how many jobs to process simultaneously
  workerId: string;             // unique ID for this worker instance
  heartbeatEveryNPolls: number; // log a heartbeat every N polls
}
```

### Step 3: Create `src/worker/jobQueue.ts`

This is the atomic claim + re-queue module. No processing logic here — only
DB-level job state transitions.

```typescript
import { JobModel } from "../models/job.model";
import type { JobDocument } from "./workerTypes";

/**
 * Atomically claim the next available job.
 * Uses findOneAndUpdate so only one worker instance can claim a given job
 * even when multiple workers poll simultaneously.
 */
export async function claimNextJob(
  workerId: string,
  claimTimeoutMs: number
): Promise<JobDocument | null> {
  const staleThreshold = new Date(Date.now() - claimTimeoutMs);
  const now = new Date();

  return JobModel.findOneAndUpdate(
    {
      $or: [
        // Normal: unclaimed QUEUED job ready to run
        {
          status: "QUEUED",
          $or: [{ nextRetryAt: null }, { nextRetryAt: { $lte: now } }],
        },
        // Recovery: stale CLAIMED or PROCESSING job whose lease expired
        {
          status: { $in: ["CLAIMED", "PROCESSING"] },
          claimedAt: { $lte: staleThreshold },
        },
      ],
    },
    {
      $set: { status: "CLAIMED", claimedAt: now, claimedBy: workerId },
    },
    {
      sort: { priority: -1, nextRetryAt: 1, createdAt: 1 },
      new: true,
      lean: true,
    }
  ) as JobDocument | null;
}

export async function markProcessing(jobId: string): Promise<void> {
  await JobModel.findByIdAndUpdate(jobId, {
    $set: { status: "PROCESSING", startedAt: new Date() },
  });
}

export async function markCompleted(
  jobId: string,
  result: Record<string, unknown>
): Promise<void> {
  await JobModel.findByIdAndUpdate(jobId, {
    $set: { status: "COMPLETED", completedAt: new Date(), result, lastError: null },
  });
}

export async function markFailed(
  jobId: string,
  error: Error,
  attempts: number,
  maxAttempts: number
): Promise<void> {
  const exhausted = attempts >= maxAttempts;
  const retryDelay = Math.min(1000 * 60 * Math.pow(2, attempts - 1), 1000 * 60 * 30);
  // Exponential backoff: 1min, 2min, 4min ... capped at 30min

  await JobModel.findByIdAndUpdate(jobId, {
    $set: {
      status: exhausted ? "FAILED" : "QUEUED",
      lastError: error.message,
      claimedAt: null,
      claimedBy: null,
      nextRetryAt: exhausted ? null : new Date(Date.now() + retryDelay),
    },
    $inc: { attempts: 1 },
  });
}

export async function getQueueDepth(): Promise<Record<string, number>> {
  const counts = await JobModel.aggregate([
    { $group: { _id: "$status", count: { $sum: 1 } } },
  ]);
  return Object.fromEntries(counts.map((c: { _id: string; count: number }) => [c._id, c.count]));
}
```

### Step 4: Create `src/worker/processorRegistry.ts`

```typescript
import type { JobProcessor } from "./workerTypes";

const registry = new Map<string, JobProcessor>();

export function registerProcessor<T>(jobType: string, processor: JobProcessor<T>): void {
  if (registry.has(jobType)) {
    throw new Error(`Processor for jobType "${jobType}" is already registered`);
  }
  registry.set(jobType, processor as JobProcessor);
}

export function getProcessor(jobType: string): JobProcessor | undefined {
  return registry.get(jobType);
}

export function listRegisteredTypes(): string[] {
  return Array.from(registry.keys());
}
```

### Step 5: Create `src/worker/worker.ts`

The main polling loop:

```typescript
import { claimNextJob, markProcessing, markCompleted, markFailed, getQueueDepth } from "./jobQueue";
import { getProcessor } from "./processorRegistry";
import type { WorkerConfig } from "./workerTypes";

export async function startWorker(config: WorkerConfig): Promise<void> {
  const {
    pollIntervalMs,
    claimTimeoutMs,
    concurrency,
    workerId,
    heartbeatEveryNPolls,
  } = config;

  let running = true;
  let pollCount = 0;
  let activeJobs = 0;

  const shutdown = (signal: string) => {
    log("info", "Shutdown signal received", { signal, workerId });
    running = false;
  };
  process.on("SIGTERM", () => shutdown("SIGTERM"));
  process.on("SIGINT", () => shutdown("SIGINT"));

  log("info", "Worker started", { workerId, config });

  while (running) {
    pollCount++;

    if (pollCount % heartbeatEveryNPolls === 0) {
      const depth = await getQueueDepth().catch(() => ({}));
      log("info", "Worker heartbeat", { workerId, pollCount, activeJobs, depth });
    }

    if (activeJobs >= concurrency) {
      await sleep(pollIntervalMs);
      continue;
    }

    const job = await claimNextJob(workerId, claimTimeoutMs).catch((err) => {
      log("error", "Failed to claim job", { workerId, error: err.message });
      return null;
    });

    if (!job) {
      await sleep(pollIntervalMs);
      continue;
    }

    // Process asynchronously — do not await, so the loop can continue to claim
    // up to `concurrency` jobs in parallel
    activeJobs++;
    processJob(job, workerId)
      .catch((err) => log("error", "Unhandled error in processJob", { jobId: job._id, error: err.message }))
      .finally(() => { activeJobs--; });

    // Small yield to allow concurrent job claims up to concurrency limit
    await sleep(50);
  }

  // Drain: wait for active jobs to finish before exit
  log("info", "Draining active jobs before exit", { workerId, activeJobs });
  while (activeJobs > 0) {
    await sleep(500);
  }
  log("info", "Worker shut down cleanly", { workerId });
}

async function processJob(job: any, workerId: string): Promise<void> {
  const processor = getProcessor(job.jobType);

  if (!processor) {
    log("warn", "No processor registered for jobType", { jobId: job._id, jobType: job.jobType });
    await markFailed(job._id, new Error(`No processor for jobType: ${job.jobType}`), job.attempts, 1);
    return;
  }

  await markProcessing(job._id);
  log("info", "Job processing started", { jobId: job._id, jobType: job.jobType, attempt: job.attempts + 1 });

  try {
    const result = await processor(job);
    await markCompleted(job._id, result);
    log("info", "Job completed", { jobId: job._id, jobType: job.jobType });
  } catch (err: any) {
    const newAttempts = job.attempts + 1;
    await markFailed(job._id, err, newAttempts, job.maxAttempts);
    log("error", "Job failed", {
      jobId: job._id,
      jobType: job.jobType,
      attempt: newAttempts,
      maxAttempts: job.maxAttempts,
      error: err.message,
      willRetry: newAttempts < job.maxAttempts,
    });
  }
}

function log(level: "info" | "warn" | "error", message: string, meta: Record<string, unknown> = {}): void {
  console[level](JSON.stringify({ timestamp: new Date().toISOString(), level, message, ...meta }));
}

function sleep(ms: number): Promise<void> {
  return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
}
```

### Step 6: Create the Worker Entry Point `src/worker/index.ts`

This file bootstraps MongoDB, registers all processors, and starts the loop.
It is a separate process — never imported by the API server.

```typescript
import mongoose from "mongoose";
import { startWorker } from "./worker";
import { registerProcessor } from "./processorRegistry";
import { captureProcessingProcessor } from "./processors/captureProcessingProcessor";

const WORKER_CONFIG = {
  pollIntervalMs: Number(process.env.WORKER_POLL_INTERVAL_MS ?? 5000),
  claimTimeoutMs: Number(process.env.WORKER_CLAIM_TIMEOUT_MS ?? 120_000),
  concurrency: Number(process.env.WORKER_CONCURRENCY ?? 2),
  workerId: `worker-${process.env.HOSTNAME ?? "local"}-${process.pid}`,
  heartbeatEveryNPolls: Number(process.env.WORKER_HEARTBEAT_EVERY_N_POLLS ?? 20),
};

async function main() {
  await mongoose.connect(process.env.MONGO_URI!);
  console.log(JSON.stringify({ level: "info", message: "MongoDB connected", workerId: WORKER_CONFIG.workerId }));

  registerProcessor("CAPTURE_PROCESSING", captureProcessingProcessor);
  // Register additional processors here as new job types are added

  await startWorker(WORKER_CONFIG);
}

main().catch((err) => {
  console.error(JSON.stringify({ level: "fatal", message: "Worker crashed", error: err.message }));
  process.exit(1);
});
```

```typescript
interface CaptureProcessingPayload {

export const captureProcessingProcessor: JobProcessor<CaptureProcessingPayload> = async (job) => {

  // TODO: implement capture processing pipeline
  // This stub exists to verify the worker loop, claim mechanics,
  // retry logic, and processor registry work end-to-end before
  // real processing logic is added.
  console.log(JSON.stringify({
    level: "info",
    message: "captureProcessingProcessor stub invoked",
    projectId,
    jobId,
    fileCount: files.length,

  return { processed: true, fileCount: files.length };
};
```

## API / Integration Contract

**Enqueuing a job** (called from `jobService.ts` after upload finalize):

```typescript
import { JobModel } from "../models/job.model";

await JobModel.create({
  jobType: "CAPTURE_PROCESSING",
  status: "QUEUED",
  priority: 0,
  projectId: project._id,
  payload: {
    projectId: project._id.toString(),
    jobId: job._id.toString(),
    files: job.files.map((f) => ({
      fileId: f.fileId,
      s3Key: f.s3Key,
      fileType: f.fileType,
      levelId: f.levelId,
    })),
  },
  maxAttempts: 3,
});
```

This call must be added to `jobService.finalizeJob` after the project status
transitions to `"PROCESSING"` (from the P6 status-lifecycle task). The worker
then picks up the queued job independently.

---

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT start the worker loop inside `src/app.ts` or `src/index.ts` — it must
  run as a separate process to avoid blocking the API event loop and to allow
  independent scaling/restart
- Do NOT add Redis, BullMQ, or any new infrastructure dependency — the entire
  implementation must run on the existing MongoDB connection
- Do NOT modify the `GET /jobs/:jobId/upload-urls` route or the manifest
  validation logic from the P6 Backend track — this task only adds the worker
  that consumes finalized jobs
- Do NOT use `setInterval` for the polling loop — use the explicit `while +
  sleep` pattern defined in Step 5 so graceful shutdown can drain correctly;
  `setInterval` does not drain cleanly on `SIGTERM`
- Do NOT make `claimNextJob` non-atomic — the `findOneAndUpdate` pattern is the
  entire correctness guarantee for multi-instance safety; replacing it with a
  separate `findOne` then `updateOne` introduces a race condition

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Two worker instances poll simultaneously for the same job → the atomic
      `findOneAndUpdate` ensures only one wins; the other gets `null` and sleeps
      until next poll — verify this works with `concurrency: 2` running two
      worker instances locally
- [ ] A processor throws synchronously (not async) → wrap the `await processor(job)`
      call in a try/catch that also catches sync throws; TypeScript async functions
      automatically wrap sync throws in a rejected promise, but validate this
      behaviorally in tests
- [ ] Worker process is killed mid-processing (`kill -9`, OOM, crash) → the job
      remains in `CLAIMED` or `PROCESSING` status; on the next worker startup,
      the stale-claim recovery in `claimNextJob` reclaims it after `claimTimeoutMs`
      — set a realistic value (e.g., 2 minutes) to balance between too-eager
      re-queue and too-slow recovery
- [ ] MongoDB connection drops during processing → the `markCompleted` /
      `markFailed` calls will throw; the job remains stuck in `PROCESSING`; the
      stale-claim recovery reclaims it on reconnect — add a mongoose reconnect
      strategy (`{ serverSelectionTimeoutMS: 5000, heartbeatFrequencyMS: 2000 }`)
      in the worker entry point, not just the API server
- [ ] A processor hangs indefinitely (no throw, no resolve) → the job stays in
      `PROCESSING` forever until the stale-claim timeout fires; add a per-job
      timeout in `processJob` using `Promise.race` with a configurable
      `JOB_TIMEOUT_MS` env var that rejects after N ms if the processor hasn't
      resolved — implement this as a future hardening step and add a TODO comment
      in the code now
- [ ] `maxAttempts` is set to 1 on a job → first failure immediately sets status
      to `FAILED` with no retry — verify `exhausted = attempts >= maxAttempts`
      evaluates correctly for `attempts: 0, maxAttempts: 1` after `$inc: 1` (new
      attempts value is 1, which equals maxAttempts — correct behavior)
- [ ] Job with unregistered `jobType` arrives in the queue → `getProcessor`
      returns `undefined`; the worker logs a warning and calls `markFailed` with
      `maxAttempts: 1` to prevent infinite retry of an unprocessable job type;
      alert on this in production logs — it indicates a deployment mismatch
- [ ] Queue depth grows faster than the worker consumes (backpressure) → the
      heartbeat log will show a growing `QUEUED` count; at MVP this is an ops
      alert, not a code problem; add a comment noting that increasing
      `WORKER_CONCURRENCY` or running multiple worker instances is the first
      scaling lever

---

## Constraints

- The `findOneAndUpdate` in `claimNextJob` must be a single atomic operation —
  do not split into find + update
- Retry backoff is exponential with a 30-minute cap —
  `Math.min(60_000 * 2^(attempts - 1), 1_800_000)` — hardcode this formula in
  `markFailed` with a named constant and a comment; do not make it configurable
  via env in this task
- Structured logs must be JSON on every line — no `console.log("string")` in
  the worker process; every log statement must go through the `log()` helper
  with `level`, `message`, and `meta`
- Worker entry point (`src/worker/index.ts`) must never be imported by the API
  server — enforce with a comment at the top of the file
- `concurrency` worker config default must be `2` — running a single-job-at-a-time
  worker at MVP is an unnecessary bottleneck; 2 concurrent jobs is safe for a
  single MongoDB worker without complex coordination

---

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Running `npm run worker` starts the polling loop and logs a structured
      `"Worker started"` JSON line with `workerId` and config
- [ ] A job document inserted directly into MongoDB with `status: "QUEUED"` is
      claimed, processed via the stub processor, and transitioned to `"COMPLETED"`
      within one poll cycle
- [ ] A job where the processor throws is transitioned to `"QUEUED"` (with
      `nextRetryAt` set) after the first failure and to `"FAILED"` after
      `maxAttempts` failures, with `lastError` populated on each
- [ ] Two worker instances running simultaneously do not double-process the same
      job — verified by running `npm run worker` in two terminals and inserting
      one job; exactly one `"Job completed"` log appears
- [ ] A job stuck in `CLAIMED` status for longer than `WORKER_CLAIM_TIMEOUT_MS`
      is reclaimed and re-processed on the next poll
- [ ] `SIGTERM` sent to the worker process causes it to stop polling, drain
      active jobs, and log `"Worker shut down cleanly"` before exit
- [ ] The heartbeat log appears every `heartbeatEveryNPolls` polls with a
      correct queue depth breakdown
- [ ] A job with an unregistered `jobType` is marked `FAILED` with
      `lastError` containing `"No processor for jobType"` — not retried
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` passes with no TypeScript errors in strict mode
- [ ] The compound index
      `{ status: 1, priority: -1, nextRetryAt: 1, createdAt: 1 }` is present on
      the jobs collection — confirmed via `db.jobs.getIndexes()` in Mongo shell

---

## Testing Instructions

1. Start the worker: `npm run worker:dev`. Confirm structured startup log.

2. Insert a test job directly in Mongo:
```javascript
   db.jobs.insertOne({
     jobType: "CAPTURE_PROCESSING",
     status: "QUEUED",
     priority: 0,
     projectId: "test-project-id",
     payload: { projectId: "test-project-id", jobId: "test-job-id", files: [] },
     attempts: 0,
     maxAttempts: 3,
     createdAt: new Date(),
   });
```
   Confirm `"Job processing started"` and `"Job completed"` appear in logs.
   Confirm job document transitions to `status: "COMPLETED"` with `completedAt` set.

3. Insert a job and patch the processor to throw on the first call:
   Temporarily modify the stub to throw `new Error("test failure")`.
   Confirm status goes `QUEUED → CLAIMED → QUEUED` (with `nextRetryAt` in the future)
   on attempt 1, and `QUEUED → CLAIMED → FAILED` on attempt 3 (maxAttempts).
   Confirm `lastError` contains `"test failure"` and `attempts: 3`.

4. Start two worker instances in two terminals. Insert one job. Confirm only one
   `"Job completed"` log across both terminals.

5. Insert a job, manually set its `status: "CLAIMED"` and `claimedAt` to
   `new Date(Date.now() - 200_000)` (beyond the 2-minute default timeout).
   Confirm the next poll reclaims and processes it.

6. Send `SIGTERM` to the worker while it is mid-processing:
```bash
   kill -TERM <pid>
```
   Confirm `"Shutdown signal received"` is logged, the active job completes,
   and `"Worker shut down cleanly"` appears before the process exits.

7. Insert a job with `jobType: "UNKNOWN_TYPE"`. Confirm it transitions to
   `FAILED` in one attempt with the correct `lastError`.

8. Run `tsc --noEmit` — confirm zero errors.

9. Verify the compound index in Mongo shell:
```javascript
   db.jobs.getIndexes()
```
   Confirm the `{ status: 1, priority: -1, nextRetryAt: 1, createdAt: 1 }` index
   is present.

## Migration Path to BullMQ (Document for Future Reference)

When volume or Redis availability justifies the switch:

1. Keep `processorRegistry.ts` and all `processors/*.ts` files — they are
   queue-agnostic.
2. Replace `jobQueue.ts` with a BullMQ `Queue` + `Worker` wrapper that has the
   same exported function signatures (`claimNextJob`, `markCompleted`, etc.).
3. Replace `worker.ts` polling loop with a BullMQ `Worker` instance.
4. Keep the `JobDocument` shape in MongoDB for audit/history — BullMQ handles
   active queue state in Redis; the Mongo document becomes a record of truth.
5. The `registerProcessor` API is already compatible with BullMQ's processor
   pattern — no changes needed to processor files.

The abstraction in this task is specifically designed to make this migration
a two-file change (`jobQueue.ts` + `worker.ts`) with zero changes to
processors, the registry, or the API server's enqueue call.

---

## Assumptions

- Assumed: `jobService.finalizeJob` will be updated (in this or a sibling task)
  to call `JobModel.create({ status: "QUEUED", ... })` after the project status
  transitions to `"PROCESSING"` — if this enqueue call has not been added yet,
  the worker will run but find nothing to process until it is.
- Assumed: `tsx` or equivalent TypeScript runner is available as a dev dependency
  for `npm run worker:dev` — if not, adjust the script to match the pattern
  already used for running other TypeScript entrypoints in this project.
- Assumed: a single MongoDB connection string (`MONGO_URI`) covers both the API
  server and the worker — if separate connection strings are needed per environment,
  the worker entry point must be updated accordingly.

[[[[[ 9  ]]]]]


Generate a coding prompt for this task: Worker: pull QUEUED job → download manifest from S3 → validate → start processing — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend Worker


=============================================================================================================>>>>>>>





# NEW FEATURE: Processing Worker — Pull QUEUED Job → Download Manifest → Validate → Start Processing
# Product: maya-viewer (Backend Processing Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature (Backend Worker / queue consumer)
# Priority: Critical

---

## Task Description

Build the worker that consumes jobs enqueued by `POST /jobs/:jobId/finalize`. For each message it
pulls, the worker loads the job, downloads the job's manifest from S3, validates the bundle, transitions
the job to a processing state, and starts (or hands off to) the processing step. It must be safe under
at-least-once delivery: the same message may arrive more than once, and two workers may pull work
concurrently — neither may cause a job to be processed twice.

- [ ] Pull messages from the processing queue (the one `finalize` enqueues to)
- [ ] Load the job by id; guard that it is in a processable state (QUEUED) via a safe transition
- [ ] Download the job's manifest from S3 and validate the bundle (manifest present, counts/consistency)
- [ ] Transition the job QUEUED → PROCESSING atomically, then start processing
- [ ] Handle failures with retry/backoff and a dead-letter path; ack/settle messages correctly
- [ ] Be idempotent: duplicate deliveries and concurrent consumers never double-process a job

## Files to Inspect First

1. The queue mechanism `finalize` enqueues to (SQS / BullMQ / etc.) and any existing worker/consumer in
   the codebase — reuse the established consumer pattern, message ack/settle semantics, and config.
2. The `finalize` handler — confirm the exact enqueued message shape (jobId? full payload?), and what
   validation finalize already performed, so this worker does defense-in-depth rather than assuming.
3. The job model and status enum — confirm QUEUED and the processing/terminal states, and the allowed
   transitions. The QUEUED → PROCESSING move is the concurrency guard.
4. The S3 client and the canonical key utility — download the manifest via the same key scheme
   (`.../{jobId}/images/...` prefix + the manifest key) that the upload engine wrote and finalize listed.
5. The manifest format (per-file MD5 manifest) — confirm structure so validation reads the right fields.
6. The processing step this worker starts/hands off to — find its entrypoint/contract (may be a separate
   task; this worker only kicks it off — see Assumptions).

### Tech note (task-specific)
Use the project's configured queue and S3 clients — do not instantiate new ones. Use the existing job-
status enum; do not add new status strings. Validate the message payload with Zod (or the project's
validation) before acting on it.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Consume messages with correct settle semantics
Implement the consumer loop/handler against the existing queue abstraction. Respect the queue's visibility
timeout / ack model: only acknowledge (delete/settle) a message after the job is safely transitioned to
PROCESSING (or routed to DLQ). A crash before that must leave the message redeliverable, not lost.

### Step 2: Load job and guard the state transition (the idempotency gate)
Load the job by id (400/skip-and-DLQ if the payload is malformed; handle not-found — see Edge Cases).
Attempt an ATOMIC conditional transition QUEUED → PROCESSING (compare-and-set / conditional update). If
the job is already PROCESSING/terminal (a duplicate delivery or a concurrent worker won the race), do NOT
reprocess — settle the message as already-handled and stop. This conditional transition is the primary
defense against double-processing; do not rely on message-level dedup alone.

### Step 3: Download and validate the manifest
Download the manifest object from S3 under the job's key prefix. Validate the bundle: manifest exists and
parses, file entries are consistent with the job's `expectedFilesCount`, and (per the agreed scope) the
listed files exist in S3. Decide whether per-file MD5 verification happens here or in the downstream
processing step — this worker is the natural place for heavier verification that finalize deferred (see
Assumptions). On validation failure, do NOT start processing (see Step 5).

### Step 4: Start / hand off to processing
On successful validation, start the processing step (or enqueue/invoke the downstream processing handler
per the pipeline design). The job is already PROCESSING from Step 2. Record processing-start metadata
(timestamp, worker id) as the codebase convention allows.

### Step 5: Failure handling, retry/backoff, and DLQ
Classify failures:
- Transient (S3 timeout, transient DB error) → allow the message to be retried per the queue's redelivery/
  backoff (do not ack); cap redeliveries so a poison message doesn't loop forever.
- Terminal/validation failure (manifest missing/inconsistent, unrecoverable) → transition the job to a
  failed state, route the message to the dead-letter queue (or the project's failed-message path), and
  settle so it does not retry endlessly.
Ensure a validation failure after the QUEUED→PROCESSING transition does not strand the job in PROCESSING —
move it to failed so it isn't stuck (see Edge Cases).

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT re-implement the actual image/model processing algorithm here — this worker validates and starts
  it; the processing itself is separate (unless the pipeline design says otherwise — confirm).
- Do NOT modify `finalize`, the upload engine, the manifest format, or the key scheme — this worker consumes them.
- Do NOT introduce a new queue or S3 client, or a new job-status string.
- Do NOT ack a message before the job is safely transitioned or DLQ'd (no premature settle → lost jobs).
- Do NOT rely solely on message dedup for idempotency — the conditional state transition is required.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Duplicate delivery of the same job (at-least-once) → conditional transition fails on the second;
      settle as already-handled, no reprocess.
- [ ] Two workers pull the same job concurrently → exactly one wins the QUEUED→PROCESSING CAS; the other stops.
- [ ] Job already PROCESSING/completed/failed when pulled → do not reprocess; settle appropriately.
- [ ] Job not found for the message id → route to DLQ / settle per policy; do not crash the consumer loop.
- [ ] Malformed message payload → validate, DLQ the bad message, keep the consumer running.
- [ ] Manifest missing/unparseable in S3 → terminal validation failure → job → failed, message → DLQ.
- [ ] File count / manifest inconsistency (that finalize should have caught but S3 changed) → treat as
      terminal validation failure; do not process a bad bundle.
- [ ] Transient S3/DB error → do not ack; let redelivery/backoff retry; cap attempts → DLQ on exhaustion.
- [ ] Worker crashes after CAS to PROCESSING but before starting processing → job stuck in PROCESSING;
      handle via a redelivery that detects PROCESSING-but-not-started, OR a stale-PROCESSING reclaim policy
      (confirm approach — see Assumptions), so jobs don't get permanently stranded.
- [ ] Visibility timeout expires mid-processing-start → ensure the CAS + idempotency prevent a redelivered
      copy from starting a second processing run.
- [ ] Poison message that repeatedly fails → bounded redeliveries then DLQ; the loop never wedges.

## Constraints

- The worker must be idempotent under at-least-once delivery; the atomic QUEUED→PROCESSING transition is
  the required guard, not optional.
- Messages are settled/acked only after safe transition or DLQ routing — never before.
- Exactly one processing run per job even under duplicate delivery / concurrent consumers.
- Use configured queue + S3 clients and the existing job-status enum; no new infra or statuses.
- A job must never be left permanently stuck in PROCESSING due to a worker crash — a reclaim/redelivery
  path must exist (or be flagged if it doesn't yet).
- Validation failures must move the job to a failed state, not leave it mid-transition.
- No secrets in logs; log jobId/message context sufficient for debugging without leaking credentials.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Worker consumes a QUEUED job message, loads the job, and transitions QUEUED→PROCESSING atomically
- [ ] The manifest is downloaded from S3 under the correct job prefix and validated
- [ ] On valid bundle, processing is started/handed off and the job is PROCESSING
- [ ] Duplicate delivery does not double-process (second delivery detects PROCESSING and stops)
- [ ] Concurrent consumers processing the same message → exactly one processing run
- [ ] Malformed payload / job-not-found → DLQ (or policy path), consumer keeps running, no crash
- [ ] Manifest missing/inconsistent → job → failed, message → DLQ, no processing started
- [ ] Transient errors are retried with backoff; exhausted retries → DLQ; no infinite loop
- [ ] A validation failure after the transition leaves the job failed, not stuck in PROCESSING
- [ ] Messages are acked only after safe transition/DLQ (a mid-crash leaves the message redeliverable)
- [ ] Stale/stuck PROCESSING jobs have a reclaim/redelivery path (or the gap is flagged)
- [ ] No new queue/S3 client or job-status string introduced
- [ ] No TypeScript errors (`tsc --noEmit` passes); lint clean; unit tests green
- [ ] Code reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — processing algorithm, finalize, manifest, keys untouched

## Testing Instructions

1. Unit/integration with a mocked queue + S3: enqueue a valid job message, run the handler, assert the job
   transitions QUEUED→PROCESSING, the manifest was fetched and validated, processing was started, and the
   message was acked.
2. Deliver the same message twice; assert exactly one processing run (the second detects PROCESSING and settles).
3. Simulate two concurrent handler invocations for one message; assert one wins the CAS and only one run occurs.
4. Feed a malformed payload and a not-found jobId; assert DLQ routing and that the consumer keeps running.
5. Make S3 return no manifest / an inconsistent manifest; assert job→failed, message→DLQ, no processing started.
6. Inject a transient S3 error; assert the message is retried (not acked) and, after capped attempts, DLQ'd.
7. Simulate a crash after CAS but before processing start; assert the reclaim/redelivery path recovers the job
   (or document the gap if no reclaim exists).
8. Run `tsc --noEmit`, lint, and unit tests; confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- Assumed the standard backend stack (Node/TypeScript) with the same queue and S3 clients used by `finalize`,
  per AGENTS.md. The enqueued message shape (jobId vs full payload) is confirmed from the finalize handler.
- Assumed the job-status enum includes QUEUED, a PROCESSING state, and terminal completed/failed states, with
  QUEUED→PROCESSING as the guarded transition. Confirm exact names/casing.
- Assumed this worker VALIDATES and STARTS processing; the actual processing algorithm (photogrammetry / model
  build) is a separate downstream task. If this worker is meant to run the processing inline, that's materially
  larger scope — confirm.
- Assumed per-file MD5 verification was DEFERRED from finalize and this worker (or the downstream step) is where
  it happens. Confirm whether checksum verification belongs here (heavier, over ~90 files) or in the processing
  step. This directly affects Step 3's validation depth.
- Assumed the queue provides at-least-once delivery with visibility timeout and a DLQ (or the project has an
  equivalent failed-message path). If it is exactly-once/FIFO, the idempotency guard is still required but the
  concurrency assertions relax. Confirm the queue's delivery semantics.
- Assumed a stale-PROCESSING reclaim mechanism exists or is needed (e.g., visibility-timeout redelivery, or a
  reaper for jobs stuck in PROCESSING beyond a threshold). If none exists, a worker crash strands the job —
  flag this as a required companion piece rather than assuming it's handled.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Processing pipeline stages: QUEUED → PROCESSING → TEXTURING → OPTIMIZING → COMPLETED — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend Worker


==========================================================================================================================>>>>>>





# NEW: Processing Pipeline Stages — QUEUED → PROCESSING → TEXTURING → OPTIMIZING → COMPLETED
# Product: MayasabhaXR — backend (Node + TypeScript)
# Scope: New Feature / Backend Worker — processing pipeline state machine
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Backend Worker
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Build the backend worker's processing pipeline: a job (created when a capture's upload is ingested) progresses through the reconstruction stages QUEUED → PROCESSING → TEXTURING → OPTIMIZING → COMPLETED (with FAILED for errors). This task delivers the STATE MACHINE + orchestration + status persistence + worker mechanics; the actual heavy reconstruction work (photogrammetry, meshing, texturing, optimization) is DELEGATED to a reconstruction-engine adapter (stubbed/adaptered here).

Core responsibilities:
- **State machine:** the ordered stages + valid transitions (sequential; any stage → FAILED); no skipping.
- **Stage execution:** each stage runs its work via the engine adapter, then transitions; PROCESSING = reconstruction, TEXTURING = texturing, OPTIMIZING = optimization.
- **Status persistence:** the job's current stage (+ progress, timestamps, error) persisted (DB) atomically on each transition — the client polls this (status endpoint is a separate task).
- **Worker mechanics:** consume jobs from a queue; claim/lock so no double-processing; idempotent; crash-recoverable (re-queue/resume); concurrency across jobs.
- **Failure/retry:** a stage failure → FAILED with error; defined retry policy (from the failed stage vs restart).

- [ ] Define the stage enum + valid transitions (sequential + any→FAILED); enforce them
- [ ] Run each stage via the reconstruction-engine adapter (delegated/stubbed), then transition
- [ ] Persist status (stage, progress, timestamps, error) atomically on each transition (DB); client-queryable
- [ ] Worker consumes the queue, claims/locks jobs (no double-processing), idempotent, crash-recoverable
- [ ] Failure → FAILED with error; defined retry policy; cancellation stops the pipeline
- [ ] Concurrency across jobs; per-job ownership (project/user) preserved

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. P1 backend — the DB/ORM, job/project models, ownership (token `sub`), the service/route conventions; where a job record lives
2. The upload/ingestion task (this phase or adjacent) — where a job is created (upload complete → QUEUED); the ingested manifest + images the pipeline consumes
3. Any existing queue/worker infra (BullMQ/Redis, SQS, etc.) — the job queue the worker consumes; concurrency/locking primitives
4. The reconstruction engine (if specced) — the adapter this delegates to (PROCESSING/TEXTURING/OPTIMIZING work); stub if absent
5. The client status/polling task (separate) — the consumer of the persisted status (shape it to serve)
6. The PRD — the exact stage set/names, retry policy, and any progress semantics (authoritative)

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Stage state machine
- Define the stage enum: `QUEUED`, `PROCESSING`, `TEXTURING`, `OPTIMIZING`, `COMPLETED`, `FAILED`. Define valid transitions:
  - Forward: QUEUED→PROCESSING→TEXTURING→OPTIMIZING→COMPLETED (strictly sequential; no skipping).
  - Any active stage → FAILED (on error).
  - (Retry: FAILED → the retry entry point per policy.)
- Enforce transitions centrally (reject/guard invalid transitions).

### Step 2: Worker + queue consumption
- A background worker consumes jobs from the queue. On picking up a job: CLAIM/LOCK it (so no other worker processes it concurrently — e.g. a queue's visibility lock or a DB claim with a lease). Process through the stages. Support concurrency across DIFFERENT jobs.

### Step 3: Stage execution (delegated)
- For each stage, call the reconstruction-engine adapter to do the work (PROCESSING → reconstruct, TEXTURING → texture, OPTIMIZING → optimize). The engine work is DELEGATED — stub/adapter here (e.g. a call-out to an external process/service). On success → transition to the next stage; on error → FAILED.
- Optionally track intra-stage progress (0–100%) if the engine reports it.

### Step 4: Status persistence (atomic)
- Persist the job's status: current stage, progress, stage timestamps (start/end), error info (on FAILED), and the output artifact refs (on COMPLETED). Update ATOMICALLY on each transition (so a crash leaves a consistent, resumable status). The client status endpoint (separate) reads this.

### Step 5: Failure + retry + cancellation
- A stage error → transition to FAILED, capture the error (message/code/stage). Define the RETRY policy: re-queue from the failed stage (if the prior stages' outputs are preserved) or restart from QUEUED — confirm with the PRD. Bound retries (max attempts) to avoid loops.
- Cancellation (client cancels the job) → stop the pipeline, mark cancelled/terminal, clean up as needed.

### Step 6: Crash recovery + idempotency
- If the worker crashes mid-stage, the job's lease expires and it's re-queued/resumed. Make stage execution idempotent or resumable from the persisted stage (don't corrupt/duplicate outputs). A re-picked job continues from its persisted stage (or re-runs the current stage idempotently). Never advance the persisted stage before the stage's work is durably done.

## API / State Contract (TypeScript, illustrative)

```typescript
enum Stage { QUEUED, PROCESSING, TEXTURING, OPTIMIZING, COMPLETED, FAILED }
const NEXT: Record<Stage, Stage|null> = { QUEUED: PROCESSING, PROCESSING: TEXTURING, TEXTURING: OPTIMIZING, OPTIMIZING: COMPLETED, COMPLETED: null, FAILED: null };

interface JobStatus { jobId: string; projectId: string; stage: Stage; progress?: number; startedAt?: Date; stageStartedAt?: Date; error?: { stage: Stage; message: string }; outputRefs?: {...}; attempts: number; }

// worker: claim job -> for each stage: engine.run(stage) -> persist(next) ; on error -> persist(FAILED, error)
```
Rules: sequential transitions + any→FAILED (enforced); stage work delegated to the engine adapter; status persisted atomically per transition; worker claims/locks (no double-process), idempotent, crash-recoverable; bounded retry; cancellation terminal.

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT implement the actual reconstruction/photogrammetry here — delegate to the engine adapter (stub); orchestrate stages.
- Do NOT allow invalid transitions (skipping a stage, going backward except retry) — enforce the state machine.
- Do NOT advance the persisted stage before the stage's work is durably complete — a crash would skip work.
- Do NOT let two workers process the same job — claim/lock with a lease.
- Do NOT leave FAILED jobs without error info or an unbounded retry loop — capture the error; bound attempts.
- Do NOT block a request handler on the heavy work — it runs in the background worker (queue-driven).
- Do NOT couple the client to worker internals — the client reads the persisted status (separate endpoint).
- Do NOT lose ownership — the job stays tied to its project/user.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Stage engine error → FAILED with {stage, error}; retry per policy (bounded).
- [ ] Worker crash mid-stage → lease expires → job re-queued/resumed; stage re-run idempotently or resumed; no duplicate/corrupt output.
- [ ] Concurrent workers → a job is claimed by exactly one; others skip it.
- [ ] Retry a FAILED job → re-enter at the policy entry point (failed stage vs QUEUED); attempts incremented; bounded.
- [ ] Cancellation mid-stage → pipeline stops; terminal cancelled state; cleanup.
- [ ] Status query during each stage → reflects the current stage/progress accurately.
- [ ] COMPLETED → output artifact refs persisted (for download — separate task).
- [ ] Strict ordering → OPTIMIZING never runs before TEXTURING, etc.
- [ ] Idempotent re-processing → re-running a stage doesn't duplicate outputs or corrupt state.
- [ ] Job with missing/invalid ingested inputs → FAILED early with a clear error (not a crash).

## Constraints

- Transitions MUST be enforced: strictly sequential QUEUED→…→COMPLETED, any active stage → FAILED; no skipping/backward (except the retry entry).
- Stage work MUST be delegated to the reconstruction-engine adapter (stub here); this task orchestrates.
- Status (stage, progress, timestamps, error, outputRefs) MUST persist atomically per transition; the persisted stage MUST NOT advance before the work is durably done.
- The worker MUST claim/lock jobs (no double-processing), be idempotent, and be crash-recoverable (lease + resume/re-queue).
- Failures MUST capture error info; retries MUST be bounded; cancellation MUST be terminal + clean.
- Jobs MUST stay tied to their project/user (ownership).
- Concurrency across different jobs MUST be supported.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A job progresses QUEUED→PROCESSING→TEXTURING→OPTIMIZING→COMPLETED, with each stage delegated to the engine adapter and each transition persisted atomically.
- [ ] Invalid transitions are rejected; strict ordering holds; any active stage can go to FAILED with error info.
- [ ] The worker claims/locks jobs (no double-processing), processes concurrently across jobs, and recovers from crashes (re-queue/resume) without duplicating/corrupting outputs.
- [ ] FAILED jobs capture the error and retry per a bounded policy; cancellation stops the pipeline (terminal).
- [ ] The persisted status is client-queryable and accurate at each stage; COMPLETED persists output artifact refs.
- [ ] The persisted stage never advances before the stage's work is durably complete.
- [ ] Jobs remain tied to their project/user; heavy work never blocks a request handler.
- [ ] Unit/integration tests pass; the build/typecheck passes.

## Testing Instructions

(Unit tests for the state machine + integration tests with a fake engine adapter + fake queue/DB.)
1. Happy path: run a job → assert stages QUEUED→PROCESSING→TEXTURING→OPTIMIZING→COMPLETED, each persisted; output refs on COMPLETED.
2. Invalid transition (e.g. force TEXTURING before PROCESSING) → assert rejected.
3. Engine error in a stage → assert FAILED with {stage, error}; retry per policy; attempts bounded (no infinite loop).
4. Worker crash mid-stage (drop the worker) → assert the job is re-queued/resumed, stage re-run idempotently, no duplicate/corrupt output; persisted stage didn't advance past durable work.
5. Two workers + one job → assert exactly one processes it (claim/lock).
6. Concurrency: multiple jobs → assert independent progress.
7. Cancellation mid-stage → assert pipeline stops, terminal cancelled, cleanup.
8. Status query at each stage → assert accurate current stage/progress.
9. Missing/invalid inputs → assert early FAILED with a clear error, not a crash.
10. Typecheck + tests → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **Orchestration, not reconstruction:** Assumes the heavy reconstruction (photogrammetry/meshing/texturing/optimization) is delegated to an engine adapter (external process/service), stubbed here. This task is the stage state machine + worker + status. Confirm the engine interface.
- **Stage set:** Assumes QUEUED/PROCESSING/TEXTURING/OPTIMIZING/COMPLETED + FAILED (+ possibly CANCELLED). Confirm the exact set/names with the PRD.
- **Queue/worker infra:** Assumes a job queue + background worker (BullMQ/Redis, SQS, or similar) with claim/lock/lease + concurrency. Confirm the infra.
- **Job creation:** Assumes a job is created at upload ingestion (→ QUEUED); the pipeline consumes the ingested manifest + images. Confirm the ingestion handoff.
- **Status consumer:** Assumes a separate client-facing status/polling endpoint reads the persisted status; shape it to serve.
- **Retry policy:** Assumes bounded retries; re-enter at the failed stage (if prior outputs preserved) or restart — confirm with the PRD.
- **Ownership:** Jobs tied to project/user (P1 conventions).
- **Scope:** The stage state machine + worker orchestration + status persistence + failure/retry/cancel + crash recovery. NOT the reconstruction engine (delegated), NOT the ingestion endpoint (adjacent), NOT the client status endpoint (separate), NOT artifact download (separate).
- **Stack:** Node + TypeScript backend worker + queue + DB; reconstruction delegated. Adjust to the repo's structure.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Integrate photogrammetry engine (Reality Capture API / Meshroom / PolyCam API — TBD at this phase) — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend Worker


==========================================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>





# FEATURE: Photogrammetry Engine Integration — Engine-Agnostic Adapter Layer
# Product: Internal / Backend Processing Pipeline
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Backend Worker
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: High

---

## Context and Decision Rationale

The photogrammetry engine is TBD at this phase. Rather than hardcoding against
one vendor, this task builds an adapter layer with a common interface so the
engine can be swapped (PolyCam API → Reality Capture → Meshroom) by changing
one registered adapter, with zero changes to the worker loop, job queue, or
downstream pipeline.

Engine comparison for the record:

| Criterion          | PolyCam API         | Reality Capture API  | Meshroom (self-hosted) |
|--------------------|---------------------|----------------------|------------------------|
| Infra needed       | None (cloud API)    | None (cloud API)     | GPU server required    |
| Integration effort | Low                 | Medium               | High                   |
| Cost model         | Per-reconstruction  | Per-reconstruction   | Compute cost only      |
| Output quality     | Good (MVP fit)      | High                 | High                   |
| Output formats     | GLB, OBJ, USDZ      | OBJ, FBX, E57        | OBJ, PLY               |
| Control over mesh  | Low                 | Medium               | Full                   |
| Best for           | Fast MVP            | Premium quality      | Cost-sensitive scale   |
| Current stage fit  | ✓ First             | ✓ Second             | Future                 |

The adapter layer built here supports all three without rework. The first
concrete adapter (PolyCam) is implemented as a working reference. Reality
Capture and Meshroom adapters are stubbed with the correct interface.

Input assumption: the photogrammetry step receives a list of S3-hosted photo
files (from the capture job's `files[]` array, `fileType === "photo"`). The
worker downloads these to a temp directory before invoking the adapter. Depth
and metadata files, if present, are passed through but treated as optional
supplementary input — no adapter is required to use them.

---

## Task Description

- [ ] Define a `PhotogrammetryAdapter` interface that all engine implementations
      must satisfy
- [ ] Implement `PolyCamAdapter` as the first concrete adapter (working
      implementation — this is the MVP engine)
- [ ] Stub `RealityCaptureAdapter` and `MeshroomAdapter` with the correct
      interface (not yet wired to real APIs)
- [ ] Implement an `AdapterRegistry` that resolves the correct adapter at
      runtime via an env var `PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE`
- [ ] Implement a `PhotogrammetryService` that owns the end-to-end
      reconstruction flow: download photos from S3 → invoke adapter →
      poll for completion → download output → upload result to S3 →
      return structured result
- [ ] Wire `PhotogrammetryService` into the `captureProcessingProcessor`
      stub registered in the worker task
- [ ] Write per-phase structured logs and emit analytics events at each
      transition: `reconstruction_started`, `reconstruction_completed`,
      `reconstruction_failed`
- [ ] Handle all failure modes: adapter API error, timeout, unsupported
      file count, output format mismatch, S3 upload failure

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `src/worker/processors/captureProcessingProcessor.ts` — the stub from the
   job queue task; this is what gets filled in by this task
2. `src/worker/workerTypes.ts` — `JobDocument` and `JobProcessor` types; the
   processor signature must not change
3. `src/services/s3Service.ts` — existing S3 client, `buildS3Key`, and any
   existing download helpers; add download helpers here if missing rather
   than creating a new S3 module
4. `src/models/job.model.ts` — confirm `files[]` shape (`fileId`, `s3Key`,
   `fileType`, `levelId`) and `result` field where the output will be written
5. `.env.example` — understand env var patterns before adding new ones
6. `package.json` — confirm `axios` or `node-fetch` is available for HTTP
   calls to external APIs; confirm `fs/promises` usage pattern in the project

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define the Adapter Interface

Create `src/photogrammetry/PhotogrammetryAdapter.ts`:

```typescript
export type ReconstructionStatus =
  | "PENDING"
  | "PROCESSING"
  | "COMPLETED"
  | "FAILED";

export interface PhotogrammetryInput {
  jobId: string;
  projectId: string;
  // Absolute local paths to downloaded photo files.
  // The service layer handles S3 download before calling the adapter.
  photoPaths: string[];
  // Optional supplementary files (depth maps, metadata JSON).
  // Adapters may ignore these if not supported.
  supplementaryPaths?: string[];
  options?: {
    outputFormat?: "glb" | "obj" | "usdz" | "ply";   // adapter resolves best match
    quality?: "draft" | "standard" | "high";           // adapter maps to its own enum
  };
}

export interface ReconstructionOutput {
  // Adapter-internal ID for polling. Null for synchronous adapters.
  reconstructionId: string | null;
  status: ReconstructionStatus;
  // Absolute local path to the output file after download.
  // Null if status is not COMPLETED.
  outputPath: string | null;
  outputFormat: "glb" | "obj" | "usdz" | "ply" | null;
  // Any engine-specific metadata worth preserving.
  engineMeta: Record<string, unknown>;
  errorMessage?: string;
}

export interface PhotogrammetryAdapter {
  /** Human-readable engine name for logs. */
  readonly engineName: string;

  /**
   * Submit a reconstruction job to the engine.
   * For async engines (PolyCam, Reality Capture): returns immediately with a
   * reconstructionId and status PENDING.
   * For sync engines (local Meshroom): blocks until complete, returns COMPLETED.
   */
  submitReconstruction(input: PhotogrammetryInput): Promise<ReconstructionOutput>;

  /**
   * Poll for the status of a previously submitted reconstruction.
   * Only required for async engines. Sync engines can throw
   * NotImplementedError — the service layer will not call this if
   * submitReconstruction already returned COMPLETED.
   */
  pollStatus(reconstructionId: string): Promise<ReconstructionOutput>;

  /**
   * Cancel an in-progress reconstruction.
   * Best-effort — adapters may not support cancellation.
   * Must not throw — return void regardless.
   */
  cancelReconstruction(reconstructionId: string): Promise<void>;
}
```

This interface is the contract. Every adapter implements it. The service layer
only calls through this interface — never imports a concrete adapter directly.

### Step 2: Implement `PolyCamAdapter`

Create `src/photogrammetry/adapters/PolyCamAdapter.ts`:

```typescript
import axios from "axios";
import * as fs from "fs/promises";
import * as path from "path";
import type {
  PhotogrammetryAdapter,
  PhotogrammetryInput,
  ReconstructionOutput,
} from "../PhotogrammetryAdapter";

const POLYCAM_API_BASE = "https://api.polycam.ai/v1";
const POLL_TIMEOUT_MS = Number(process.env.POLYCAM_POLL_TIMEOUT_MS ?? 1_800_000); // 30 min
const POLL_INTERVAL_MS = Number(process.env.POLYCAM_POLL_INTERVAL_MS ?? 15_000);  // 15 sec

export class PolyCamAdapter implements PhotogrammetryAdapter {
  readonly engineName = "PolyCam";
  private readonly apiKey: string;

  constructor() {
    if (!process.env.POLYCAM_API_KEY) {
      throw new Error("POLYCAM_API_KEY environment variable is not set");
    }
    this.apiKey = process.env.POLYCAM_API_KEY;
  }

  async submitReconstruction(input: PhotogrammetryInput): Promise<ReconstructionOutput> {
    const { jobId, photoPaths, options } = input;

    // Step 1: Create a reconstruction session
    const session = await this.apiPost("/reconstructions", {
      name: `mayasabhaxr-${jobId}`,
      output_format: this.resolveOutputFormat(options?.outputFormat),
      quality: this.resolveQuality(options?.quality),
    });

    const reconstructionId: string = session.data.id;

    // Step 2: Upload all photos to the session
    await this.uploadPhotos(reconstructionId, photoPaths);

    // Step 3: Trigger processing
    await this.apiPost(`/reconstructions/${reconstructionId}/process`, {});

    return {
      reconstructionId,
      status: "PENDING",
      outputPath: null,
      outputFormat: null,
      engineMeta: { sessionId: reconstructionId, engine: "polycam" },
    };
  }

  async pollStatus(reconstructionId: string): Promise<ReconstructionOutput> {
    const response = await this.apiGet(`/reconstructions/${reconstructionId}`);
    const data = response.data;

    const status = this.mapStatus(data.status);
    if (status !== "COMPLETED") {
      return {
        reconstructionId,
        status,
        outputPath: null,
        outputFormat: null,
        engineMeta: { rawStatus: data.status },
        errorMessage: data.error ?? undefined,
      };
    }

    // Download output to temp path
    const outputUrl: string = data.download_url;
    const format = this.detectFormat(outputUrl);
    const tempPath = path.join(
      process.env.WORKER_TEMP_DIR ?? "/tmp",
      `${reconstructionId}.${format}`
    );
    await this.downloadFile(outputUrl, tempPath);

    return {
      reconstructionId,
      status: "COMPLETED",
      outputPath: tempPath,
      outputFormat: format,
      engineMeta: { rawStatus: data.status, downloadUrl: outputUrl },
    };
  }

  async cancelReconstruction(reconstructionId: string): Promise<void> {
    try {
      await this.apiPost(`/reconstructions/${reconstructionId}/cancel`, {});
    } catch {
      // Best effort — log but do not throw
    }
  }

  // --- Private helpers ---

  private async uploadPhotos(reconstructionId: string, photoPaths: string[]): Promise<void> {
    // Upload photos in batches of 10 to avoid overwhelming the API
    const BATCH_SIZE = 10;
    for (let i = 0; i < photoPaths.length; i += BATCH_SIZE) {
      const batch = photoPaths.slice(i, i + BATCH_SIZE);
      await Promise.all(batch.map((filePath) => this.uploadSinglePhoto(reconstructionId, filePath)));
    }
  }

  private async uploadSinglePhoto(reconstructionId: string, filePath: string): Promise<void> {
    const buffer = await fs.readFile(filePath);
    const fileName = path.basename(filePath);
    const FormData = (await import("form-data")).default;
    const form = new FormData();
    form.append("file", buffer, { filename: fileName, contentType: "image/jpeg" });
    await axios.post(
      `${POLYCAM_API_BASE}/reconstructions/${reconstructionId}/images`,
      form,
      { headers: { ...form.getHeaders(), Authorization: `Bearer ${this.apiKey}` } }
    );
  }

  private async downloadFile(url: string, destPath: string): Promise<void> {
    const response = await axios.get(url, { responseType: "arraybuffer" });
    await fs.writeFile(destPath, Buffer.from(response.data));
  }

  private resolveOutputFormat(requested?: string): string {
    const map: Record<string, string> = { glb: "glb", obj: "obj", usdz: "usdz", ply: "obj" };
    return map[requested ?? "glb"] ?? "glb";
  }

  private resolveQuality(requested?: string): string {
    const map: Record<string, string> = {
      draft: "low", standard: "medium", high: "high",
    };
    return map[requested ?? "standard"] ?? "medium";
  }

  private mapStatus(raw: string): ReconstructionOutput["status"] {
    const map: Record<string, ReconstructionOutput["status"]> = {
      pending: "PENDING",
      processing: "PROCESSING",
      complete: "COMPLETED",
      completed: "COMPLETED",
      failed: "FAILED",
      error: "FAILED",
    };
    return map[raw.toLowerCase()] ?? "PENDING";
  }

  private detectFormat(url: string): "glb" | "obj" | "usdz" | "ply" {
    if (url.includes(".glb")) return "glb";
    if (url.includes(".usdz")) return "usdz";
    if (url.includes(".ply")) return "ply";
    return "obj";
  }

  private async apiPost(path: string, body: Record<string, unknown>) {
    return axios.post(`${POLYCAM_API_BASE}${path}`, body, {
      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${this.apiKey}`, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    });
  }

  private async apiGet(path: string) {
    return axios.get(`${POLYCAM_API_BASE}${path}`, {
      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${this.apiKey}` },
    });
  }
}
```

### Step 4: Implement the Adapter Registry

Create `src/photogrammetry/AdapterRegistry.ts`:

```typescript
import type { PhotogrammetryAdapter } from "./PhotogrammetryAdapter";
import { PolyCamAdapter } from "./adapters/PolyCamAdapter";
import { RealityCaptureAdapter } from "./adapters/RealityCaptureAdapter";
import { MeshroomAdapter } from "./adapters/MeshroomAdapter";

type EngineKey = "polycam" | "reality_capture" | "meshroom";

const ADAPTER_MAP: Record<EngineKey, () => PhotogrammetryAdapter> = {
  polycam: () => new PolyCamAdapter(),
  reality_capture: () => new RealityCaptureAdapter(),
  meshroom: () => new MeshroomAdapter(),
};

let _instance: PhotogrammetryAdapter | null = null;

/**
 * Returns the configured photogrammetry adapter.
 * Resolves once and caches — adapters are stateless across jobs.
 * Set PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE env var to switch engines with zero code change.
 */
export function getPhotogrammetryAdapter(): PhotogrammetryAdapter {
  if (_instance) return _instance;

  const engine = (process.env.PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE ?? "polycam").toLowerCase() as EngineKey;
  const factory = ADAPTER_MAP[engine];

  if (!factory) {
    throw new Error(
      `Unknown PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE: "${engine}". Valid values: ${Object.keys(ADAPTER_MAP).join(", ")}`
    );
  }

  _instance = factory();
  return _instance;
}

/** For testing only — reset the cached instance. */
export function _resetAdapterInstance(): void {
  _instance = null;
}
```

### Step 5: Implement `PhotogrammetryService`

Create `src/photogrammetry/PhotogrammetryService.ts`. This owns the full
end-to-end reconstruction flow and is the only file the processor imports.

```typescript
import * as fs from "fs/promises";
import * as path from "path";
import { s3Client } from "../services/s3Service";
import { GetObjectCommand } from "@aws-sdk/client-s3";
import { Upload } from "@aws-sdk/lib-storage";
import { getPhotogrammetryAdapter } from "./AdapterRegistry";
import type { PhotogrammetryInput, ReconstructionOutput } from "./PhotogrammetryAdapter";

const POLL_TIMEOUT_MS = Number(process.env.PHOTOGRAMMETRY_POLL_TIMEOUT_MS ?? 1_800_000);
const POLL_INTERVAL_MS = Number(process.env.PHOTOGRAMMETRY_POLL_INTERVAL_MS ?? 15_000);
const TEMP_DIR = process.env.WORKER_TEMP_DIR ?? "/tmp";
const MIN_PHOTOS = Number(process.env.PHOTOGRAMMETRY_MIN_PHOTOS ?? 10);
const MAX_PHOTOS = Number(process.env.PHOTOGRAMMETRY_MAX_PHOTOS ?? 200);

export interface PhotogrammetryServiceInput {
  jobId: string;
  projectId: string;
  photoS3Keys: string[];          // s3Key for each photo file
  supplementaryS3Keys?: string[]; // optional depth/metadata files
  outputS3KeyPrefix: string;      // where to write the output, e.g. projects/{id}/jobs/{id}/output/
  options?: PhotogrammetryInput["options"];
}

export interface PhotogrammetryServiceResult {
  outputS3Key: string;
  outputFormat: string;
  reconstructionId: string | null;
  engineName: string;
  durationMs: number;
}

export async function runPhotogrammetry(
  input: PhotogrammetryServiceInput
): Promise<PhotogrammetryServiceResult> {
  const startMs = Date.now();
  const adapter = getPhotogrammetryAdapter();
  const sessionTempDir = path.join(TEMP_DIR, `recon-${input.jobId}`);

  log("info", "Photogrammetry started", {
    jobId: input.jobId,
    engine: adapter.engineName,
    photoCount: input.photoS3Keys.length,
  });

  // Guard: photo count
  if (input.photoS3Keys.length < MIN_PHOTOS) {
    throw new PhotogrammetryError(
      `Insufficient photos: ${input.photoS3Keys.length} provided, minimum is ${MIN_PHOTOS}`,
      "INSUFFICIENT_PHOTOS"
    );
  }
  if (input.photoS3Keys.length > MAX_PHOTOS) {
    throw new PhotogrammetryError(
      `Too many photos: ${input.photoS3Keys.length} provided, maximum is ${MAX_PHOTOS}`,
      "TOO_MANY_PHOTOS"
    );
  }

  await fs.mkdir(sessionTempDir, { recursive: true });

  try {
    // Phase 1: Download photos from S3
    log("info", "Downloading photos from S3", { jobId: input.jobId, count: input.photoS3Keys.length });
    const photoPaths = await downloadAllFromS3(input.photoS3Keys, sessionTempDir);
    const supplementaryPaths = input.supplementaryS3Keys?.length
      ? await downloadAllFromS3(input.supplementaryS3Keys, sessionTempDir)
      : [];

    // Phase 2: Submit to engine
    log("info", "Submitting reconstruction to engine", { jobId: input.jobId, engine: adapter.engineName });
    let output = await adapter.submitReconstruction({
      jobId: input.jobId,
      projectId: input.projectId,
      photoPaths,
      supplementaryPaths,
      options: input.options,
    });

    emitAnalytics("reconstruction_started", {
      jobId: input.jobId,
      projectId: input.projectId,
      engine: adapter.engineName,
      photoCount: input.photoS3Keys.length,
    });

    // Phase 3: Poll until complete (skip for sync engines that return COMPLETED immediately)
    if (output.status !== "COMPLETED" && output.reconstructionId) {
      output = await pollUntilComplete(adapter, output.reconstructionId, input.jobId);
    }

    if (output.status !== "COMPLETED" || !output.outputPath) {
      throw new PhotogrammetryError(
        output.errorMessage ?? "Reconstruction did not complete successfully",
        "RECONSTRUCTION_FAILED"
      );
    }

    // Phase 4: Upload output to S3
    const outputFormat = output.outputFormat ?? "glb";
    const outputS3Key = `${input.outputS3KeyPrefix}model.${outputFormat}`;
    log("info", "Uploading reconstruction output to S3", { jobId: input.jobId, outputS3Key });
    await uploadFileToS3(output.outputPath, outputS3Key);

    const durationMs = Date.now() - startMs;
    log("info", "Photogrammetry completed", { jobId: input.jobId, outputS3Key, durationMs });

    emitAnalytics("reconstruction_completed", {
      jobId: input.jobId,
      projectId: input.projectId,
      engine: adapter.engineName,
      outputS3Key,
      outputFormat,
      durationMs,
    });

    return {
      outputS3Key,
      outputFormat,
      reconstructionId: output.reconstructionId,
      engineName: adapter.engineName,
      durationMs,
    };
  } catch (err: any) {
    const durationMs = Date.now() - startMs;
    emitAnalytics("reconstruction_failed", {
      jobId: input.jobId,
      projectId: input.projectId,
      engine: adapter.engineName,
      errorCode: err.code ?? "UNKNOWN",
      errorMessage: err.message,
      durationMs,
    });
    throw err;
  } finally {
    // Always clean up temp files
    await fs.rm(sessionTempDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
  }
}

async function pollUntilComplete(
  adapter: ReturnType<typeof getPhotogrammetryAdapter>,
  reconstructionId: string,
  jobId: string
): Promise<ReconstructionOutput> {
  const deadline = Date.now() + POLL_TIMEOUT_MS;

  while (Date.now() < deadline) {
    await sleep(POLL_INTERVAL_MS);
    const status = await adapter.pollStatus(reconstructionId);
    log("info", "Reconstruction poll", { jobId, reconstructionId, status: status.status });

    if (status.status === "COMPLETED" || status.status === "FAILED") {
      return status;
    }
  }

  // Timeout — attempt cancellation (best effort)
  await adapter.cancelReconstruction(reconstructionId).catch(() => {});
  throw new PhotogrammetryError(
    `Reconstruction timed out after ${POLL_TIMEOUT_MS / 60_000} minutes`,
    "POLL_TIMEOUT"
  );
}

async function downloadAllFromS3(s3Keys: string[], destDir: string): Promise<string[]> {
  return Promise.all(
    s3Keys.map(async (s3Key) => {
      const fileName = path.basename(s3Key);
      const destPath = path.join(destDir, fileName);
      const command = new GetObjectCommand({
        Bucket: process.env.S3_BUCKET_NAME!,
        Key: s3Key,
      });
      const response = await s3Client.send(command);
      const body = response.Body as NodeJS.ReadableStream;
      const chunks: Buffer[] = [];
      for await (const chunk of body) chunks.push(Buffer.from(chunk));
      await fs.writeFile(destPath, Buffer.concat(chunks));
      return destPath;
    })
  );
}

async function uploadFileToS3(localPath: string, s3Key: string): Promise<void> {
  const fileBuffer = await fs.readFile(localPath);
  const upload = new Upload({
    client: s3Client,
    params: {
      Bucket: process.env.S3_BUCKET_NAME!,
      Key: s3Key,
      Body: fileBuffer,
    },
  });
  await upload.done();
}

export class PhotogrammetryError extends Error {
  public readonly code: string;
  constructor(message: string, code: string) {
    super(message);
    this.name = "PhotogrammetryError";
    this.code = code;
  }
}

function log(level: "info" | "warn" | "error", message: string, meta: Record<string, unknown> = {}): void {
  console[level](JSON.stringify({ timestamp: new Date().toISOString(), level, message, ...meta }));
}

function emitAnalytics(event: string, properties: Record<string, unknown>): void {
  // TODO: replace with actual analytics sink (Segment, Mixpanel, or internal events table)
  // For now: structured log with event type clearly marked for easy parsing
  console.log(JSON.stringify({
    timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
    type: "ANALYTICS_EVENT",
    event,
    properties,
  }));
}

function sleep(ms: number): Promise<void> {
  return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
}
```

### Step 6: Wire Into `captureProcessingProcessor`

Replace the stub body in
`src/worker/processors/captureProcessingProcessor.ts`:

```typescript
import type { JobProcessor } from "../workerTypes";
import { runPhotogrammetry } from "../../photogrammetry/PhotogrammetryService";

interface CaptureProcessingPayload {
  projectId: string;
  jobId: string;
  files: Array<{
    fileId: string;
    s3Key: string;
    fileType: "photo" | "video" | "metadata";
    levelId: string;
  }>;
}

export const captureProcessingProcessor: JobProcessor<CaptureProcessingPayload> = async (job) => {
  const { projectId, jobId, files } = job.payload;

  const photoS3Keys = files
    .filter((f) => f.fileType === "photo")
    .map((f) => f.s3Key);

  const supplementaryS3Keys = files
    .filter((f) => f.fileType === "metadata")
    .map((f) => f.s3Key);

  const result = await runPhotogrammetry({
    jobId,
    projectId,
    photoS3Keys,
    supplementaryS3Keys,
    outputS3KeyPrefix: `projects/${projectId}/jobs/${jobId}/output/`,
    options: {
      outputFormat: "glb",
      quality: "standard",
    },
  });

  return {
    outputS3Key: result.outputS3Key,
    outputFormat: result.outputFormat,
    reconstructionId: result.reconstructionId,
    engineName: result.engineName,
    durationMs: result.durationMs,
  };
};
```
