---

## Directory Structure After This Task
src/
photogrammetry/
PhotogrammetryAdapter.ts          ← interface (the contract)
PhotogrammetryService.ts          ← end-to-end flow (download → reconstruct → upload)
AdapterRegistry.ts                ← env-driven adapter resolution
adapters/
PolyCamAdapter.ts               ← working implementation
RealityCaptureAdapter.ts        ← stub
MeshroomAdapter.ts              ← stub
worker/
processors/
captureProcessingProcessor.ts   ← now wired to PhotogrammetryService

---

## Analytics Events

All three events are emitted inside `PhotogrammetryService`. Wire to the actual
analytics sink when available — currently written as structured JSON logs.

**`reconstruction_started`**
```json
{
  "jobId": "string",
  "projectId": "string",
  "engine": "PolyCam | RealityCapture | Meshroom",
  "photoCount": 42
}
```

**`reconstruction_completed`**
```json
{
  "jobId": "string",
  "projectId": "string",
  "engine": "string",
  "outputS3Key": "string",
  "outputFormat": "glb | obj | usdz | ply",
  "durationMs": 312000
}
```

**`reconstruction_failed`**
```json
{
  "jobId": "string",
  "projectId": "string",
  "engine": "string",
  "errorCode": "INSUFFICIENT_PHOTOS | TOO_MANY_PHOTOS | RECONSTRUCTION_FAILED | POLL_TIMEOUT | UNKNOWN",
  "errorMessage": "string",
  "durationMs": 15000
}
```

---

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT change `PhotogrammetryAdapter.ts` interface after it is defined —
  it is the stable contract all adapters depend on; breaking it requires
  updating all three adapters simultaneously
- Do NOT import `PolyCamAdapter`, `RealityCaptureAdapter`, or `MeshroomAdapter`
  directly anywhere outside `AdapterRegistry.ts` — all callers must go through
  `getPhotogrammetryAdapter()`
- Do NOT change the `JobProcessor` signature in `workerTypes.ts` — the
  processor must remain compatible with the worker loop
- Do NOT add polling logic inside adapter files — polling is owned by
  `PhotogrammetryService`; adapters only implement `pollStatus` (one call)
- Do NOT delete temp files inside the adapter — cleanup is owned by
  `PhotogrammetryService`'s finally block

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] `photoS3Keys` is empty or below `MIN_PHOTOS` → throw `PhotogrammetryError`
      with code `INSUFFICIENT_PHOTOS` before calling the adapter; the worker
      retry logic will not help here — this should be caught by manifest
      validation upstream; add a clear error message naming the actual count
      and minimum
- [ ] `photoS3Keys` exceeds `MAX_PHOTOS` → same pattern, code `TOO_MANY_PHOTOS`
- [ ] Two photo files share the same `path.basename(s3Key)` (e.g., two levels
      both have a file named `IMG_001.jpg`) → `downloadAllFromS3` writes to the
      same local path, overwriting the first; prefix the local filename with the
      `fileId` to guarantee uniqueness:
      `const fileName = \`${fileId}-${path.basename(s3Key)}\``
- [ ] S3 download fails for one photo (permission error, key not found) →
      `Promise.all` in `downloadAllFromS3` rejects; `PhotogrammetryService`
      catches this and rethrows; the worker retries the full job; add specific
      error logging that names the failing `s3Key`
- [ ] PolyCam API returns an unexpected `status` string not in the `mapStatus`
      map → falls through to `"PENDING"`, polling continues; add a warning log
      when an unmapped status is encountered so it is visible without crashing
- [ ] Reconstruction completes but `download_url` in PolyCam response is expired
      or returns a 403 → `downloadFile` throws an axios error; propagates as
      `RECONSTRUCTION_FAILED`; worker retries the full job including
      re-submitting to PolyCam (costs one more reconstruction credit)
- [ ] `WORKER_TEMP_DIR` fills up (large jobs on a small disk) → `fs.mkdir`
      may not fail, but `fs.writeFile` will; the finally block's `fs.rm` will
      still run to clean up partial downloads; add a disk-space check warning
      in the startup log of the worker (`df -h /tmp`)
- [ ] `PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE` env var is missing → defaults to `"polycam"` via
      `?? "polycam"` in `AdapterRegistry`; log the resolved engine name at
      worker startup so it is always visible in logs
- [ ] PolyCam API rate-limits photo uploads → `uploadSinglePhoto` will get a
      429 response; add a simple retry with 2-second backoff on 429 inside
      `uploadSinglePhoto` (max 3 retries); do not propagate a 429 as a job failure

---

## Constraints

- `PhotogrammetryService` must clean up temp files in a `finally` block
  regardless of success or failure — disk leaks on a long-running worker will
  fill `/tmp`
- `PHOTOGRAMMETRY_MIN_PHOTOS` defaults to 10 — PolyCam requires a minimum of
  ~20 photos for a usable reconstruction, but 10 is the guard for an obviously
  broken job; adjust upward once the minimum for acceptable quality is empirically
  determined
- Output must always be uploaded to S3 before `runPhotogrammetry` returns —
  the local temp file is deleted immediately after; downstream tasks must read
  from S3, never from local disk
- The adapter registry must be a singleton — do not instantiate a new adapter
  per job; adapters must be stateless across calls

---

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Setting `PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE=polycam` and running a job with ≥10 photo
      S3 keys results in: photos downloaded to temp, reconstruction submitted
      to PolyCam, polling until completion, output file uploaded to
      `projects/{projectId}/jobs/{jobId}/output/model.glb`
- [ ] The job document's `result` field contains `outputS3Key`, `outputFormat`,
      `engineName`, `reconstructionId`, and `durationMs` after completion
- [ ] Setting `PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE=reality_capture` causes `submitReconstruction`
      to throw `"RealityCaptureAdapter is not yet implemented"` — the worker
      marks the job `FAILED` after `maxAttempts`; no crash, no hang
- [ ] A job with fewer than `PHOTOGRAMMETRY_MIN_PHOTOS` photo keys is marked
      `FAILED` immediately with `errorCode: "INSUFFICIENT_PHOTOS"` — not retried
      (since retrying won't add photos); confirm `lastError` is populated
- [ ] Temp directory `recon-{jobId}` is deleted after job completion — confirmed
      by checking `/tmp` after the worker processes a job
- [ ] `reconstruction_started`, `reconstruction_completed` analytics events
      appear in structured logs for a successful job
- [ ] `reconstruction_failed` event appears with correct `errorCode` for a
      failed job
- [ ] Swapping `PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE` env var and restarting the worker changes
      the active adapter with zero code changes — confirmed by the startup log
      line showing the new engine name
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` passes with zero errors in strict mode

---

## Testing Instructions

1. **Unit test `AdapterRegistry`:**

PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE=polycam → returns PolyCamAdapter instance
PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE=reality_capture → returns RealityCaptureAdapter
PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE=unknown_engine → throws clear error
called twice → returns same cached instance
_resetAdapterInstance() + re-call → returns a fresh instance


2. **Unit test `PhotogrammetryService` with a mock adapter:**
   Create a `MockAdapter` implementing `PhotogrammetryAdapter` that returns
   `COMPLETED` synchronously with a known temp file path. Test:

happy path: photos downloaded, adapter called, output uploaded to S3,
temp dir deleted, correct result shape returned
photoCount < MIN_PHOTOS → throws PhotogrammetryError INSUFFICIENT_PHOTOS
before adapter is called
photoCount > MAX_PHOTOS → throws PhotogrammetryError TOO_MANY_PHOTOS
adapter returns FAILED → throws PhotogrammetryError RECONSTRUCTION_FAILED
poll timeout exceeded → throws PhotogrammetryError POLL_TIMEOUT;
cancelReconstruction called
S3 upload fails → throws; temp dir still deleted (finally block)


3. **Integration test with a real PolyCam API key (staging):**
   - Seed a test job in MongoDB with 20+ photo S3 keys pointing to real test
     images in the dev bucket.
   - Start the worker: `npm run worker:dev`.
   - Confirm the job transitions `QUEUED → CLAIMED → PROCESSING → COMPLETED`.
   - Confirm the output file exists in S3 at the expected key.
   - Confirm temp dir is cleaned up.
   - Confirm all three analytics events appear in stdout logs.

4. **Test engine swap:**
   - Set `PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE=reality_capture` and restart the worker.
   - Insert a test job. Confirm the job fails with `"not yet implemented"` in
     `lastError` and the engine name `RealityCapture` in logs.
   - Swap back to `polycam`. Confirm recovery.

5. **Test filename collision:**
   - Seed a job where two files from different levels both have `s3Key`
     ending in the same `basename` (e.g., `level_1/photo_001.jpg` and
     `level_2/photo_001.jpg`).
   - Confirm both files are written to distinct local paths and both are
     submitted to the adapter.

6. Run `tsc --noEmit` — confirm zero errors.

## Migration Path: Swapping to a Real Engine

To activate Reality Capture or Meshroom when ready:

1. Fill in the implementation inside the respective adapter file — no other
   files change.
2. Set `PHOTOGRAMMETRY_ENGINE=reality_capture` (or `meshroom`) in the env and
   restart the worker.
3. The registry, service, processor, and worker loop are completely unaffected.

This is the full scope of an engine swap — two files touched, one env var changed.

- Assumed PolyCam API accepts multipart photo upload per image (not a zip/archive
  bulk upload) — verify against actual PolyCam API docs and adjust
  `uploadSinglePhoto` if bulk upload is supported and preferred for speed.
- Assumed output format `glb` is the required downstream format for the
  maya-viewer and Mirage Menu pipeline; change `outputFormat: "glb"` in the
  processor to `"obj"` or `"usdz"` if the 3D pipeline requires a different
  format at this stage.
- Assumed `@aws-sdk/lib-storage` (`Upload`) is already a dependency for
  multipart S3 uploads from the upload-urls task — if not, add it:
  `npm install @aws-sdk/lib-storage`.
- Assumed `axios` and `form-data` are already in `package.json` — if not, add:
  `npm install axios form-data`.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: On completion: upload GLB to msxr-model-artifacts bucket — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend Worker



==============================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>




# NEW FEATURE: On Processing Completion — Upload GLB to msxr-model-artifacts Bucket
# Product: maya-viewer (Backend Processing Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature (Backend Worker)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

When a processing job completes successfully and produces a GLB model artifact, upload that GLB to the
`msxr-model-artifacts` S3 bucket, record its location on the job, and transition the job to its completed
state. This is the artifact-persistence step at the end of processing — distinct from the raw capture-image
bucket used during upload.

- [ ] Upload the produced GLB to the `msxr-model-artifacts` bucket under a deterministic, job-scoped key
- [ ] Set correct object metadata (content type `model/gltf-binary`) on the uploaded artifact
- [ ] Record the artifact location (bucket + key, or resolved reference) on the job record
- [ ] Transition the job to completed ONLY after the GLB upload is confirmed
- [ ] Handle upload failure without marking the job complete; make the step retry-safe/idempotent

## Files to Inspect First

1. The processing worker (from the prior task) — find where processing completes and produces the GLB
   output (local path / stream / buffer). This step runs on that success signal.
2. The job model and status enum — confirm the completed state and the field(s) for storing the artifact
   reference (existing `artifactKey` / `modelUrl`-type field, or a gap to add).
3. The S3 client and bucket configuration — confirm how `msxr-model-artifacts` is referenced (config/env,
   NOT hardcoded), and reuse the configured client. Confirm it is a DIFFERENT bucket from the capture-image
   upload bucket, with its own access/lifecycle policy.
4. The canonical key utility (the capture-image key scheme) — the artifact key is a SEPARATE scheme (GLB, not
   `images/{LEVEL}/`); decide whether to extend the utility with an artifact-key builder or add a parallel one
   following the same env/user/project/job convention (see Assumptions).
5. Any existing artifact/model-serving code (e.g., how maya-viewer later loads the GLB) — so the stored
   reference is in the form downstream consumers expect (raw key vs presigned URL vs CDN path).

### Tech note (task-specific)
Reference `msxr-model-artifacts` via configuration/env, never a hardcoded literal (so staging/dev use their
own artifact buckets). Use the project's configured S3 client. Set `ContentType: model/gltf-binary` on the
GLB object. Stream/upload the file rather than buffering a large GLB fully in memory where the SDK supports it.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define the artifact key
Build a deterministic, job-scoped key for the GLB, e.g. `{env}/{userId}/{projectId}/{jobId}/model.glb`
(align segments with the existing key convention). Deterministic-by-job means a retry overwrites the same
key rather than creating duplicates. Use a key-builder function (extend the key utility or add an
artifact-key builder), not an inline template.

### Step 2: Upload the GLB to msxr-model-artifacts
Upload the produced GLB to the artifact bucket at that key with `ContentType: model/gltf-binary` and any
required metadata (e.g., jobId tag). Confirm the upload succeeded (SDK success + optionally a HEAD) before
proceeding. Do not assume success.

### Step 3: Record the artifact reference on the job
Persist the artifact location on the job (bucket + key, or the resolved reference downstream expects). This
must be written such that the completed job unambiguously points at the uploaded artifact.

### Step 4: Transition to completed — ordering matters
Transition the job to its completed state ONLY after the GLB upload is confirmed and the reference is
recorded. Never mark a job completed with a missing or unwritten artifact. If the artifact reference and the
status live in the same record, write them together; if separate, order so status flips last.

### Step 5: Failure handling and idempotency
- If the GLB upload fails, do NOT complete the job; surface a retryable failure per the worker's retry/DLQ
  policy (the job stays in PROCESSING or moves to a failed/retry state per the pipeline design).
- Make the step idempotent: a retried completion (same job, same GLB) re-uploads to the same deterministic
  key (overwrite) and results in one completed job pointing at one artifact — no duplicate artifacts, no
  double-complete.
- If the job is already completed when this step runs again (duplicate), do not re-run destructively; treat
  as already-done.

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT run or modify the processing algorithm itself — this step consumes its GLB output.
- Do NOT upload the GLB to the capture-image bucket; it goes to `msxr-model-artifacts` (separate bucket).
- Do NOT hardcode the bucket name — use configuration so environments are isolated.
- Do NOT reuse the capture-image `images/{LEVEL}/...` key scheme for the artifact; use the artifact key scheme.
- Do NOT mark the job completed before the GLB upload is confirmed.
- Do NOT introduce a new S3 client or a new job-status string.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] GLB upload fails (network/S3 error) → job NOT completed; retryable failure per worker policy.
- [ ] Retry after a partial/failed upload → deterministic key overwrite; exactly one artifact, one completion.
- [ ] Job already completed when the step re-runs (duplicate delivery) → no destructive re-run, treated as done.
- [ ] Missing/empty GLB output from processing → do NOT upload a zero-byte artifact or complete the job; treat
      as a processing failure.
- [ ] Very large GLB → uploaded via streaming/multipart per SDK capability; no OOM from buffering in memory.
- [ ] Artifact-reference write succeeds but status write fails (if separate operations) → job not left
      "artifact present but status not completed" in a way that strands it; ordering/idempotency allows a
      clean retry to finish the transition.
- [ ] Wrong environment → artifact lands in the env's own artifact bucket (config-driven), never prod from staging.
- [ ] ContentType omitted by SDK default → explicitly set `model/gltf-binary` so downstream serving/CDN is correct.

## Constraints

- The artifact bucket name comes from configuration; `msxr-model-artifacts` is the prod value, not a hardcoded literal.
- The artifact key is deterministic per job (retry-overwrite-safe); duplicate completions never create duplicate artifacts.
- Job completion happens only after confirmed GLB upload + recorded reference — no completed job without a present artifact.
- The GLB object has `ContentType: model/gltf-binary`.
- Use the configured S3 client and existing job-status enum; no new infra or statuses.
- The step is idempotent under at-least-once worker delivery (consistent with the processing worker's idempotency model).
- No secrets in logs; log jobId/bucket/key context without leaking credentials.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] On processing success, the GLB is uploaded to the configured artifact bucket at a deterministic job-scoped key
- [ ] The uploaded object has `ContentType: model/gltf-binary`
- [ ] The artifact location is recorded on the job in the form downstream consumers expect
- [ ] The job transitions to completed ONLY after the upload is confirmed and the reference is recorded
- [ ] A failed GLB upload does NOT complete the job and surfaces a retryable failure
- [ ] A retried completion overwrites the same key → one artifact, one completed job (no duplicates, no double-complete)
- [ ] An already-completed job is not destructively re-run on duplicate delivery
- [ ] Missing/empty GLB output is treated as a processing failure, not a completion
- [ ] The bucket name is config-driven (staging/dev use their own buckets; verified)
- [ ] The artifact uses its own key scheme, not the capture-image `images/{LEVEL}` scheme
- [ ] No new S3 client or job-status string introduced
- [ ] No TypeScript errors (`tsc --noEmit` passes); lint clean; unit tests green
- [ ] Code reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — processing algorithm and capture-image bucket untouched

## Testing Instructions

1. Unit/integration with a mocked S3: on a simulated processing success with a sample GLB, assert the object
   is uploaded to the configured artifact bucket at the expected deterministic key with `model/gltf-binary`.
2. Assert the artifact reference is written to the job and the job is completed — and that completion occurs
   AFTER the upload (e.g., ordering/verified by mock call order).
3. Make the S3 upload fail; assert the job is NOT completed and a retryable failure is surfaced.
4. Retry completion after a failure; assert the same key is overwritten and there is exactly one artifact and
   one completed job.
5. Re-run the step on an already-completed job; assert no destructive re-upload and no double-complete.
6. Provide an empty/missing GLB; assert it is treated as a failure, not a completion, and nothing is uploaded.
7. Point config at a staging bucket; assert the artifact lands there, not in `msxr-model-artifacts` (prod).
8. Run `tsc --noEmit`, lint, and unit tests; confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- Assumed the standard backend stack with the configured S3 client, per AGENTS.md, and that this step runs
  within/after the processing worker on its success signal.
- Assumed `msxr-model-artifacts` is a distinct bucket from the capture-image upload bucket, with its own
  access/lifecycle policy, and is referenced via config (prod = `msxr-model-artifacts`; staging/dev differ).
  Confirm the exact config keys and non-prod bucket names.
- Assumed the artifact key is deterministic per job (e.g., `{env}/{userId}/{projectId}/{jobId}/model.glb`),
  making retries idempotent by overwrite. Confirm the desired key/filename and whether versioning (vs overwrite)
  is wanted — if artifact history must be retained, the key includes a version/timestamp and completion points
  at the latest.
- Assumed processing produces exactly one GLB per job. If a job can produce multiple artifacts (e.g., GLB +
  textures + thumbnail), this step generalizes to an artifact set with per-artifact keys — confirm the output shape.
- Assumed the job model has (or gains) a field for the artifact reference; if it must be added, that's in scope.
  Confirm whether downstream (maya-viewer) expects a raw key, a presigned URL, or a CDN path so the stored form matches.
- Assumed completion status/reference can be written consistently (same record or ordered writes with idempotent
  retry). If a transaction/outbox is available, prefer it; the hard requirement is "no completed job without a
  present artifact."
- Assumed the processing worker's idempotency/at-least-once model applies here too; this step re-asserts it for
  the artifact-upload/completion transition.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: On completion: generate quality_report.json + upload — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend Worker


===================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>



# NEW: On Completion — Generate quality_report.json + Upload
# Product: MayasabhaXR — backend (Node + TypeScript)
# Scope: New Feature / Backend Worker — completion artifact
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Backend Worker
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

When the processing pipeline reaches COMPLETED, generate a `quality_report.json` describing the reconstruction's quality and upload it to artifact storage so the client can fetch and display it. This is the server-side, OUTPUT-quality counterpart to the client's `capture_manifest.json` (which described the INPUT capture).

It AGGREGATES (does not fabricate): the reconstruction engine's quality metrics (coverage, vertex/face counts, texture resolution, alignment success, quality warnings) + the input-capture summary (from the ingested manifest/metadata: photos used/rejected, per-level, blur/exposure). It references the produced model artifacts, carries a schema version, and is uploaded durably to the job's artifact location.

RECONCILIATIONS (inferred structure is a STARTING POINT):
- **PRD is authoritative** for the exact report schema (fields, metrics, units, warning taxonomy). Reconcile to the PRD / AGENTS.md.
- **Input-quality summary MUST reconcile with the manifest/metadata** (the same photo/level stats, consistent representation).
- **Metrics MUST come from the engine** — omit/compute honestly what's available; NEVER fabricate a metric the engine didn't provide.

- [ ] Generate `quality_report.json` on COMPLETED: identity + reconstruction metrics + coverage/warnings + input summary + artifact refs + version
- [ ] Aggregate engine metrics + input stats (from the manifest); reconcile input summary with the manifest/metadata
- [ ] Upload the report durably to the job's artifact storage (retryable/idempotent); client-fetchable (via a separate endpoint)
- [ ] Reference the produced model artifacts (paths) so the client can locate them
- [ ] Define the failure policy (report failure blocks COMPLETED vs best-effort post-step)
- [ ] Deterministic assembly (same inputs → same report) + upload IO around it; no fabricated metrics

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The processing-pipeline state-machine task (this phase) — the COMPLETED transition where this runs; the job/status model
2. The reconstruction engine adapter — the quality metrics it emits (coverage, counts, texture res, alignment, warnings); the source of the report's metrics
3. The client `capture_manifest.json` builder + CapturePhotoMetadata — the INPUT representation the report's input-summary reconciles with
4. The ingestion task — the ingested manifest/metadata (input stats) the report aggregates
5. The artifact storage layer (S3/GCS/blob) — where the model artifacts + this report are stored; the durable upload
6. The client status/download tasks (separate) — the consumer that fetches the report
7. The PRD — the AUTHORITATIVE report schema + warning taxonomy + quality scoring

## Inferred Report Structure (RECONCILE WITH PRD — not final)

```json
{
  "reportVersion": "1.0",
  "jobId": "...", "projectId": "...", "generatedAt": "...",
  "reconstruction": {
    "vertexCount": ..., "faceCount": ..., "textureResolution": [w,h],
    "boundingBox": {...}, "fileSizes": {...}
  },
  "coverage": { "overall": 0.0-1.0, "warnings": [ { "code": "LOW_COVERAGE_BASE", "severity": "...", "detail": "..." } ] },
  "input": {
    "photosTotal": ..., "photosUsed": ..., "photosRejected": ...,
    "perLevel": [ { "levelId": "A", "photos": ..., "aligned": ... } ],
    "blur": {...}, "exposure": {...}
  },
  "qualityScore": ...,               // overall grade/score (if specced)
  "artifacts": { "mesh": "path", "textures": [...], "lods": [...] }
}
```
> Group/illustrative. The PRD governs the actual fields/metrics/units/warnings; align the input summary to the manifest/metadata.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Trigger on COMPLETED
- As the pipeline reaches COMPLETED (final step of / after OPTIMIZING), run report generation. Decide (Step 5) whether success is required for the COMPLETED transition or a best-effort post-step.

### Step 2: Gather inputs (aggregate, honest)
- Pull the engine's quality metrics (reconstruction counts, coverage, texture res, alignment, warnings) and the input-capture stats (from the ingested manifest/metadata: photo counts, per-level, blur/exposure). Only include metrics the engine actually provides — omit or compute honestly; NO fabrication.

### Step 3: Assemble the report (PRD-aligned, deterministic)
- Build the JSON per the PRD schema: identity, reconstruction metrics, coverage + warnings, input summary, quality score (if specced), artifact refs, `reportVersion`. Deterministic (stable ordering; same inputs → same bytes) for testability.
- Reconcile the input summary with the manifest/metadata (same stats, consistent shape).

### Step 4: Upload to artifact storage (durable)
- Upload `quality_report.json` to the job's artifact location (same store as the model artifacts), so the client can fetch it (via a separate endpoint). The upload must be durable/retryable and idempotent (re-generating overwrites consistently). Reference the model artifacts by their stored paths.

### Step 5: Failure policy
- Define + implement: if report generation/upload fails, either (a) retry (bounded) and, on persistent failure, mark the job COMPLETED-with-missing-report (the model is still usable) + flag, OR (b) treat the report as required and hold/flag COMPLETED until it succeeds. Confirm with the PRD; default to bounded-retry + best-effort (don't discard a completed model over a report hiccup), with the failure surfaced.

### Step 6: Purity + IO
- Keep the report ASSEMBLY pure/deterministic (inputs → report object); the metric reads + the storage upload are IO wired around it (injectable for tests).

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT treat the inferred structure as final — the PRD is authoritative; reconcile fields/metrics/units/warnings.
- Do NOT fabricate metrics the engine didn't provide — aggregate available metrics; omit/compute honestly.
- Do NOT diverge the input summary from the manifest/metadata — consistent representation.
- Do NOT omit the report schema version or the artifact references.
- Do NOT couple report generation to a request handler — it's part of the worker pipeline (on COMPLETED).
- Do NOT lose the report on upload failure — retry (bounded); handle per the failure policy.
- Do NOT discard a completed model over a report failure (default) — best-effort + flag, unless the PRD requires the report.
- Do NOT build a non-deterministic report — stable ordering; same inputs → same bytes.
- Do NOT include PII beyond what the PRD/client needs.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Engine provides partial metrics → include available; omit missing (no fabrication).
- [ ] Low-quality reconstruction → report reflects warnings (holes, low coverage, e.g. base) + lower score.
- [ ] Report generation fails → retry (bounded); on persistent failure, per the failure policy (default: COMPLETED-with-missing-report + flag).
- [ ] Upload fails → retry (durable/idempotent); on success, overwrites consistently.
- [ ] Artifact refs must point to existing stored artifacts → consistency check.
- [ ] Re-run/retry a completed job → report regenerated + overwritten idempotently.
- [ ] Input summary reconciles with the manifest (same photo/level stats).
- [ ] `reportVersion` present → client can handle evolution.
- [ ] Large/edge metric values → serialized correctly.

## Constraints

- The report MUST be generated on COMPLETED and match the PRD schema (authoritative); the input summary MUST reconcile with the manifest/metadata.
- Metrics MUST come from the engine (aggregated); NO fabrication; omit/compute honestly what's unavailable.
- The report MUST include a schema version + artifact references to the produced model files.
- It MUST be uploaded durably/idempotently to the job's artifact storage (client-fetchable via a separate endpoint).
- The failure policy MUST be defined + implemented (default: bounded retry + best-effort COMPLETED-with-missing-report + flag).
- Assembly MUST be deterministic + pure; metric reads + upload are IO adapters.
- No PII beyond what's required.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] On COMPLETED, `quality_report.json` is generated with the PRD field set (reconciled): identity, reconstruction metrics, coverage + warnings, input summary, artifact refs, version.
- [ ] Metrics are aggregated from the engine (no fabrication); the input summary reconciles with the manifest/metadata.
- [ ] The report is uploaded durably/idempotently to the job's artifact storage and is fetchable by the client (separate endpoint).
- [ ] Artifact references point to the produced model files (consistency verified).
- [ ] The report is deterministic (same inputs → same bytes).
- [ ] Report/upload failures follow the defined policy (default: bounded retry + best-effort + flag), without discarding a completed model.
- [ ] Assembly is pure/testable; metric reads + upload are adaptered.
- [ ] Typecheck + tests pass.

## Testing Instructions

(Unit tests for assembly + integration with a fake engine + fake artifact store.)
1. Complete a job with known engine metrics + input stats → assert the report structure (identity/reconstruction/coverage/input/artifacts) + `reportVersion`; assert deterministic (rebuild → identical).
2. Engine provides partial metrics → assert missing ones omitted (not fabricated).
3. Low-quality reconstruction → assert warnings + lower score reflected.
4. Assert the input summary matches the manifest/metadata stats (reconciliation).
5. Upload the report → assert it lands in the job's artifact storage; re-run → assert idempotent overwrite.
6. Report generation fails → assert bounded retry + failure policy (default: COMPLETED-with-missing-report + flag).
7. Artifact refs → assert they point to existing stored artifacts.
8. Confirm assembly is pure (fake IO) and the upload is durable/idempotent.
9. Typecheck + tests → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **PRD authoritative (KEY):** Assumes the PRD defines the report schema + warning taxonomy + quality scoring; the inferred structure is reconciled to it. Confirm.
- **Engine metrics (KEY):** Assumes the reconstruction engine emits the quality metrics; this step aggregates them (no fabrication). Confirm the metric set/interface.
- **Input reconciliation:** Assumes the input summary matches the client manifest/metadata stats (photos/levels/quality). Reconcile representations.
- **Artifact storage:** Assumes model artifacts + this report are stored in a per-job artifact location (S3/GCS/blob); the report references the artifacts; a separate endpoint serves them to the client. Confirm the store + reference format.
- **Runs on COMPLETED:** Assumes this runs as the completion step of the processing pipeline. Confirm the trigger point.
- **Failure policy:** Defaulted to bounded retry + best-effort (COMPLETED-with-missing-report + flag); alternative = report required for COMPLETED. Confirm with the PRD.
- **Scope:** Report generation (aggregate + assemble) + durable upload + artifact refs + version + failure policy. NOT the reconstruction (engine), NOT the pipeline state machine (prior task), NOT the client fetch/download endpoint (separate).
- **Stack:** Node + TypeScript worker; aggregates engine outputs + manifest; uploads to artifact storage; deterministic assembly + IO adapters. Adjust to the repo's structure.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: On completion: update jobs.state = COMPLETED, fill artifacts pointers — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend Worker



===============================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>



# NEW FEATURE: On Completion — Set jobs.state = COMPLETED + Fill Artifact Pointers (Atomic)
# Product: maya-viewer (Backend Processing Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature (Backend Worker) — authoritative completion transition
# Priority: Critical

---

## Task Description

Implement the authoritative job-completion transition: a single operation that records the artifact
pointer(s) on the job AND sets `jobs.state = COMPLETED` together, atomically, so a job is never
COMPLETED without valid artifact pointers and never has pointers written while still showing an
in-progress state. This is the commit point invoked after the GLB (and any other artifacts) have been
uploaded to the artifact bucket and their locations are known.

NOTE ON OVERLAP: the GLB-upload step already uploads the artifact and knows its key. This task owns the
FINAL state+pointer write as one transactional unit and is the single place that performs it — the upload
step calls into this rather than writing state/pointers itself. Consolidate any state/pointer writing here;
do not leave a second completion write in the upload step.

- [ ] Provide one completion operation that writes artifact pointer(s) and sets `state = COMPLETED` atomically
- [ ] Ensure completion is only reachable when the artifact(s) exist and their locations are confirmed
- [ ] Make it idempotent: repeated calls for an already-COMPLETED job do not corrupt or duplicate pointers
- [ ] Guard the transition so only a valid prior state (e.g., PROCESSING) can move to COMPLETED
- [ ] Store pointers in the exact form downstream consumers (maya-viewer) expect

## Files to Inspect First

1. The GLB-upload step (prior task) — find where it currently records the artifact reference and/or sets
   COMPLETED. That logic is CONSOLIDATED into this completion operation; the upload step should call this
   instead of writing state/pointers itself.
2. The job model and status enum — confirm the `state` field, the COMPLETED value, the valid prior state(s),
   and the artifact-pointer field(s) shape (single `artifactKey`/`modelUrl` vs an `artifacts` object/array).
3. The persistence layer — confirm whether atomic multi-field updates / transactions / conditional updates
   are available (e.g., a single document update for Mongo, a transaction for SQL), since state+pointers must
   be written together.
4. Downstream artifact consumers (how maya-viewer loads the model) — confirm the pointer form (raw key,
   bucket+key, presigned URL, or CDN path) so the stored pointers match what's read later.
5. The processing worker's idempotency model — this completion transition must be consistent with the
   at-least-once / duplicate-delivery handling already established.

### Tech note (task-specific)
Use the existing job-status enum (`COMPLETED`) — do not add a new string. Perform state + pointer writes in
a SINGLE atomic operation (one document update / one transaction / conditional update), not two sequential
writes that can partially fail. Reuse the configured DB client.

## API / Data Contract

Job completion write (single atomic update), conceptually:
  UPDATE job
    SET state = "COMPLETED",
        artifacts = { glb: { bucket, key }, ...(other artifacts if any) },
        completedAt = <now>
    WHERE jobId = :jobId AND state = <valid prior state, e.g. "PROCESSING">

Artifact pointer shape (confirm exact structure/field names against the model):
  {
    "glb": { "bucket": string, "key": string /* or a resolved url/cdn path per downstream */ }
    // extend to an artifact set if processing emits more than the GLB
  }

The conditional `WHERE ... state = PROCESSING` clause is the concurrency/idempotency guard: it makes the
transition a compare-and-set so a duplicate or racing completion does not double-apply.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define the completion operation
Create a single function/method `completeJob(jobId, artifactPointers)` (name per conventions) that performs
the atomic state+pointer write. It is the ONLY place that sets `state = COMPLETED`. It receives the confirmed
artifact pointer(s) from the upload step (which has already verified the objects exist in the artifact bucket).

### Step 2: Validate pointers before writing
Before the write, validate that the provided artifact pointers are well-formed and non-empty (a bucket + key
at minimum for the GLB). Refuse to complete with missing/empty pointers — a COMPLETED job MUST have valid
pointers. (Whether to also HEAD-verify the object exists here is optional; the upload step should already
have confirmed it — see Assumptions.)

### Step 3: Perform the atomic conditional transition
Write `state = COMPLETED`, the artifact pointer(s), and `completedAt` in ONE atomic operation, conditioned on
the job being in the valid prior state (PROCESSING). If the condition matches, the job completes. If it does
not match (already COMPLETED, or in a terminal/failed state), do NOT overwrite — handle idempotently (Step 4).

### Step 4: Idempotency and duplicate handling
- Already COMPLETED with the same pointers → treat as success (no-op); do not duplicate or rewrite pointers.
- Already COMPLETED with different pointers → do NOT silently overwrite; log/flag as an anomaly per policy
  (a job shouldn't complete twice with different artifacts — confirm handling).
- In a failed/terminal state → do not force to COMPLETED; surface per policy.
The conditional CAS in Step 3 provides the primary guard; duplicate calls resolve to no-ops, not corruption.

### Step 5: Wire the upload step to call this
Update the GLB-upload step so that, after confirming the artifact upload, it calls `completeJob(...)` with the
pointers instead of independently writing state/pointers. Remove any now-duplicate completion write from the
upload step so there is exactly one completion path.

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT set `state = COMPLETED` or write artifact pointers anywhere except this completion operation.
- Do NOT split state and pointers into two separately-committing writes — they are one atomic unit.
- Do NOT re-upload artifacts or run processing here — this consumes confirmed pointers and commits completion.
- Do NOT add a new job-status string or a new DB client.
- Do NOT complete a job with missing/empty artifact pointers.
- Do NOT overwrite an already-COMPLETED job's pointers on a duplicate call.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Duplicate completion call (same pointers) → idempotent no-op success; pointers not duplicated.
- [ ] Concurrent completion calls for one job → conditional CAS ensures exactly one applies; others no-op.
- [ ] Completion attempted from a non-PROCESSING state (already COMPLETED / FAILED) → not force-completed;
      handled per policy, no corruption.
- [ ] Empty/missing pointers passed in → refuse to complete; surface an error (job stays PROCESSING for retry).
- [ ] Partial write / DB error mid-transition → atomicity ensures no half-state (COMPLETED without pointers,
      or pointers without COMPLETED); on failure the job remains PROCESSING and the operation is retryable.
- [ ] Artifact set (multiple pointers) where one pointer is missing → do not complete with an incomplete set
      (confirm whether all artifacts are required for COMPLETED — see Assumptions).
- [ ] Already-COMPLETED with DIFFERENT pointers on a re-run → flagged as anomaly, not silently overwritten.

## Constraints

- `state = COMPLETED` and artifact pointers are written in a SINGLE atomic operation; no divergence possible.
- Completion is guarded by a conditional transition from the valid prior state (CAS), making it idempotent
  under at-least-once delivery / concurrent callers.
- A COMPLETED job ALWAYS has valid, non-empty artifact pointers; the invariant holds after every code path.
- This is the sole writer of COMPLETED/pointers; no duplicate completion logic elsewhere.
- Pointer form matches exactly what downstream consumers read; no ad-hoc shape.
- Use the existing enum and configured DB client; no new infra/status.
- No secrets in logs; log jobId + pointer keys (not credentials).

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A single completion operation writes artifact pointer(s) and `state = COMPLETED` atomically
- [ ] The transition is conditional on the valid prior state (PROCESSING) — a compare-and-set
- [ ] A COMPLETED job always has valid, non-empty artifact pointers (invariant verified across paths)
- [ ] Duplicate completion (same pointers) is an idempotent no-op — no duplicate/rewritten pointers
- [ ] Concurrent completions resolve to exactly one applied transition
- [ ] Completion from a non-PROCESSING state does not force COMPLETED or corrupt the record
- [ ] Empty/missing pointers → completion refused, job left PROCESSING (retryable)
- [ ] A DB failure mid-transition leaves no half-state (atomicity holds); retry can complete cleanly
- [ ] The GLB-upload step now calls this operation and no longer writes state/pointers itself (single path)
- [ ] Pointers are stored in the form downstream consumers expect
- [ ] No new job-status string or DB client introduced
- [ ] No TypeScript errors (`tsc --noEmit` passes); lint clean; unit tests green
- [ ] Code reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — no second completion writer remains

## Testing Instructions

1. Unit-test `completeJob` with valid pointers from a PROCESSING job; assert state → COMPLETED, pointers
   written, `completedAt` set, all in one update (verify single atomic call / transaction).
2. Assert the invariant: after completion, the job has non-empty pointers; there is no code path that reaches
   COMPLETED without them.
3. Call `completeJob` twice with the same pointers; assert the second is a no-op (pointers unchanged, no duplication).
4. Simulate concurrent completion calls; assert exactly one applies (CAS) and the others no-op.
5. Call from a job already COMPLETED (different pointers) and from a FAILED job; assert no overwrite/corruption
   and the anomaly is handled per policy.
6. Pass empty/missing pointers; assert completion is refused and the job stays PROCESSING.
7. Inject a DB error during the write; assert atomicity (no half-state) and that a retry completes cleanly.
8. Confirm the GLB-upload step calls `completeJob` and contains no independent state/pointer write.
9. Run `tsc --noEmit`, lint, and unit tests; confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- Assumed heavy OVERLAP with the GLB-upload task: that task uploads the artifact and confirms it exists; THIS
  task owns the atomic state+pointer commit. They must be reconciled so completion is written in exactly one
  place (here). If the upload task already implemented the state/pointer write, this task refactors it into a
  single atomic operation and removes the duplicate — confirm which task lands first to avoid two writers.
- Assumed the persistence layer supports an atomic multi-field conditional update (single-document update for
  a document store, or a transaction for SQL). If state and pointers live in separate stores/tables, a
  transaction or outbox is required to preserve the invariant — flag if unavailable.
- Assumed the valid prior state is PROCESSING and COMPLETED is an existing enum value; confirm exact names.
- Assumed the artifact pointer form (raw key vs bucket+key vs presigned URL vs CDN path) is dictated by how
  maya-viewer loads the model; the stored shape must match that consumer. Confirm before fixing the schema.
- Assumed a job may produce a single GLB or an artifact set. If a set, confirm whether ALL artifacts must be
  present for COMPLETED (all-or-nothing) or a partial set is acceptable; the prompt defaults to requiring the
  GLB and any declared-required artifacts.
- Assumed completing a job that's already COMPLETED with DIFFERENT pointers indicates an anomaly (reprocessing/
  bug) rather than normal flow; the prompt flags rather than overwrites. Confirm the desired policy if
  reprocessing is expected to replace artifacts.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: On completion: update projects.latestCompletedJobId, projects.status = COMPLETED — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend Worker




=======================================================================================================================>>>>>>





# NEW: On Completion — Update projects.latestCompletedJobId + projects.status = COMPLETED
# Product: MayasabhaXR — backend (Node + TypeScript)
# Scope: New Feature / Backend Worker — project-level completion update
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Backend Worker
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

When a processing job reaches COMPLETED, atomically update its parent project: set `projects.latestCompletedJobId = job.id` and `projects.status = COMPLETED`, so the projects hub reflects the finished reconstruction and can reference the latest completed job for viewing/download.

Focused update, real correctness concerns:
- **Atomic:** both fields updated in one transaction, consistent with the job's completion.
- **Out-of-order guard:** a project may have multiple capture jobs over time (re-captures); `latestCompletedJobId` must reflect the MOST RECENT completion — an older job completing late MUST NOT regress it over a newer already-completed job.
- **Ordering vs outputs:** do NOT flip the project to COMPLETED before the quality report + artifacts are fetchable (else the client sees "done" but can't fetch).
- **Idempotent:** a retried job completing twice sets the same values safely.
- **Soft-delete edge:** the P1 projects use `deletedAt`; decide whether to update a project deleted mid-processing.

- [ ] On job COMPLETED, atomically set `latestCompletedJobId` + `status = COMPLETED` on the correct project
- [ ] Guard out-of-order: latest completion wins; don't regress `latestCompletedJobId` to an older job
- [ ] Order after outputs (report/artifacts) are ready so a COMPLETED project has fetchable outputs
- [ ] Idempotent (retry-safe); correct project via the job's `projectId`; ownership consistent
- [ ] Respect the project status model (PROCESSING→COMPLETED; re-transitions on new jobs)
- [ ] Handle the soft-deleted-project case per policy

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. P1 Projects model/service — `status` (the state set), `deletedAt` soft-delete, ownership (token `sub`), the DB/ORM + transaction API
2. The processing-pipeline state-machine task — the COMPLETED transition where this runs; the job model (`projectId`, completion time)
3. The quality-report + artifact tasks (this phase) — the outputs that must be ready BEFORE the project flips to COMPLETED
4. The client projects hub (P1) + status/download tasks — the consumers of `status` + `latestCompletedJobId`
5. The PRD — the exact project status states + `latestCompletedJobId` semantics (authoritative)

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Trigger + correct project
- Runs when the pipeline reaches COMPLETED (after the outputs are ready — Step 3). Resolve the parent project via the job's `projectId`. Keep ownership consistent (the project belongs to the job's owner).

### Step 2: Atomic update
- In a single transaction, set `projects.latestCompletedJobId = job.id` and `projects.status = COMPLETED`. Both together (no partial update). Use the ORM's transaction.

### Step 3: Order after outputs
- Perform this project update AFTER the quality report + model artifacts are uploaded/available, so a project showing COMPLETED always has fetchable outputs. (Sequence within the COMPLETED handling: outputs ready → update project.)

### Step 4: Out-of-order guard
- `latestCompletedJobId` must reflect the MOST RECENT completed job. If an older job completes after a newer one already completed (out-of-order), DO NOT overwrite the newer `latestCompletedJobId`. Guard by comparing recency (job creation/completion time): only set `latestCompletedJobId` if this job is newer than the current one (or none). Consider a conditional/compare-and-set update.

### Step 5: Idempotency + status model
- Idempotent: completing the same job twice sets the same `latestCompletedJobId` + `status` (no adverse effect). 
- Status: transition to `COMPLETED` per the project status model (e.g. PROCESSING→COMPLETED). Note re-transitions: a NEW capture job later moves status back to PROCESSING, then COMPLETED again with the new `latestCompletedJobId`. Respect valid transitions.

### Step 6: Soft-delete edge
- If the project was soft-deleted (`deletedAt` set) while the job processed: per policy, either skip the update (don't resurrect a deleted project's status) or update while keeping it deleted. Default: still record `latestCompletedJobId` but do NOT un-delete / do NOT surface it as an active COMPLETED project (respect the soft-delete). Confirm the policy.

## API / Update Contract (TypeScript, illustrative)

```typescript
// within the pipeline COMPLETED handling, AFTER outputs are ready:
await tx(async (t) => {
  const project = await getProject(job.projectId, t);
  if (project.deletedAt) { /* per policy: skip surfacing / record only */ }
  // out-of-order guard: only if this job is newer than the current latestCompletedJobId
  if (isNewerCompletion(job, project.latestCompletedJobId)) {
    await updateProject(job.projectId, { latestCompletedJobId: job.id, status: 'COMPLETED' }, t);
  }
});
```
Rules: atomic (both fields); after outputs ready; out-of-order guard (latest wins); idempotent; correct project + ownership; valid status transition; soft-delete per policy.

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT update the wrong project — resolve via the job's `projectId`; ownership consistent.
- Do NOT update the two fields non-atomically — one transaction.
- Do NOT regress `latestCompletedJobId` to an older job when a newer already completed — guard out-of-order.
- Do NOT set `status = COMPLETED` before the report + artifacts are fetchable — order after outputs.
- Do NOT make it non-idempotent — retry-safe (same values).
- Do NOT ignore the project status model / valid transitions — COMPLETED per the states; re-transitions on new jobs.
- Do NOT resurrect a soft-deleted project as an active COMPLETED one — respect `deletedAt` per policy.
- Do NOT couple to the client — this is the DB update; clients read `status` + `latestCompletedJobId`.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Single job completes → project `status=COMPLETED`, `latestCompletedJobId=job.id`.
- [ ] Re-capture: a NEW job later → status back to PROCESSING, then COMPLETED with the NEW `latestCompletedJobId`.
- [ ] Out-of-order: an OLDER job completes after a NEWER already completed → `latestCompletedJobId` NOT regressed.
- [ ] Idempotent retry: same job completes twice → same values; no adverse effect.
- [ ] Project soft-deleted mid-processing → per policy (default: record but don't surface/un-delete).
- [ ] Concurrent completions for one project → transaction/serialized; consistent final `latestCompletedJobId` (newest).
- [ ] Ownership: project belongs to the job's owner (consistent).
- [ ] Client reads COMPLETED → the referenced latest job's outputs are fetchable (ordering held).

## Constraints

- The update MUST be atomic (both `latestCompletedJobId` + `status` in one transaction) and target the correct project (via `projectId`, ownership consistent).
- It MUST run AFTER the outputs (report/artifacts) are ready, so a COMPLETED project has fetchable outputs.
- `latestCompletedJobId` MUST reflect the most recent completion (out-of-order guard; latest wins; no regression).
- It MUST be idempotent (retry-safe) and respect the project status model / valid transitions.
- The soft-deleted-project case MUST be handled per policy (default: don't resurrect).
- It MUST NOT couple to the client (DB update only; clients read the fields).

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] On job COMPLETED (after outputs ready), the parent project is atomically updated: `latestCompletedJobId=job.id`, `status=COMPLETED`.
- [ ] Out-of-order completions don't regress `latestCompletedJobId`; the newest completion wins.
- [ ] Re-capture flows re-transition status (PROCESSING→COMPLETED) and update `latestCompletedJobId` to the new job.
- [ ] The update is idempotent and respects valid status transitions.
- [ ] A soft-deleted project is handled per policy (not resurrected by default).
- [ ] A project showing COMPLETED always has fetchable outputs (ordering held).
- [ ] Ownership/consistency preserved; no client coupling.
- [ ] Typecheck + tests pass.

## Testing Instructions

(Integration tests with the DB/ORM + a fake pipeline completion.)
1. Complete a job → assert the project has `status=COMPLETED` + `latestCompletedJobId=job.id`, updated atomically after outputs.
2. Re-capture: complete a newer job → assert `latestCompletedJobId` updates to the newer; status COMPLETED.
3. Out-of-order: complete an OLDER job after a NEWER already completed → assert `latestCompletedJobId` NOT regressed.
4. Idempotent: complete the same job twice → assert same values, no adverse effect.
5. Soft-deleted project mid-processing → assert the policy (default: recorded but not surfaced/un-deleted).
6. Concurrent completions → assert serialized, consistent newest `latestCompletedJobId`.
7. Assert the update happens AFTER outputs are ready (a COMPLETED project's outputs are fetchable).
8. Assert ownership consistency + valid status transition.
9. Typecheck + tests → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **Project status model:** Assumes `projects.status` includes COMPLETED (+ PROCESSING etc.) and `latestCompletedJobId` exists (or is added via migration). Confirm the states + field with the PRD/P1 model.
- **Runs on COMPLETED after outputs:** Assumes this is part of the pipeline's COMPLETED handling, sequenced AFTER the quality report + artifacts are uploaded. Confirm the sequence.
- **Out-of-order guard:** Assumes jobs have a comparable recency (creation/completion time) to determine "latest." Confirm the recency basis.
- **Soft-delete:** Assumes P1 `deletedAt`; default policy = record but don't resurrect. Confirm.
- **Ownership:** Project tied to the job's owner (P1 conventions).
- **Migration:** If `latestCompletedJobId` doesn't exist, add it (nullable FK to the job). Confirm.
- **Scope:** The atomic project update on job completion (latestCompletedJobId + status) + ordering + out-of-order guard + idempotency + soft-delete handling. NOT the pipeline state machine (prior), NOT the report/artifacts (prior), NOT the client hub/status/download (separate).
- **Stack:** Node + TypeScript worker; DB transaction; P1 projects model. Adjust to the repo's structure.
