Generate a coding prompt for this task: On failure: set jobs.state = FAILED, fill jobs.error.{code, message} — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend Worker



=============================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>





# NEW FEATURE: On Failure — Set jobs.state = FAILED + Fill jobs.error.{code, message} (Atomic)
# Product: maya-viewer (Backend Processing Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature (Backend Worker) — authoritative failure transition
# Priority: Critical

---

## Task Description

Implement the authoritative job-failure transition: a single operation that sets `jobs.state = FAILED`
and populates a structured `jobs.error` object (`{ code, message }`, plus optional context) together,
atomically. This is the single place a job is marked FAILED. It is invoked when the worker hits a
TERMINAL failure (not a transient/retryable one), so the job record carries a clear, structured reason
downstream consumers (and support) can act on.

MIRRORS the completion task: just as completion has one atomic state+pointer writer, failure has one
atomic state+error writer. No other code path sets FAILED directly.

- [ ] Provide one operation that sets `state = FAILED` and writes `error.{code, message}` atomically
- [ ] Use a defined, enumerated set of error codes (not free-form strings) with human-readable messages
- [ ] Only mark FAILED for TERMINAL failures; transient/retryable errors must NOT reach this (retry instead)
- [ ] Guard the transition (compare-and-set from a valid prior state) so it is idempotent under duplicate delivery
- [ ] Ensure the worker's failure sites route through this single operation, not inline state writes

## Files to Inspect First

1. The processing worker (pull/validate/start) and the GLB-upload/completion tasks — find every failure site
   (validation failure, manifest missing, S3 error, processing error, artifact-upload failure) and confirm
   which are terminal vs transient. Terminal ones route through this operation.
2. The completion task (`completeJob`) — mirror its structure and atomicity approach for consistency; this is
   the FAILED counterpart to COMPLETED.
3. The job model and status enum — confirm the `FAILED` state, the valid prior state(s) it transitions from
   (PROCESSING, and possibly QUEUED), and add/confirm the `error` field shape (`{ code, message, ... }`).
4. The persistence layer — confirm atomic multi-field conditional updates/transactions (state + error written together).
5. Any existing error-code catalog / error taxonomy in the codebase — reuse it; if none exists, define a small
   enumerated set for the processing pipeline (see Step 1). The retry/DLQ policy from the worker task — this
   operation is the terminal end of that policy.

### Tech note (task-specific)
Use the existing job-status enum (`FAILED`) — no new string. Write `state` + `error` in a SINGLE atomic
operation (one document update / one transaction), conditioned on the valid prior state. `error.code` MUST be
from an enumerated set, not an arbitrary string, so failures are queryable/classifiable.

## API / Data Contract

Job failure write (single atomic update), conceptually:
  UPDATE job
    SET state = "FAILED",
        error = { code: <ErrorCode>, message: string, detail?: string, failedAt: <now> },
    WHERE jobId = :jobId AND state IN (<valid prior states, e.g. "PROCESSING","QUEUED">)

Error object shape (confirm exact field names against the model):
  {
    "code": "MANIFEST_MISSING" | "MANIFEST_INVALID" | "FILE_COUNT_MISMATCH" |
            "PROCESSING_ERROR" | "ARTIFACT_UPLOAD_FAILED" | "INTERNAL_ERROR" | ... ,
    "message": string,          // human-readable, safe to surface (no secrets/PII/stack traces)
    "detail"?: string,          // optional extra context for debugging (still no secrets)
    "failedAt": string          // ISO timestamp
  }

The conditional `WHERE ... state IN (...)` clause is the concurrency/idempotency guard (compare-and-set).

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define/confirm the error-code enum
Reuse an existing error catalog if present; otherwise define an enumerated `ErrorCode` for the processing
pipeline covering the known terminal failures (manifest missing/invalid, file-count mismatch, processing
error, artifact-upload failure, generic internal). Each code maps to a default human-readable message.
Codes are stable identifiers (for querying/alerting); messages are for humans.

### Step 2: Define the failure operation
Create a single function `failJob(jobId, { code, message?, detail? })` (name per conventions). It is the ONLY
place that sets `state = FAILED`. It composes the `error` object (defaulting `message` from the code if not
provided) and performs the atomic write.

### Step 3: Perform the atomic conditional transition
Write `state = FAILED` + `error` in ONE atomic operation, conditioned on a valid prior state. If matched, the
job fails cleanly. If not matched (already FAILED, already COMPLETED, or otherwise terminal), do NOT overwrite —
handle idempotently (Step 4).

### Step 4: Idempotency and precedence
- Already FAILED (same or new call) → idempotent no-op; do NOT overwrite the FIRST recorded error with a later
  one (the original cause is usually the most useful). Confirm this precedence — first-error-wins is the default.
- Already COMPLETED → do NOT flip a completed job to FAILED (a late failure signal must not clobber a success).
  Log/flag as an anomaly per policy.
- Concurrent fail calls → CAS ensures one applies; others no-op.

### Step 5: Enforce terminal-only usage and route failure sites
This operation is for TERMINAL failures only. Transient/retryable errors (transient S3/DB, redeliverable
conditions) must NOT call `failJob` — they go through the worker's retry/backoff path and only reach `failJob`
when retries are exhausted or the failure is classified terminal. Update the worker's terminal failure sites to
call `failJob(...)` instead of writing FAILED inline; remove any duplicate inline FAILED writes.

### Step 6: Keep error content safe
`error.message`/`detail` must be safe to persist and potentially surface: no secrets, credentials, tokens,
signed URLs, or raw stack traces with sensitive paths. Sanitize before writing.

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT set `state = FAILED` anywhere except this operation.
- Do NOT split state and error into two separately-committing writes — one atomic unit.
- Do NOT mark FAILED for transient/retryable errors — those retry; only terminal failures fail the job.
- Do NOT overwrite an already-recorded error (first-error-wins) or flip a COMPLETED job to FAILED.
- Do NOT put free-form strings in `error.code` — use the enumerated set.
- Do NOT leak secrets/stack traces into `error.message`/`detail`.
- Do NOT add a new job-status string or a new DB client.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Transient error (e.g., transient S3 timeout) → NOT failed here; retried per worker policy; only after
      retry exhaustion does a terminal code (e.g., ARTIFACT_UPLOAD_FAILED) reach `failJob`.
- [ ] Duplicate fail call for same job → idempotent no-op; original error preserved (first-error-wins).
- [ ] Concurrent fail calls → CAS ensures exactly one applies.
- [ ] Fail called on an already-COMPLETED job → refused/flagged; success not clobbered.
- [ ] Fail called on an already-FAILED job with a different code → original error retained (do not overwrite);
      later code logged as secondary per policy.
- [ ] Missing/unknown error code passed → default to INTERNAL_ERROR with the provided message rather than
      writing an invalid code; do not throw and strand the job.
- [ ] DB error mid-transition → atomicity ensures no half-state (FAILED without error, or error without FAILED);
      the fail write itself is retryable.
- [ ] Job stuck in PROCESSING due to an upstream crash (from the worker task's reclaim concern) → when reclaimed
      and deemed terminal, this operation records the failure cleanly.

## Constraints

- `state = FAILED` and `error` are written in a SINGLE atomic operation; no divergence possible.
- The transition is a compare-and-set from a valid prior state; idempotent under at-least-once delivery.
- A FAILED job ALWAYS has a populated `error.{code, message}`; invariant holds after every path.
- This is the sole writer of FAILED/error; no duplicate failure-write logic elsewhere.
- Only terminal failures reach this; transient errors retry and never land the job in FAILED prematurely.
- `error.code` is from the enumerated set; `error.message`/`detail` contain no secrets/PII/stack traces.
- First recorded error is preserved on repeated failures; COMPLETED is never overwritten by FAILED.
- Use the existing enum and configured DB client; no new infra/status.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A single operation sets `state = FAILED` and `error.{code, message}` atomically
- [ ] The transition is conditional on a valid prior state (compare-and-set)
- [ ] A FAILED job always has a populated, enumerated `error.code` and a human-readable `error.message`
- [ ] Transient/retryable errors do NOT mark the job FAILED; only terminal failures do (verified)
- [ ] Duplicate fail calls are idempotent no-ops; the first error is preserved (not overwritten)
- [ ] Concurrent fail calls resolve to exactly one applied transition
- [ ] A COMPLETED job is never flipped to FAILED by a late failure signal
- [ ] Unknown/missing code defaults to INTERNAL_ERROR (with message), never an invalid code, never a throw-and-strand
- [ ] A DB failure mid-transition leaves no half-state; the fail write is retryable
- [ ] All terminal worker failure sites route through this operation; no inline FAILED writes remain
- [ ] `error.message`/`detail` contain no secrets/PII/stack traces
- [ ] No new job-status string or DB client introduced
- [ ] No TypeScript errors (`tsc --noEmit` passes); lint clean; unit tests green
- [ ] Code reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — single failure writer, terminal-only

## Testing Instructions

1. Unit-test `failJob` from a PROCESSING job with a valid code; assert state → FAILED, `error.{code,message}`
   populated, `failedAt` set, in one atomic update.
2. Assert the invariant: after failure, `error.code` is a valid enum value and `message` is non-empty; no path
   reaches FAILED without a populated error.
3. Call `failJob` twice with different codes; assert the FIRST error is retained (first-error-wins) and the
   second is a no-op/secondary-logged per policy.
4. Simulate concurrent fail calls; assert exactly one applies (CAS).
5. Call `failJob` on an already-COMPLETED job; assert it is refused/flagged and the completion is not clobbered.
6. Pass an unknown code; assert it defaults to INTERNAL_ERROR with the provided message and does not throw.
7. Verify a transient error path does NOT call `failJob` (it retries); only after exhaustion does a terminal
   code reach `failJob`.
8. Inject a DB error during the write; assert atomicity (no half-state) and that a retry can record the failure.
9. Confirm no worker failure site writes FAILED inline; all route through `failJob`.
10. Attempt to pass a secret/stack trace into message; confirm sanitization keeps it out of the stored error.
11. Run `tsc --noEmit`, lint, and unit tests; confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- Assumed this MIRRORS the completion task: one atomic state+error writer, sole owner of FAILED, mirroring
  `completeJob`'s structure and atomicity. Confirm sequencing with the worker/upload/completion tasks so their
  terminal failure sites route here and no inline FAILED writes remain.
- Assumed valid prior states for FAILED are PROCESSING (and possibly QUEUED) and FAILED is an existing enum
  value; confirm exact names and whether a QUEUED job can fail before processing starts.
- Assumed `error.code` should be an enumerated set. If a shared error catalog already exists, reuse it;
  otherwise a small processing-pipeline enum is defined here. Confirm the canonical code list and default messages.
- Assumed FIRST-error-wins on repeated failures (original cause is most diagnostic). Confirm; if latest-error
  is preferred, the overwrite policy inverts.
- Assumed the transient-vs-terminal classification lives in the worker's retry/DLQ logic and only terminal
  outcomes call `failJob`. Confirm the classification boundary so retryable conditions aren't prematurely failed.
- Assumed the persistence layer supports atomic multi-field conditional updates; if state and error live
  separately, a transaction/outbox is required to preserve the invariant — flag if unavailable.
- Assumed `error` is surfaced to clients/support; messages are sanitized (no secrets/PII/stack traces). Confirm
  what portion of `error` is exposed vs internal-only (e.g., `detail` internal, `message` surfaced).





This is deliberately the structural twin of the completion task, and the same single-writer discipline is what makes it worth its own prompt: exactly one place sets FAILED, writing state and the structured error as one atomic unit. The half-state failure mode is just as bad on this side — a job marked FAILED with an empty error (support has no idea why), or an error written while the job still shows PROCESSING (looks stuck, not failed). One conditional compare-and-set write closes both.
The distinction unique to the failure side, and the thing most likely to be gotten wrong, is terminal vs. transient. A transient S3 timeout is not a job failure — it's a retry. If every error site calls failJob, jobs fail on blips that a retry would have sailed through, and your failure rate is dominated by noise that masks real problems. So failJob is explicitly terminal-only: transient errors ride the worker's retry/backoff path and only reach failJob once retries are exhausted or the error is classified unrecoverable. That classification boundary lives in the worker's retry logic (from the pull/validate task), so the two need to agree on what counts as terminal — flagged in Assumptions.
Two policy calls worth a deliberate decision. First-error-wins vs. latest-error on repeated failures — I defaulted to preserving the first, since the original cause is usually the most diagnostic and later cascading errors tend to be symptoms, but that's a real choice. And error-code as an enumerated set, not free-form strings — this matters more than it looks: enumerated codes are queryable and alertable ("how many MANIFEST_INVALID this week"), free-form messages are not. The message is for humans; the code is for machines and dashboards. Getting a proper ErrorCode enum defined now (or reusing an existing catalog) pays off across the whole pipeline's observability.
And the safety line: error.message/detail get persisted and likely surfaced to clients or support, so they must be sanitized — no credentials, no signed URLs, no raw stack traces with sensitive paths. Easy to leak an exception's full message straight into the field; the prompt guards against it explicitly.
We're now four tasks into the Backend Processing Pipeline (pull/validate/start, upload GLB, complete, fail), and the last three are tightly coupled around one thing this phase keeps circling: the job state machine and its single-writer transitions. COMPLETED and FAILED are mirror commit points; both need a valid prior state, both must be atomic, both must be the sole writer of their status. That's not four independent tasks — it's one state machine implemented across four prompts, each re-deriving the same transition rules and idempotency model.
I'll make the offer concrete one more time, because it's now the clearly right move: let me assemble the Backend Processing Pipeline contract — the full job state machine (QUEUED → PROCESSING → COMPLETED | FAILED, with the valid transitions and who owns each write), the completeJob/failJob single-writer rule, the ErrorCode enum, the artifact-pointer + error shapes, the idempotency/reclaim model, and the bucket/key config. Every remaining task in this phase would then build against one authoritative reference instead of re-establishing the state model per prompt — and it would catch things like "can a QUEUED job fail before processing starts?" once, centrally, rather than as a per-task assumption.
Want the phase contract doc now, or should I scope the reclaim/reaper task for jobs stranded in PROCESSING (the gap flagged back in the worker prompt, which both completeJob and failJob depend on to resolve stuck jobs)?




































[[[[[ 1 ]]]]]


Generate a coding prompt for this task: Idempotent job processing: re-run same jobId safely — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend Worker


=============================================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>




# NEW: Idempotent Job Processing — Re-run Same jobId Safely
# Product: MayasabhaXR — backend (Node + TypeScript)
# Scope: Hardening / Backend Worker — cross-cutting idempotency
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Backend Worker
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Make processing a job idempotent: re-running the same `jobId` — from at-least-once queue delivery, a worker crash, a retry, or a manual re-run — MUST be safe. No duplicate work, no corrupted state, no duplicate artifacts, no duplicate side effects. Re-processing converges to the same final state.

Core mechanisms (cross-cutting across the pipeline):
- **Persisted stage = source of truth:** on pickup, resume from the persisted stage; skip already-done stages (or re-run them deterministically). Never double-advance or skip work.
- **Stable job-keyed artifact paths:** artifacts (model, textures, quality report) written to deterministic paths keyed by job → re-writing OVERWRITES, never duplicates (no `model_1`, `model_2`).
- **Idempotent side effects:** the project update is a set (same values); the "completed" notification fires once (dedup marker); external engine/service calls use idempotency keys or produce deterministic overwrites.
- **Complete-or-absent stage outputs:** a stage's output is atomic (fully present or absent) — a crash never leaves a half-output that corrupts the re-run.
- **At-least-once assumption:** design for re-delivery (don't assume exactly-once); composes with the state-machine's claim/lock (locks = concurrency; idempotency = re-runs across time).

- [ ] Resume from the persisted stage; skip done stages or re-run deterministically (no double-advance/skip)
- [ ] Write artifacts to stable job-keyed paths (overwrite, never duplicate)
- [ ] Make side effects idempotent (set-updates, once-only notifications via marker, idempotency keys for external calls)
- [ ] Stage outputs complete-or-absent (atomic); re-run from a clean point
- [ ] No duplicate job records on re-delivery (same `jobId`, same record)
- [ ] Verified safe under duplicate delivery, crash-resume, retry, and re-run of a COMPLETED job

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The processing-pipeline state-machine task — persisted stage, transitions, claim/lock, "advance only after durable"; the resume mechanism this formalizes
2. The quality-report + artifact tasks — artifact paths (must be stable/job-keyed for overwrite); the report's idempotent overwrite
3. The project-completion-update task — the set-update + out-of-order guard (already idempotent; verify)
4. The queue/worker infra — delivery semantics (at-least-once?), retry, visibility/lease; how re-delivery happens
5. The reconstruction engine adapter — whether its calls are idempotent or need an idempotency key / deterministic output path
6. Any notification/webhook/side-effect code — to dedup (once-per-job)

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Resume from persisted stage (skip/redo deterministically)
- On job pickup, read the persisted stage. Resume from there: stages already marked done are SKIPPED (or re-run deterministically if cheap/safe). Combined with the state-machine's "advance stage only after durable work," a re-run redoes at most the CURRENT (unfinished) stage — never earlier ones destructively.

### Step 2: Stable, job-keyed artifact paths
- All artifacts (mesh, textures, LODs, `quality_report.json`, intermediate stage outputs) written to DETERMINISTIC paths keyed by `jobId` (and stage). Re-writing OVERWRITES the same path — never appends a suffix / creates duplicates. No timestamps/random in the path.

### Step 3: Complete-or-absent stage outputs (atomic)
- A stage's output must be atomic: write to a temp/staging location then atomically move/commit, so a crash mid-write leaves either the previous state or a complete new one — never a corrupt partial that a re-run would mistake for done. The persisted "stage done" marker is set only after the output is durably committed.

### Step 4: Idempotent side effects
- **Project update:** already a set (`latestCompletedJobId`/`status`) with an out-of-order guard — verify idempotent.
- **Notifications/webhooks:** fire once per job completion — track a "notified" marker (or use an idempotency key) so a re-run doesn't re-notify.
- **External engine/service calls:** pass an idempotency key (e.g. derived from `jobId`+stage) or ensure deterministic overwrite, so a retried call doesn't duplicate downstream work.

### Step 5: No duplicate job records
- Re-processing uses the EXISTING job record (by `jobId`); never creates a new one on re-delivery. Ingestion/creation is keyed by `jobId` (or the client's idempotency key) so a duplicate ingest maps to the same job.

### Step 6: Verify under re-run scenarios
- Explicitly handle: duplicate queue delivery (second = no-op/resume), crash mid-stage (resume, redo current stage only), retry a FAILED job (re-enter idempotently), re-run a COMPLETED job (re-generate/overwrite artifacts idempotently, set-update the project, do NOT re-notify). All converge to the same final state.

## Idempotency Rules (summary)

```
- persisted stage = truth; resume/skip; advance marker only after durable output
- artifact paths deterministic + job-keyed -> overwrite, never duplicate
- stage output atomic (staging -> commit); done-marker after commit
- side effects: set-updates; once-only notifications (marker/idempotency key); external calls idempotency-keyed
- one job record per jobId (creation keyed by jobId)
- at-least-once delivery assumed; claim/lock for concurrency + idempotency for re-runs
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT re-run completed stages destructively — resume from the persisted stage; skip done (or re-run deterministically).
- Do NOT write artifacts to non-deterministic paths — stable job-keyed paths; overwrite, never duplicate.
- Do NOT perform side effects more than once — set-updates, once-only notifications (marker), idempotency-keyed external calls.
- Do NOT create a duplicate job record on re-delivery — same `jobId`, same record.
- Do NOT assume exactly-once delivery — design for at-least-once re-delivery.
- Do NOT leave partial stage outputs that a re-run mistakes for done — atomic staging→commit; done-marker after commit.
- Do NOT rely only on the claim/lock — locks handle concurrency; idempotency handles re-runs across time (need both).

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Duplicate queue delivery of the same job → second delivery is a no-op/resume (not a re-do from scratch, not a new record).
- [ ] Crash mid-stage → resume; redo at most the current stage; earlier stages/artifacts intact; no duplicates.
- [ ] Retry a FAILED job → re-enter at the retry point idempotently; bounded attempts.
- [ ] Re-run a COMPLETED job → artifacts overwritten idempotently; project set-updated; NO duplicate notification.
- [ ] Partial artifact from a crashed write → detected/overwritten (not treated as complete); atomic commit prevents corrupt "done".
- [ ] Concurrent re-delivery → claim/lock ensures one worker; idempotency ensures the other's later attempt is safe.
- [ ] External engine call retried → idempotency key / deterministic output → no duplicate downstream work.
- [ ] Duplicate ingest (same client idempotency key) → maps to the same job (no duplicate job).

## Constraints

- Re-running the same `jobId` MUST be safe: no duplicate work, corrupted state, duplicate artifacts, or duplicate side effects; it MUST converge to the same final state.
- The persisted stage MUST be the resume source of truth; the "stage done" marker MUST be set only after the output is durably committed (atomic staging→commit).
- Artifact paths MUST be deterministic + job-keyed (overwrite, never duplicate).
- Side effects MUST be idempotent (set-updates; once-only notifications via marker; idempotency-keyed external calls).
- There MUST be exactly one job record per `jobId` (creation keyed by `jobId`).
- The design MUST assume at-least-once delivery and compose with the state-machine's claim/lock.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Duplicate delivery, crash-resume, retry, and re-run of a COMPLETED job all converge to the same final state with no duplicate work/artifacts/side effects.
- [ ] Completed stages are not re-run destructively; a re-run redoes at most the current stage.
- [ ] Artifacts overwrite at stable job-keyed paths (no `_1`/`_2` duplicates).
- [ ] The "completed" notification fires exactly once across re-runs; external calls are idempotency-keyed.
- [ ] Stage outputs are atomic (no corrupt partial mistaken for done); the done-marker follows the durable commit.
- [ ] Exactly one job record per `jobId`; duplicate ingest maps to the same job.
- [ ] Verified under at-least-once delivery + concurrency (claim/lock + idempotency).
- [ ] Typecheck + tests pass.

## Testing Instructions

(Integration tests with a fake queue (forcing re-delivery), fake engine, fake artifact store, fake notifier.)
1. Deliver the same job twice → assert the second is a no-op/resume; one job record; no duplicate artifacts/notifications.
2. Crash mid-stage (kill after partial work, before the done-marker) → re-run → assert resume redoes only the current stage; earlier artifacts intact; atomic output (no corrupt partial).
3. Retry a FAILED job → assert idempotent re-entry; bounded attempts.
4. Re-run a COMPLETED job → assert artifacts overwritten at the same paths, project set-updated, notification NOT re-sent.
5. Force a partial artifact write then re-run → assert it's overwritten (not treated as done).
6. Concurrent re-delivery → assert claim/lock + idempotency → one processes, the other is safe.
7. Retried external engine call → assert idempotency key prevents duplicate downstream work.
8. Duplicate ingest with the same key → assert one job.
9. Typecheck + tests → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **Builds on the state machine:** Assumes the persisted-stage + claim/lock + "advance only after durable" from the pipeline task; this formalizes resume + adds artifact/side-effect idempotency. Blocked without it.
- **At-least-once delivery:** Assumes the queue is at-least-once (re-delivery possible). Confirm the delivery semantics.
- **Artifact paths:** Assumes artifacts can be written to stable job-keyed paths with atomic commit (staging→move). Confirm the storage supports atomic overwrite.
- **Side effects:** Assumes notifications/webhooks/external calls can be deduped (marker/idempotency key). Confirm the side-effect surface.
- **Job identity:** Assumes creation is keyed by `jobId` (or a client idempotency key) so duplicate ingest maps to one job. Confirm the ingest keying.
- **Scope:** Cross-cutting idempotency of job processing (resume, artifact overwrite, side-effect dedup, one-record) + verification under re-run scenarios. NOT the state machine itself (prior), NOT the report/project-update (prior; verified idempotent here), NOT the ingestion endpoint (adjacent; provides the job-keying).
- **Stack:** Node + TypeScript worker; builds on the pipeline state machine + artifacts + side effects; at-least-once queue. Adjust to the repo's structure.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: GET /jobs/:jobId — return state, stageProgress, artifacts URLs (CloudFront), error — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend APIs

=====================================================================================================>>>>>>>




# NEW FEATURE: GET /jobs/:jobId — Return state, stageProgress, artifact URLs (CloudFront), error
# Product: maya-viewer (Backend Processing Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature (Backend API — read/status endpoint)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Implement `GET /jobs/:jobId`, the endpoint clients poll to track a job through the pipeline and retrieve
the finished model. It returns the job's current `state`, stage/progress information, artifact URLs served
via CloudFront (for COMPLETED jobs), and the structured error (for FAILED jobs). It is a read-only view over
the job record produced by the processing pipeline.

- [ ] Return the job `state` (QUEUED / PROCESSING / COMPLETED / FAILED, etc.) for an authorized requester
- [ ] Return `stageProgress` (current stage and/or percentage) so clients can show progress
- [ ] For COMPLETED jobs, return artifact URLs as CloudFront URLs (not raw S3 keys/URLs)
- [ ] For FAILED jobs, return the client-safe subset of `error.{code, message}`
- [ ] Authorize the requester against the job; 404/403 appropriately; leak nothing cross-tenant

## Files to Inspect First

1. The job model — confirm the exact fields this endpoint reads: `state`, stage/progress field(s),
   `artifacts` pointer shape (from the completion task), and `error.{code, message, detail}` (from the
   failure task). This endpoint maps those to the response.
2. The existing route/controller layer and auth/authorization middleware — reuse the pattern for authorizing
   access to a job/project (the requester must own/have access to the job).
3. The CloudFront configuration — find the distribution domain and how S3 keys map to CloudFront URLs, and
   whether artifacts require SIGNED CloudFront URLs (private distribution) or are served via a public/CDN path.
   This determines how artifact pointers become client URLs (see Assumptions).
4. The artifact key scheme / bucket (`msxr-model-artifacts`) and the stored artifact-pointer form — so the
   endpoint converts stored pointers to CloudFront URLs correctly.
5. Any existing stage/progress tracking the worker writes — confirm what `stageProgress` can actually report
   (discrete stages, a percentage, or just the coarse state). Do not invent progress the pipeline doesn't record.

### Tech note (task-specific)
Validate the `jobId` path param with Zod. Reuse the configured CloudFront/signing utility and auth middleware.
Do NOT return raw S3 keys or raw S3 URLs for artifacts — artifacts are served via CloudFront. If the
distribution is private, generate SIGNED CloudFront URLs with a bounded expiry.

## API / Data Contract

Endpoint: `GET /jobs/:jobId`
Auth: required (requester must have access to the job's project).

Path param: `jobId` (string)

Success response (200) — shape varies by state:
{
  "jobId": string,
  "state": "QUEUED" | "PROCESSING" | "COMPLETED" | "FAILED",
  "stageProgress": {
    "stage": string,            // current pipeline stage label (confirm the stage set)
    "percent"?: number          // 0–100 if the pipeline records it; omit if not tracked
  },
  "artifacts"?: {               // present only when COMPLETED
    "glb": { "url": string, "expiresAt"?: string }   // CloudFront URL (signed → include expiresAt)
    // extend to the artifact set if more than the GLB
  },
  "error"?: {                   // present only when FAILED
    "code": string,             // enumerated ErrorCode
    "message": string           // client-safe human-readable; NO internal detail/stack/secrets
  },
  "updatedAt"?: string
}

Error responses:
  400 — malformed jobId
  401/403 — unauthenticated / not authorized for the job
  404 — job not found (or not visible to requester — see Assumptions on 404-vs-403)
  500 — unexpected (e.g., CloudFront signing failure); do not leak internals in the body

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Validate, load, authorize
Validate `jobId` (Zod). Load the job; 404 if absent (or if not visible to the requester per the chosen
tenant-isolation policy). Authorize via existing middleware; 403 if the requester lacks access. Do not reveal
another tenant's job existence.

### Step 2: Map state and stageProgress
Return the job's `state`. Populate `stageProgress` from what the pipeline actually records: the current stage
label and a percentage if tracked. If only the coarse state is known, return a stage derived from state (e.g.,
QUEUED→"queued", PROCESSING→"processing") and omit `percent` rather than fabricating a number.

### Step 3: Build artifact URLs (COMPLETED only) via CloudFront
Only when `state == COMPLETED` and artifact pointers exist: convert each stored artifact pointer (bucket+key)
into a CloudFront URL. If the distribution is private, generate a SIGNED CloudFront URL with a bounded expiry
and include `expiresAt`. Never return the raw S3 key/URL. If artifacts are somehow missing on a COMPLETED job
(should not happen per the completion invariant), handle defensively (see Edge Cases).

### Step 4: Surface error (FAILED only), client-safe
Only when `state == FAILED`: return `error.code` and a client-safe `error.message`. Do NOT expose internal
`error.detail`, stack traces, internal identifiers, or anything sensitive — expose only the fields designated
client-facing (confirm the split; `message` surfaced, `detail` internal-only per the failure task).

### Step 5: Assemble and return
Return the state-appropriate response. `artifacts` present only for COMPLETED; `error` present only for FAILED;
`stageProgress` for in-flight states. Keep the response minimal and consistent.

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify the pipeline that writes job state/artifacts/error — this endpoint only reads.
- Do NOT return raw S3 keys or raw S3 URLs for artifacts — use CloudFront (signed if the distribution is private).
- Do NOT expose internal error detail, stack traces, or credentials in the response.
- Do NOT fabricate progress the pipeline doesn't record (no fake percentages).
- Do NOT leak the existence of jobs the requester can't access.
- Do NOT bypass the existing auth/authorization middleware.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Job not found → 404 (or 404-not-403 to avoid leaking existence — per chosen policy).
- [ ] Requester not authorized → 403 (or 404 per tenant-isolation policy), no job data leaked.
- [ ] COMPLETED but artifact pointers missing/invalid (invariant violation) → do not 500 with internals;
      return a defensible response (e.g., state COMPLETED with an artifacts-unavailable indicator) and log
      the anomaly server-side. Confirm desired behavior.
- [ ] FAILED with an unrecognized/absent error code → return a generic client-safe code/message; never a blank error.
- [ ] In-flight (QUEUED/PROCESSING) → no `artifacts`, no `error`; `stageProgress` reflects current stage.
- [ ] CloudFront signing failure → 500 without leaking signing internals; log server-side. (Consider whether a
      partial response without URLs is preferable to a hard 500 — confirm.)
- [ ] Signed URL expiry → `expiresAt` communicates validity; a client polling later gets a freshly signed URL
      (URLs are generated per request, not cached stale).
- [ ] Malformed jobId → 400, not 500.
- [ ] High-frequency polling → endpoint is cheap and read-only; avoid heavy per-request work (e.g., don't
      HEAD S3 on every poll unless necessary — see Assumptions).

## Constraints

- Read-only: no writes, no state mutation on GET.
- Artifact URLs are CloudFront URLs; if the distribution is private, they are signed with a bounded expiry and
  generated per request (not stored/cached stale). Raw S3 keys/URLs are never returned.
- Error exposure is limited to the client-safe subset (`code`, `message`); internal detail is never returned.
- Authorization is enforced; cross-tenant existence is not leaked (consistent 404/403 policy).
- `jobId` validated with Zod; configured CloudFront/signing + auth middleware reused; no new clients.
- Response shape is state-appropriate and stable (artifacts↔COMPLETED, error↔FAILED).

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] `GET /jobs/:jobId` returns 200 with `state` and `stageProgress` for an authorized requester
- [ ] COMPLETED jobs return artifact URLs as CloudFront URLs (signed with `expiresAt` if the distribution is private)
- [ ] No raw S3 key or raw S3 URL ever appears in the response
- [ ] FAILED jobs return `error.{code, message}` limited to the client-safe subset (no internal detail/stack/secrets)
- [ ] In-flight jobs return no `artifacts`/`error` and a `stageProgress` reflecting the current stage
- [ ] `stageProgress.percent` is only present when the pipeline actually records it (no fabricated progress)
- [ ] Job not found → 404; unauthorized → 403 (or per the chosen no-leak policy); malformed jobId → 400
- [ ] Cross-tenant job existence is not leaked
- [ ] COMPLETED-but-artifacts-missing anomaly is handled defensibly (no internal-leaking 500), logged server-side
- [ ] CloudFront signing failure → 500 without leaking internals
- [ ] Signed URLs are generated per request (not stale-cached); `expiresAt` communicates validity
- [ ] Endpoint is read-only and cheap enough for client polling
- [ ] Zod validation, configured CloudFront/auth reused; no new clients or state writes
- [ ] No TypeScript errors (`tsc --noEmit` passes); lint clean
- [ ] Code reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — pipeline writers untouched, no raw S3, no error leakage

## Testing Instructions

1. Seed jobs in each state and `GET /jobs/:jobId`:
   - QUEUED/PROCESSING → assert `state` + `stageProgress`, no artifacts/error.
   - COMPLETED → assert artifact URLs are CloudFront URLs (signed + `expiresAt` if private), no raw S3 keys.
   - FAILED → assert `error.{code, message}` present and that internal `detail`/stack is NOT present.
   Example: `curl $BASE/jobs/$JOB_ID -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN"`
2. Grep the COMPLETED response to confirm no raw S3 domain/key leaks (only the CloudFront domain).
3. Request with a token lacking access → 403 (or 404 per policy); confirm no job data leaks.
4. Request an unknown jobId → 404; a malformed jobId → 400.
5. If the distribution is private, request a COMPLETED job twice with a gap → confirm freshly signed URLs each
   time and correct `expiresAt`.
6. Simulate a COMPLETED job with missing artifact pointers → confirm a defensible response (no internal-leaking
   500) and a server-side log.
7. Simulate a CloudFront signing failure → confirm 500 with no signing internals in the body.
8. Confirm the endpoint performs no writes (state unchanged after GET).
9. Run `tsc --noEmit` and lint; confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- Assumed the standard backend stack (Node/TypeScript, Zod), configured CloudFront distribution over the
  `msxr-model-artifacts` bucket, and the existing auth middleware, per AGENTS.md.
- Assumed CloudFront serves the artifacts. The KEY UNKNOWN: whether the distribution is PUBLIC (URL = domain +
  key, no signing) or PRIVATE (requires SIGNED CloudFront URLs with a key pair + bounded expiry). This changes
  the URL-generation step and whether `expiresAt` applies. Confirm — for private user models, signed URLs are
  the likely/secure choice.
- Assumed `stageProgress` reflects whatever the pipeline records. If the worker only tracks coarse state (no
  per-stage percentage), `stageProgress` returns a stage label derived from state and omits `percent`. Confirm
  what granularity the pipeline actually persists so the endpoint doesn't promise progress it can't provide.
- Assumed the client-safe error subset is `{ code, message }` with `detail` internal-only, consistent with the
  failure task's sanitization. Confirm the exact split.
- Assumed a tenant-isolation policy where unauthorized/absent jobs return a consistent response that does not
  leak existence (404-for-both is common). Confirm 404-vs-403 convention.
- Assumed polling is the client's mechanism for tracking jobs (no push/webhook here). If a completion webhook/
  push exists or is wanted, that's a separate task; this endpoint remains the pollable status source.
- Assumed URLs are signed per request rather than stored on the job; if signed URLs are pre-generated and stored,
  expiry management shifts to write-time — confirm. Per-request signing is the more robust default.






The decision that actually shapes the artifact half of this endpoint — flagged as the key unknown — is whether the CloudFront distribution is public or private. For user-generated 3D models, private-with-signed-URLs is the secure and likely-correct choice: a public distribution means anyone with the CloudFront domain and a job's key can pull someone else's model. If it's private, artifact URLs are signed with a bounded expiry and generated per request (so a client polling later always gets a fresh, valid URL rather than a stale one), and expiresAt tells the client how long it's good for. If it's public, the URL is just domain-plus-key and there's no signing. This needs confirming before build because it changes the URL-generation code entirely — and the security posture materially.
Two things this endpoint must resist that are easy to get wrong. First, never return raw S3 keys or URLs — the whole point of fronting artifacts with CloudFront is that clients hit the CDN, not the bucket directly; a leaked raw S3 URL both bypasses the CDN and can expose the bucket structure. I made "grep the response for S3 domain leaks" an explicit test. Second, error exposure discipline: the failure task deliberately separated a client-safe message from internal detail, and this endpoint is where that separation gets enforced at the boundary. Dumping the full error object — with its detail, internal identifiers, or an unsanitized underlying exception — straight into the response is the classic way internal state leaks to clients. Only { code, message } crosses the wire.
The stageProgress field is worth a reality check against the pipeline, flagged in Assumptions: the endpoint can only report progress the worker actually persists. If the pipeline tracks coarse state only (QUEUED/PROCESSING/etc.) and not per-stage percentages, the honest response is a stage label with percent omitted — not a fabricated number that makes a progress bar move without corresponding to anything. So this endpoint's richness is capped by what the worker records; if richer progress is wanted, that's upstream work in the worker, not something to invent here.
This is the first read task in the phase, and it's downstream of essentially everything: it reads the state machine from the worker, the artifact pointers from completeJob, and the error from failJob. That makes it the clearest demonstration yet of why the shared definitions want to live in one place — this endpoint has to know the exact artifacts shape, the error.{code, message, detail} split, the state set, and the stage vocabulary, all of which were each defined (or assumed) in a different prior prompt. If those drift, this endpoint returns wrong or leaky data.
So the phase-contract offer is now not just a convenience but a correctness safeguard: a Backend Processing Pipeline contract pinning the job state machine, the completeJob/failJob single-writer transitions, the ErrorCode enum and client-safe/internal error split, the artifact-pointer shape, the CloudFront public-vs-private decision, and the stage vocabulary would let this GET endpoint and all four writer tasks build against one agreed schema instead of each defining a piece of it. Given this endpoint literally consumes the output of the other four, I'd build that contract before implementing this so the response shape is derived from the canonical definitions rather than re-guessed.
Want me to assemble that phase contract doc now — it's the natural consolidation point for this cluster — or scope the completion-notification/webhook task (the push alternative to polling this endpoint) next?

=================================================================================================>>>>>>>



# NEW: POST /jobs/:jobId/retry — Re-queue a FAILED Job for Reprocessing
# Product: MayasabhaXR — backend (Node + TypeScript)
# Scope: New Feature / Backend APIs — retry endpoint
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Backend APIs
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Add `POST /jobs/:jobId/retry`: for a FAILED job owned by the requester, re-queue it for reprocessing (transition to the retry entry point, enqueue for the worker, increment attempts) and return the updated job. Built on P1 API conventions (auth, ownership, DTOs, error handling) with pipeline-specific preconditions.

Key behaviors:
- **Auth + ownership:** authenticated; the job must belong to the requester (via project/user, token `sub`). Non-owned/non-existent → 404 (NOT 403), per the P1 no-leak convention.
- **FAILED-only precondition:** only a FAILED job is retryable. QUEUED/PROCESSING/…/COMPLETED → reject (409/422, "not in a retryable state").
- **Bounded retries:** respect the pipeline's max-attempts; if exceeded → reject (422, "max retries exceeded").
- **Idempotent/guarded re-queue:** double-POST or concurrent retries MUST NOT double-enqueue — guard so one re-queue happens.
- **Delegates execution:** the endpoint transitions FAILED → retry entry point + enqueues; the WORKER executes per the pipeline's retry policy (from the failed stage vs restart). Idempotent processing makes the re-run safe.

- [ ] `POST /jobs/:jobId/retry` — auth + ownership (404-not-403); FAILED-only (else 409/422)
- [ ] Bounded retries (422 if max exceeded); attempts incremented
- [ ] Safe/idempotent re-queue (transition + enqueue; no double-enqueue on double/concurrent POST)
- [ ] Delegate execution to the worker/pipeline (retry policy); don't reimplement processing in the endpoint
- [ ] Return the updated job DTO (status QUEUED, attempts)
- [ ] Handle edges (non-existent, non-FAILED, soft-deleted project, concurrent retry)

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. P1 API conventions — auth middleware (token `sub`), ownership + 404-not-403, route/controller/service structure, DTO shape, error/status conventions, Zod validation
2. The processing-pipeline state-machine task — the FAILED state, the retry entry point + policy, attempts/bound; the transition + enqueue mechanism
3. The idempotent-job-processing task — the re-run safety the retry relies on (so re-queue is safe)
4. The job model — `status`, `attempts`, `projectId`/ownership, timestamps
5. The queue/worker infra — how a job is enqueued (the re-queue)
6. The project-completion / status tasks — the job DTO shape returned

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Route + auth + ownership
- `POST /jobs/:jobId/retry`, authenticated. Load the job by `jobId`; verify it belongs to the requester (via project/user, token `sub`). If missing or not owned → 404 (NOT 403). Validate `jobId` format (Zod/param validation).

### Step 2: FAILED-only precondition
- If the job's status is not FAILED → reject: 409 Conflict (or 422) with a clear message ("job is not in a retryable state; current: <status>"). Do not enqueue.

### Step 3: Bounded retries
- Check the attempts against the pipeline's max-attempts bound. If exceeded → 422 ("max retries exceeded"). Otherwise proceed; increment attempts on re-queue.

### Step 4: Idempotent/guarded re-queue
- Transition FAILED → the retry entry point (QUEUED or resume-from-failed-stage per the pipeline policy) and enqueue for the worker — in a way that's safe under double/concurrent POST: guard so only ONE re-queue happens (e.g. a conditional status transition FAILED→QUEUED that a second concurrent request can't repeat, or an idempotency check). A second POST after re-queue (now QUEUED/PROCESSING) hits the FAILED-only precondition → rejected/no-op.

### Step 5: Delegate execution
- The endpoint only transitions + enqueues; the WORKER runs the job per the pipeline's retry policy (from the failed stage if prior outputs preserved, else restart). Do NOT reimplement stage processing in the endpoint. The idempotent-processing guarantees make the re-run safe.

### Step 6: Response + edges
- Return the updated job DTO (status QUEUED, incremented attempts, cleared/retained error per policy). Handle: non-existent (404), non-FAILED (409/422), soft-deleted project (per policy — reject or allow; confirm), concurrent retry (one re-queue).

## API Contract (illustrative)

```
POST /jobs/:jobId/retry            (auth required)
 200 -> { jobId, projectId, status: "QUEUED", attempts, ... }   // re-queued
 404 -> not found / not owned (no leak)
 409/422 -> not in a retryable state (current status) 
 422 -> max retries exceeded
```
Rules: auth + ownership (404-not-403); FAILED-only; bounded retries; guarded/idempotent re-queue (no double-enqueue); delegate execution to the worker (retry policy); return the job DTO.

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT retry a non-FAILED job — enforce the FAILED-only precondition (409/422).
- Do NOT leak jobs the requester doesn't own — 404-not-403.
- Do NOT double-enqueue on double/concurrent POST — guarded/idempotent re-queue (one enqueue).
- Do NOT ignore the max-attempts bound — reject (422) if exceeded.
- Do NOT reimplement the retry/stage processing in the endpoint — transition + enqueue; the worker executes.
- Do NOT skip auth/ownership/validation — authenticated, owned, valid `jobId`.
- Do NOT lose attempts tracking — increment on re-queue.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Retry a FAILED job → 200; status QUEUED; attempts++; enqueued.
- [ ] Retry a non-FAILED job (QUEUED/PROCESSING/…/COMPLETED) → 409/422 with current status.
- [ ] Retry a job not owned / non-existent → 404 (no leak).
- [ ] Double POST / concurrent retries → one re-queue; the second hits FAILED-only → rejected/no-op (no double-enqueue).
- [ ] Max attempts exceeded → 422.
- [ ] Retry fails again → job → FAILED again; retryable until max.
- [ ] Soft-deleted project → per policy (reject/allow); confirm.
- [ ] Worker picks up the re-queued job → idempotent processing handles it safely.
- [ ] Invalid `jobId` format → 400/validation error.

## Constraints

- The endpoint MUST require auth and enforce ownership with 404-not-403 (no leak).
- It MUST enforce the FAILED-only precondition (409/422 otherwise) and the max-attempts bound (422 if exceeded).
- The re-queue MUST be guarded/idempotent (no double-enqueue on double/concurrent POST).
- It MUST transition + enqueue only; the WORKER executes per the pipeline's retry policy (no reimplementation).
- It MUST increment attempts and return the updated job DTO.
- It MUST follow P1 API conventions (auth, DTO, errors, validation).

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] `POST /jobs/:jobId/retry` re-queues a FAILED owned job (→ QUEUED, attempts++), returns the job DTO, and the worker reprocesses it.
- [ ] Non-FAILED → 409/422; non-owned/non-existent → 404; invalid id → 400.
- [ ] Max attempts exceeded → 422.
- [ ] Double/concurrent POST → exactly one re-queue (no double-enqueue).
- [ ] Execution is delegated to the worker (retry policy); the re-run is safe (idempotent processing).
- [ ] Auth/ownership/validation follow P1 conventions.
- [ ] Typecheck + tests pass.

## Testing Instructions

1. Retry a FAILED owned job → assert 200, status QUEUED, attempts++, enqueued; worker reprocesses.
2. Retry a PROCESSING/COMPLETED job → assert 409/422 with the current status.
3. Retry a job owned by another user / non-existent → assert 404 (no leak).
4. Double POST (and concurrent) → assert exactly one re-queue; the second is rejected/no-op.
5. Exceed max attempts → assert 422.
6. Retry → fails again → assert FAILED again, still retryable until max.
7. Soft-deleted project → assert the policy.
8. Invalid `jobId` → assert 400/validation.
9. Confirm the endpoint only transitions+enqueues (worker executes); typecheck + tests → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **Pipeline retry policy:** Assumes the state machine defines the retry entry point (failed stage vs restart) + max-attempts; this endpoint triggers it. Blocked without the FAILED state + policy.
- **Idempotent processing:** Assumes the idempotent-job-processing guarantees make the re-run safe; the endpoint's guard prevents double-enqueue.

- **Soft-deleted project:** Confirm whether retry is allowed for a soft-deleted project's job (default: reject).

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Push notification on COMPLETED / FAILED (Firebase FCM) — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend APIs


==============================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>



# FEATURE: Push Notifications on Job COMPLETED / FAILED — Firebase FCM
# Product: Internal / Backend Processing Pipeline
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Backend APIs
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: Medium

---

## Context

When a capture job transitions to `COMPLETED` or `FAILED` status (driven by the
MongoDB-polled worker from the job queue task), the mobile app (Flutter) must
receive a push notification. The notification is sent server-side via Firebase
Cloud Messaging (FCM) HTTP v1 API immediately after the worker writes the final
status to the job document.

This is a fire-and-forget notification layer — it does not affect job processing
correctness. A failed FCM send must never crash the worker or cause a job retry.

---

## Task Description

- [ ] Create `src/services/fcmService.ts` — a thin wrapper around the FCM
      HTTP v1 API that sends a notification to a specific device token
- [ ] Create `src/services/notificationService.ts` — business logic layer that
      composes the correct notification payload for `COMPLETED` vs `FAILED` job
      outcomes and calls `fcmService`
- [ ] Add a `deviceToken` field to the `Project` or `User` model (whichever
      owns the FCM token for the project owner) — store the token registered
      by the Flutter app on login/startup
- [ ] Add `POST /users/device-token` endpoint so the Flutter app can register
      or refresh its FCM token server-side
- [ ] Wire `notificationService.notifyJobOutcome()` into the worker's
      `markCompleted` and `markFailed` calls in `src/worker/jobQueue.ts` — call
      it after the DB write, never before
- [ ] Handle all FCM failure modes silently — log the error, never throw, never
      affect the job status

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `src/worker/jobQueue.ts` — `markCompleted` and `markFailed` functions; this
   is where `notifyJobOutcome` is wired in after the DB write
2. `src/models/job.model.ts` — confirm `projectId` field shape so the
   notification service can look up the project owner's device token
3. `src/models/user.model.ts` or `src/models/project.model.ts` — find where
   the project owner is stored; `deviceToken` is added to whichever model owns
   the user identity for the project
4. `src/routes/users.ts` or `src/routes/auth.ts` — find existing user-facing
   routes to understand where `POST /users/device-token` fits in the router
   structure and which auth middleware to apply
5. `.env.example` — understand env var patterns before adding FCM credentials
6. `package.json` — confirm whether `firebase-admin` is already a dependency;
   if not, it will be added in Step 1

---

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Install and Initialize Firebase Admin SDK

Install if not present:
```bash
npm install firebase-admin
```

Create `src/lib/firebaseAdmin.ts`:

```typescript
import * as admin from "firebase-admin";

/**
 * Firebase Admin SDK singleton.
 * Initialized once at process start.
 * Used by fcmService — do not import directly elsewhere.
 */
let _app: admin.app.App | null = null;

export function getFirebaseApp(): admin.app.App {
  if (_app) return _app;

  const projectId = process.env.FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID;
  const clientEmail = process.env.FIREBASE_CLIENT_EMAIL;
  const privateKey = process.env.FIREBASE_PRIVATE_KEY?.replace(/\\n/g, "\n");

  if (!projectId || !clientEmail || !privateKey) {
    throw new Error(
      "Missing Firebase credentials. Set FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID, " +
      "FIREBASE_CLIENT_EMAIL, and FIREBASE_PRIVATE_KEY environment variables."
    );
  }

  _app = admin.initializeApp({
    credential: admin.credential.cert({ projectId, clientEmail, privateKey }),
  });

  return _app;
}

/** For testing only — reset the singleton. */
export function _resetFirebaseApp(): void {
  _app = null;
}
```

Call `getFirebaseApp()` at worker startup in `src/worker/index.ts` — not lazily
inside each notification call — so a misconfigured credential fails fast on boot
rather than silently on the first job completion:

```typescript
// In src/worker/index.ts, inside main(), before startWorker():
try {
  getFirebaseApp();
  log("info", "Firebase Admin SDK initialized");
} catch (err: any) {
  log("warn", "Firebase Admin SDK failed to initialize — push notifications disabled", {
    error: err.message,
  });
  // Do NOT exit — notifications are non-critical; worker continues without them
}
```

### Step 2: Create `src/services/fcmService.ts`

```typescript
import * as admin from "firebase-admin";
import { getFirebaseApp } from "../lib/firebaseAdmin";

export interface FcmPayload {
  token: string;
  title: string;
  body: string;
  data?: Record<string, string>;   // FCM data payload — string values only
}

export interface FcmResult {
  success: boolean;
  messageId?: string;
  error?: string;
  errorCode?: string;
}

/**
 * Send a single FCM push notification to a device token.
 * Always resolves — never rejects. Errors are returned in the result object.
 * Callers must not await this in a way that blocks critical path execution.
 */
export async function sendFcmNotification(payload: FcmPayload): Promise<FcmResult> {
  try {
    const app = getFirebaseApp();
    const messaging = admin.messaging(app);

    const message: admin.messaging.Message = {
      token: payload.token,
      notification: {
        title: payload.title,
        body: payload.body,
      },
      data: payload.data ?? {},
      android: {
        priority: "high",
        notification: {
          sound: "default",
          channelId: "job_status",        // Flutter must create this channel
        },
      },
      apns: {
        payload: {
          aps: {
            sound: "default",
            badge: 1,
          },
        },
      },
    };

    const messageId = await messaging.send(message);
    return { success: true, messageId };
  } catch (err: any) {
    // FCM error codes: https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/send-message#rest
    const errorCode: string = err.errorInfo?.code ?? err.code ?? "UNKNOWN";
    return { success: false, error: err.message, errorCode };
  }
}

/**
 * Check whether a FCM error code indicates a permanently invalid token.
 * When true, the caller should delete the token from the DB to avoid
 * repeated failed sends.
 */
export function isTokenInvalidError(errorCode: string): boolean {
  return [
    "messaging/invalid-registration-token",
    "messaging/registration-token-not-registered",
    "messaging/invalid-argument",
  ].includes(errorCode);
}
```

### Step 3: Create `src/services/notificationService.ts`

```typescript
import { sendFcmNotification, isTokenInvalidError } from "./fcmService";
import { ProjectModel } from "../models/project.model";   // or UserModel — inspect first
import { UserModel } from "../models/user.model";

interface JobOutcomeParams {
  jobId: string;
  projectId: string;
  status: "COMPLETED" | "FAILED";
  errorMessage?: string;
  outputS3Key?: string;
}

/**
 * Send a push notification to the project owner when a job reaches a
 * terminal state. Fire-and-forget — never throws.
 */
export async function notifyJobOutcome(params: JobOutcomeParams): Promise<void> {
  const { jobId, projectId, status, errorMessage, outputS3Key } = params;

  try {
    // Resolve device token from project owner
    const deviceToken = await resolveDeviceToken(projectId);
    if (!deviceToken) {
      log("info", "No device token for project — skipping notification", { projectId, jobId });
      return;
    }

    const payload = buildPayload(status, jobId, projectId, errorMessage, outputS3Key);
    const result = await sendFcmNotification({ token: deviceToken, ...payload });

    if (result.success) {
      log("info", "Push notification sent", { jobId, projectId, status, messageId: result.messageId });
    } else {
      log("warn", "Push notification failed", {
        jobId, projectId, status,
        errorCode: result.errorCode,
        error: result.error,
      });

      // Stale token — remove from DB to stop future failed sends
      if (result.errorCode && isTokenInvalidError(result.errorCode)) {
        await clearDeviceToken(projectId).catch(() => {});
        log("warn", "Stale FCM token removed", { projectId });
      }
    }
  } catch (err: any) {
    // Outer safety net — this function must never throw
    log("error", "Unexpected error in notifyJobOutcome", { jobId, projectId, error: err.message });
  }
}

function buildPayload(
  status: "COMPLETED" | "FAILED",
  jobId: string,
  projectId: string,
  errorMessage?: string,
  outputS3Key?: string
): { title: string; body: string; data: Record<string, string> } {
  if (status === "COMPLETED") {
    return {
      title: "Capture Complete ✓",
      body: "Your 3D model is ready to preview.",
      data: {
        type: "JOB_COMPLETED",
        jobId,
        projectId,
        outputS3Key: outputS3Key ?? "",
      },
    };
  }

  return {
    title: "Capture Failed",
    body: errorMessage
      ? `Processing failed: ${truncate(errorMessage, 80)}`
      : "Something went wrong. Please try again.",
    data: {
      type: "JOB_FAILED",
      jobId,
      projectId,
      errorMessage: errorMessage ?? "",
    },
  };
}

async function resolveDeviceToken(projectId: string): Promise<string | null> {
  // Strategy: look up the project → get ownerId → look up user → get deviceToken
  // Adjust the field traversal to match the actual model relationships after
  // inspecting project.model.ts and user.model.ts

  const project = await ProjectModel.findById(projectId).select("ownerId").lean();
  if (!project?.ownerId) return null;

  const user = await UserModel.findById(project.ownerId).select("deviceToken").lean();
  return user?.deviceToken ?? null;
}

async function clearDeviceToken(projectId: string): Promise<void> {
  const project = await ProjectModel.findById(projectId).select("ownerId").lean();
  if (!project?.ownerId) return;
  await UserModel.findByIdAndUpdate(project.ownerId, { $unset: { deviceToken: "" } });
}

function truncate(str: string, maxLen: number): string {
  return str.length <= maxLen ? str : str.slice(0, maxLen - 1) + "…";
}

function log(level: "info" | "warn" | "error", message: string, meta: Record<string, unknown> = {}): void {
  console[level](JSON.stringify({ timestamp: new Date().toISOString(), level, message, ...meta }));
}
```

### Step 4: Add `deviceToken` Field to User Model

In `src/models/user.model.ts`, add the `deviceToken` field if not present:

```typescript
deviceToken: { type: String, default: null },
deviceTokenUpdatedAt: { type: Date, default: null },
```

Do not make `deviceToken` a required field — users who have not yet granted
notification permission will not have one, and that is a valid state.

### Step 5: Add `POST /users/device-token` Endpoint

In the appropriate router file (likely `src/routes/users.ts`), add:

```typescript
/**
 * POST /users/device-token
 * Called by the Flutter app after FCM token is obtained or refreshed.
 * Must be called on every app launch (FCM tokens rotate).
 */
router.post(
  "/device-token",
  authMiddleware,
  async (req, res, next) => {
    try {
      const { token } = req.body;

      if (!token || typeof token !== "string" || token.trim().length === 0) {
        return res.status(400).json({ error: "token is required and must be a non-empty string" });
      }

      // req.user is populated by authMiddleware — adjust field name to match
      const userId = req.user._id ?? req.user.id;

      await UserModel.findByIdAndUpdate(userId, {
        $set: {
          deviceToken: token.trim(),
          deviceTokenUpdatedAt: new Date(),
        },
      });

      return res.status(200).json({ success: true });
    } catch (err) {
      next(err);
    }
  }
);
```

### Step 6: Wire `notifyJobOutcome` into Worker Job Queue

In `src/worker/jobQueue.ts`, import and call `notifyJobOutcome` after each
terminal state write. Never await it in a blocking way — use `.catch(() => {})`
to prevent any notification failure from propagating into the worker loop:

```typescript
import { notifyJobOutcome } from "../services/notificationService";

export async function markCompleted(
  jobId: string,
  projectId: string,
  result: Record<string, unknown>
): Promise<void> {
  await JobModel.findByIdAndUpdate(jobId, {
    $set: { status: "COMPLETED", completedAt: new Date(), result, lastError: null },
  });

  // Fire-and-forget — notification failure must never affect job status
  notifyJobOutcome({
    jobId,
    projectId,
    status: "COMPLETED",
    outputS3Key: result.outputS3Key as string | undefined,
  }).catch(() => {});
}

export async function markFailed(
  jobId: string,
  projectId: string,
  error: Error,
  attempts: number,
  maxAttempts: number
): Promise<void> {
  const exhausted = attempts >= maxAttempts;
  const retryDelay = Math.min(1000 * 60 * Math.pow(2, attempts - 1), 1000 * 60 * 30);

  await JobModel.findByIdAndUpdate(jobId, {
    $set: {
      status: exhausted ? "FAILED" : "QUEUED",
      lastError: error.message,
      claimedAt: null,
      claimedBy: null,
      nextRetryAt: exhausted ? null : new Date(Date.now() + retryDelay),
    },
    $inc: { attempts: 1 },
  });

  // Only notify on permanent failure — not on intermediate retries
  if (exhausted) {
    notifyJobOutcome({
      jobId,
      projectId,
      status: "FAILED",
      errorMessage: error.message,
    }).catch(() => {});
  }
}
```

Note: `markCompleted` and `markFailed` now require `projectId` as a parameter.
Update all call sites in `src/worker/worker.ts` accordingly — `job.projectId`
is already available on the `JobDocument`.

### Step 7: Add Environment Variables to `.env.example`
Firebase FCM (Push Notifications)
FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID=your-firebase-project-id
FIREBASE_CLIENT_EMAIL=firebase-adminsdk-xxxxx@your-project.iam.gserviceaccount.com
FIREBASE_PRIVATE_KEY="-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----\nYOUR_KEY_HERE\n-----END PRIVATE KEY-----\n"

Note on `FIREBASE_PRIVATE_KEY`: the newlines in the PEM key must be stored as
literal `\n` in the `.env` file and converted back with `.replace(/\\n/g, "\n")`
in `firebaseAdmin.ts` (already handled in Step 1).

## API Contract

**`POST /users/device-token`**

Auth: Required (same token/session guard as all `/users` routes)

Request body:
```json
{ "token": "fcm_device_token_string_from_flutter" }
```

Success `200`:
```json
{ "success": true }
```

Errors:
400  { "error": "token is required and must be a non-empty string" }
401  { "error": "Unauthorized" }
500  { "error": "Internal server error" }

**FCM Notification Payloads**

On `COMPLETED`:
```json
{
  "notification": { "title": "Capture Complete ✓", "body": "Your 3D model is ready to preview." },
  "data": { "type": "JOB_COMPLETED", "jobId": "...", "projectId": "...", "outputS3Key": "..." }
}
```

On `FAILED` (permanent, after all retries exhausted):
```json
{
  "notification": { "title": "Capture Failed", "body": "Processing failed: <truncated error>" },
  "data": { "type": "JOB_FAILED", "jobId": "...", "projectId": "...", "errorMessage": "..." }
}
```

This Flutter wiring is out of scope for this backend prompt but must be done
in parallel for end-to-end testing.

---

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT make `notifyJobOutcome` throw under any circumstances — it must be
  a silent fire-and-forget operation; notification failure must never affect
  job processing outcome
- Do NOT await `notifyJobOutcome` in a way that blocks `markCompleted` or
  `markFailed` — use `.catch(() => {})` and let it run asynchronously
- Do NOT send a notification on intermediate retries — only on `exhausted`
  permanent failure; users should not receive multiple "failed" notifications
  for one job
- Do NOT store FCM tokens in the `Project` model if the project can have
  multiple collaborators in future — store on `User` and resolve via the
  project's `ownerId`; this task stores it on `User`
- Do NOT change the `JobProcessor` signature or the worker loop mechanics from
  the job queue task
- Do NOT add FCM logic inside the photogrammetry service or any processing
  layer — notification is a post-processing concern owned by the job queue
  transition functions only

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] No device token exists for the project owner (user hasn't logged into the
      app yet, or denied notification permission) → `resolveDeviceToken` returns
      `null`; log and return silently; never throw
- [ ] Firebase Admin SDK credentials are missing or invalid → `getFirebaseApp()`
      throws at worker startup; the worker logs a warning and continues without
      notifications — FCM failure must not prevent the worker from processing jobs
- [ ] FCM token is stale (user reinstalled app, token rotated) → FCM returns
      `messaging/registration-token-not-registered`; `isTokenInvalidError` returns
      `true`; `clearDeviceToken` removes the token from the DB; user will re-register
      on next app launch via `POST /users/device-token`
- [ ] `POST /users/device-token` is called multiple times in rapid succession
      (app restarts, token refresh) → `$set` is idempotent; the latest token wins;
      `deviceTokenUpdatedAt` timestamp is always refreshed
- [ ] Same user is logged in on two devices simultaneously → only the most
      recently registered token is stored; only that device receives notifications;
      multi-device fan-out is out of scope for MVP — add a comment noting this
- [ ] Job has no `projectId` (defensive: a job created without a project reference)
      → `resolveDeviceToken` receives `null`/`undefined`; `ProjectModel.findById(null)`
      returns `null`; the function returns `null` silently without throwing
- [ ] FCM API is rate-limited or temporarily unavailable (5xx) → FCM client
      throws; caught by the outer try/catch in `notifyJobOutcome`; logged as
      `warn`; no retry — the job is already in a terminal state, and a missed
      notification is acceptable at MVP; add a comment noting retry logic as a
      future enhancement
- [ ] `FIREBASE_PRIVATE_KEY` contains literal backslash-n (`\\n`) from env var
      parsing → the `.replace(/\\n/g, "\n")` in `firebaseAdmin.ts` converts it
      correctly; test this explicitly in a local `.env` file before deploying

---

## Constraints

- Firebase Admin SDK must be initialized as a singleton — never call
  `admin.initializeApp()` more than once per process; the guard in `getFirebaseApp()`
  enforces this
- `notifyJobOutcome` must be `async` but callers must invoke it as fire-and-forget
  with `.catch(() => {})` — never `await` it on the critical path
- FCM `data` payload values must all be strings — no numbers, booleans, or
  nested objects; `outputS3Key ?? ""` and `errorMessage ?? ""` are already
  handled in `buildPayload`
- Notifications are only sent on terminal states: `COMPLETED` and `FAILED`
  (after all retries exhausted); no notifications for intermediate transitions
  (`QUEUED`, `CLAIMED`, `PROCESSING`)

---

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] When a job transitions to `COMPLETED`, the project owner's device receives
      a push notification with title "Capture Complete ✓" within 30 seconds of
      the worker writing the final status
- [ ] When a job transitions to `FAILED` (all retries exhausted), the project
      owner's device receives a push notification with title "Capture Failed"
- [ ] Intermediate retry failures (job goes back to `QUEUED`) do NOT trigger
      a notification
- [ ] `POST /users/device-token` with a valid token updates `user.deviceToken`
      and returns `200 { success: true }`
- [ ] `POST /users/device-token` without auth returns `401`
- [ ] `POST /users/device-token` with an empty `token` body returns `400`
- [ ] A stale/invalid FCM token is removed from the DB after a failed send —
      confirmed by querying the user document after the notification attempt
- [ ] Missing Firebase credentials at worker startup → worker logs a warning and
      continues processing jobs normally; no crash, no job failure
- [ ] `notifyJobOutcome` never causes an unhandled promise rejection in any
      failure scenario — confirmed by checking worker logs during error conditions
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` passes with zero TypeScript errors in strict mode

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Admin: GET /admin/jobs — list all jobs with status, upload size, device info — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Backend APIs
