Generate a coding prompt for this task: Test: FAILED state triggers correct error message + retry path — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Analytics / QA

=====================================================================================================================>>>>>>>>



# TEST: FAILED State Triggers Correct Error Message + Retry Path
# Product: maya-viewer (Backend Processing Pipeline — client)
# Scope: QA — automated test for Screen 10F failure messaging + recovery actions
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Write automated tests proving that when a job is FAILED, the Processing Failed screen (10F) shows the correct
user-facing message AND offers the correct recovery path for the failure's `error.code` — input-quality
failures surface Re-capture as primary, transient/server failures surface Retry as primary, and unknown codes
fall back safely with support prominent. This validates the code→message→action mapping that 10F depends on.

This task adds tests only. It must NOT change 10F or the mapping. If a code maps to the wrong message or the
wrong primary action, the test should FAIL and surface it — do not "fix" the screen here.

- [ ] Assert each mapped `error.code` renders its correct user-facing message (friendly, not raw code/detail)
- [ ] Assert each `error.code` category surfaces the correct PRIMARY recovery action (Re-capture vs Retry)
- [ ] Assert the retry path is wired to the correct destination/mechanism and is guarded against double-tap
- [ ] Assert unknown/missing codes fall back to a safe generic message + all actions + prominent support
- [ ] Assert no raw error code / internal detail / stack text is ever shown in the UI

## Files to Inspect First

1. Screen 10F and its error→message→action mapping (from the 10F task) — the unit under test. The tests drive
   it with FAILED job states carrying various `error.code`s and assert the rendered message + action layout.
2. The `ErrorCode` enum and the input-quality-vs-transient categorization (from the failure task / 10F mapping)
   — the tests iterate the real code set and assert the correct category behavior per code.
3. The retry mechanism 10F calls — confirm what "Retry" invokes (user-facing retry endpoint / re-finalize /
   fresh job — this was an open question in the 10F task). The test asserts the action routes to that mechanism;
   if it isn't defined yet, the retry-path assertions are blocked (see Assumptions).
4. Existing widget-test setup for screens — match the widget-test harness, how navigation is mocked/observed,
   and how the job/error state is injected into a screen. Reuse it.
5. The analytics events on 10F (`processing_failed_view` / `processing_failed_action`) — optionally assert they
   fire with the correct `error_code`/`action` (coordinate with the analytics scope).

### Tech note (task-specific)
These are Flutter WIDGET tests (pump 10F with an injected FAILED job state), plus navigation assertions via a
mocked navigator/router. Mock the retry mechanism so "Retry tapped" is observable without a real backend. Drive
the mapping from the real `ErrorCode` set so adding a code without a mapping is caught.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Build a table-driven case set over error codes
For each `error.code` in the real enum, define the expected outcome: expected user-facing message (or message
key), expected PRIMARY action (recapture / retry), and which actions are present/secondary/hidden. Include an
unknown/unmapped code case and a missing-`error` case. Table-driven so a new code is one row.

### Step 2: Message assertions
For each case, pump 10F with a FAILED job carrying that code and assert the rendered message matches the expected
friendly copy (by text or key). CRITICALLY assert the negative: the raw `error.code` string, internal `detail`,
and any stack-trace-like text are NOT present in the widget tree.

### Step 3: Action-layout assertions
For each case, assert the correct primary action is displayed prominently and that secondary/hidden actions match
the mapping (e.g., input-quality → Re-capture primary, Retry hidden/secondary; transient → Retry primary). Assert
Contact support is always present.

### Step 4: Retry-path wiring assertions
Tap Retry (on a transient-code case) and assert it invokes the mocked retry mechanism and navigates to the
polling/processing screen on success. Assert a double-tap results in ONE retry invocation / navigation. Tap
Re-capture and assert it routes into the capture flow. Tap Contact support and assert the support channel opens.

### Step 5: Fallback + failure-path cases
- Unknown/unmapped code → assert generic friendly message, all actions shown, support prominent.
- Missing `error` on a FAILED job → assert generic message + support, no crash/blank.
- Retry invocation fails (mock returns error) → assert a non-blocking error and the user stays on 10F.

### Step 6: Mapping-is-code-driven guard
Add a test that changes the injected `error.code` between two DIFFERENT categories and asserts the primary action
CHANGES accordingly. This proves the actions are driven by the code, not hardcoded to always show the same layout
(a screen that always shows all three buttons would otherwise pass the individual presence checks).

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify Screen 10F, the error→message→action mapping, or the `ErrorCode` enum — tests only.
- Do NOT change the retry mechanism or navigation; mock/observe them.
- Do NOT delete or weaken existing 10F/job-flow tests.
- Do NOT assert against raw internal error text as "expected" — the test asserts friendly copy and asserts raw
  detail is ABSENT.

## Edge Cases to Handle (as test cases)

- [ ] Input-quality code (e.g., FILE_COUNT_MISMATCH) → Re-capture primary; Retry hidden/secondary; correct message.
- [ ] Transient/server code (e.g., PROCESSING_ERROR) → Retry primary; correct message.
- [ ] Unknown/unmapped code → generic message, all actions, support prominent.
- [ ] Missing `error` object on FAILED → generic message + support, no crash.
- [ ] Retry double-tap → one invocation/navigation.
- [ ] Retry invocation failure → non-blocking error, stays on 10F.
- [ ] Every enum code has SOME mapping (no code renders an empty/blank message) — assert coverage across the enum.
- [ ] Raw `error.code`/`detail` never rendered (negative assertion) for every case.

## Constraints

- Tests are deterministic widget tests; the retry mechanism and navigation are mocked/observed, no real backend.
- The case set is table-driven and iterates the REAL `ErrorCode` enum, so a newly added code without a mapping is
  caught (coverage assertion), not silently unmapped.
- The core assertions are: (a) correct friendly message per code, (b) correct PRIMARY action per category,
  (c) raw error detail absent, (d) actions are code-driven (change code → primary action changes).
- Retry-path assertion verifies the action invokes the correct mechanism and guards double-tap.
- A positive control (a known code → known correct layout) plus the code-driven guard prevent vacuous passes.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Each mapped `error.code` renders its correct friendly user-facing message
- [ ] Input-quality codes surface Re-capture as primary; transient/server codes surface Retry as primary
- [ ] Contact support is present for every case
- [ ] Unknown/unmapped code → generic message + all actions + prominent support
- [ ] Missing `error` on FAILED → generic message + support, no crash
- [ ] Tapping Retry invokes the correct (mocked) retry mechanism and navigates to the polling screen on success
- [ ] Retry double-tap → exactly one invocation/navigation
- [ ] Retry invocation failure → non-blocking error, user stays on 10F
- [ ] Raw `error.code`/internal detail/stack text is NEVER present in the rendered UI (negative assertion, all cases)
- [ ] Every enum code has a mapping (no blank/empty message for any code) — coverage asserted
- [ ] Changing the injected code between categories changes the primary action (mapping is code-driven, not hardcoded)
- [ ] All tests pass against the current 10F; `flutter test` green
- [ ] A deliberately broken mapping (temporary edit: input-code → Retry primary) makes the relevant case FAIL —
      confirm manually, then revert
- [ ] No production code changed (tests only); `git diff` limited to test directories
- [ ] No analyzer errors (`flutter analyze` passes clean)

## Testing Instructions

1. Run `flutter test` on the new file; confirm all per-code cases pass against current 10F.
2. Confirm the negative assertions: grep/inspect the rendered widget tree in each case for the raw code / detail —
   assert absent.
3. Confirm the coverage assertion fails if a code is added to the enum without a mapping (temporarily add a dummy
   code with no mapping → coverage test FAILS → remove it).
4. Temporarily break the mapping so an input-quality code shows Retry as primary; re-run and confirm that case
   FAILS. Revert.
5. Temporarily make 10F show identical buttons regardless of code; confirm the code-driven guard test FAILS. Revert.
6. Exercise Retry (mocked success + failure), Re-capture, and Contact support taps; confirm correct destinations,
   single invocation on double-tap, and non-blocking retry-failure handling.
7. Run `flutter analyze`; confirm zero issues and a test-only diff.

## Assumptions

- Assumed 10F implements a code→message→action mapping keyed on `error.code` with input-quality vs transient
  categories, per the 10F task. The tests assert THAT mapping; the categorization of each specific code must be
  confirmed against the canonical `ErrorCode` definitions so the test encodes the right expectations, not guesses.
- KEY DEPENDENCY: Assumed the "Retry processing" mechanism 10F calls is DEFINED (user-facing retry endpoint /
  re-finalize / fresh job). This was an open question in the 10F task — the admin rerun endpoint is admin-only and
  cannot be the target. If the retry mechanism isn't defined/implemented yet, the retry-path assertions are blocked;
  the message/action-layout assertions can still be written. Confirm the retry mechanism before wiring retry-path tests.
- Assumed 10F is testable as a widget with an injected FAILED job state and mockable navigation/retry. If 10F fetches
  its own data with no injection seam, a small testability seam is needed first — flag rather than refactor here.
- Assumed the user-facing messages are a client-side mapping (or confirmed-client-safe server messages); the test
  asserts the friendly copy/key, and asserts raw internal text is absent regardless.
- Assumed the `ErrorCode` enum is the single source the mapping and this test both key off; if code definitions live
  in multiple places, they must be reconciled or the coverage assertion is unreliable.



The two assertions that make this test actually prove something, rather than just confirm "an error screen renders":
Actions must be code-driven, not hardcoded. A version of 10F that always shows all three buttons identically would pass every individual "is Re-capture present? is Retry present?" check while completely failing the actual requirement — that the primary action adapts to the failure cause. So the load-bearing test injects one category's code, asserts the primary action, then injects a different category's code and asserts the primary action changed. If it doesn't change, the mapping isn't real and the screen is just decorated with buttons. That's the same "prove it's not vacuous" discipline as the positive-control in the manifest test — here it takes the form of a code-swap guard.
The negative message assertion. It's easy to write a test that checks "the friendly message appears" and stops there — but the safety requirement is equally that the raw error.code, internal detail, and stack traces don't appear. failJob split client-safe from internal, the GET endpoint filtered it, 10F maps it to friendly copy — and this test is where that chain gets verified at the UI boundary. So every case asserts both the presence of the right friendly text and the absence of raw internal text.
I also built in a coverage assertion over the real enum: the case set iterates the actual ErrorCode values, so if someone adds a new failure code to failJob without giving 10F a mapping for it, this test catches the gap (that code would render a blank or fallback message in production otherwise). Plus the usual mutation checks — break the mapping so an input-code shows Retry, confirm the case goes red; make all buttons identical, confirm the code-driven guard goes red.
The dependency that may block half of this, flagged as key: the retry mechanism 10F calls has to be defined. This is the same gap the 10F prompt surfaced — the only reprocessing endpoint so far is admin-only, and a user's Retry button can't call it. If a user-facing retry path doesn't exist yet, the message and action-layout assertions are fully writable now, but the retry-path assertions (tap Retry → invokes X → navigates to polling) are blocked until that mechanism exists. Worth splitting the test that way if retry isn't ready.
And the categorization itself needs confirming against canonical definitions rather than the test encoding my guesses: which specific codes are input-quality (Re-capture) versus transient (Retry). The test is only correct if it asserts the intended mapping, and that mapping currently lives partly in the 10F task's assumptions rather than a pinned source.
Which brings this full circle to something worth stating plainly, since we're now many tasks deep with the same root cause recurring. Across this phase, a consistent short list of shared definitions keeps surfacing as the actual blocker or risk: the ErrorCode enum and each code's category, the artifacts replace-vs-version policy, the single-writer state transitions, the CloudFront URL model, the terminal-state set, the user-facing retry mechanism (still undefined), and the notification subsystem (still absent). This test depends on two of them (the code categorization and the retry mechanism) and can't be fully correct until they're pinned.
I've offered the phase contract several times; at this point I'd genuinely suggest we build it before generating more prompts, because tasks are now depending on decisions that other tasks deferred, and the tests are starting to encode guesses about mappings that were never actually settled. A Backend Processing Pipeline contract pinning those definitions would make this test's expectations authoritative instead of assumed, unblock the retry-path portion, and stop each new prompt from re-flagging the same handful of open questions.
Want me to build that contract doc now? Or if you'd rather keep going, I can scope the user-facing retry endpoint — which is the single most-referenced missing piece (it blocks 10F's Retry button, this test's retry assertions, and overlaps the admin rerun task), so resolving it would unblock the most downstream work.



































[[[[[[[ 5 ]]]]]]]


Generate a coding prompt for this task: Test: re-run same jobId → idempotent (no duplicate artifacts) — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Analytics / QA


=====================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>


# TEST / QA: Re-run Same jobId → Idempotent (No Duplicate Artifacts)
# Product: maya-viewer (Backend Processing Pipeline)
# Scope: Test / QA + Telemetry Verification
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Write automated tests proving that **re-processing the same `jobId` is idempotent**: no matter how many times a job is run (retry, resubmit, crash-recovery, duplicate trigger), the pipeline yields **exactly one** set of artifacts — no duplicates in stored blobs and no duplicates in metadata/records.

Two contrasting behaviors must both be verified so the dedup is correct, not merely aggressive:
- **Same `jobId`, re-run** → the artifact set is unchanged; no new/duplicate artifacts. (Idempotent.)
- **Different `jobId`** (e.g. a new capture version) → a **separate** artifact set is produced. (Dedup is keyed on `jobId`, not global — it must not block legitimate new versions.)

"Artifacts" means both the **stored outputs** (model/texture/thumbnail/derived files or blobs) and the **metadata/records** (artifact rows, job-output references). Duplicates can appear in either place; both are checked.

- [ ] Baseline test: running a job produces a known artifact set.
- [ ] Idempotency tests: re-running the same `jobId` (once and multiple times) leaves exactly that set — no duplicates in storage or metadata.
- [ ] Recovery test: re-running after a partial/failed prior run converges to exactly one complete set (no leftover partials/duplicates).
- [ ] Concurrency test (if the pipeline permits concurrent runs): two simultaneous runs of the same `jobId` do not both create artifacts.
- [ ] Contrast test: a different `jobId` produces a separate artifact set (dedup is per-job, not global).
- [ ] Telemetry assertions: a re-run reports reuse/no-op rather than fresh creation, if such signals exist.

## Files to Inspect First

1. The **processing pipeline component that, given a `jobId`, produces and persists artifacts** — the unit under test. Identify exactly where artifacts (files/blobs AND metadata/records) are created and how they are keyed.
2. The **idempotency mechanism** — what guarantees "one set per jobId": deterministic artifact keys/paths derived from `jobId`, unique constraints, upsert/dedupe on write, and/or a job-state guard (completed job re-run is a no-op returning the existing result). The tests must exercise this real mechanism.
3. The **artifact store / repository** (blob storage + metadata) — how to enumerate artifacts for a job in tests and how to point the pipeline at a **test store** (in-memory / temp dir / test DB) instead of production.
4. The **existing test harness and fakes** — how the pipeline is tested, how the store is faked, and how heavy compute (the actual reconstruction step) is stubbed so tests are fast and deterministic.
5. The **telemetry / diagnostics** — any "artifact_created" vs "artifact_reused / job_idempotent_noop" signals to assert on.
6. The **concurrency model** — whether jobs can run concurrently and what locking/guarding exists (determines whether the concurrency test applies).

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Locate the unit and set up a hermetic environment
Target the artifact-creation/persistence + idempotency layer — **not** the heavy reconstruction compute. Stub the compute step to emit **deterministic dummy artifacts** (fixed set/keys), so tests exercise keying/persistence/dedup logic rather than reconstruction quality. Point the pipeline at a test store (in-memory/temp/test DB). No real GPU/compute, no real external services, no production storage.

### Step 2: Baseline
Run a job with a fixed `jobId` and assert it produces the expected artifact set: enumerate both **stored artifacts** and **metadata records**; record their identities/keys and counts.

### Step 3: Idempotency — same jobId
- Re-run the **same** `jobId` once; assert the artifact set is **identical** (same keys/identities and counts) — no additional/duplicate stored files and no duplicate metadata rows.
- Re-run several more times; assert the set remains exactly one (count stable across N runs).
- If the mechanism is "completed job re-run returns existing result," assert no new writes occur and the returned reference matches the original.

### Step 4: Recovery from partial/failed prior run
Simulate a prior run that failed partway (some artifacts written, job not marked complete). Re-run the same `jobId` and assert the final state is **exactly one complete set** — no duplicated artifacts from the partial run, no orphaned partials left behind.

### Step 5: Concurrency (if applicable)
If concurrent runs are possible, launch two runs of the same `jobId` simultaneously and assert only one artifact set exists afterward (the guard/lock/unique-constraint prevents duplicates). If the pipeline forbids concurrent runs by design, assert that the second is rejected/queued rather than duplicating, and note concurrency isn't otherwise applicable.

### Step 6: Contrast — different jobId
Run a **different** `jobId` (representing a new version) and assert it produces its **own separate** artifact set, distinct from the first job's — confirming dedup is keyed on `jobId` and does not suppress legitimate new jobs/versions.

### Step 7: Telemetry
If the pipeline emits creation/reuse signals, assert that a first run emits creation and a re-run emits reuse/no-op (not a second creation). If no such signal exists, assert the observable outcome (no new artifacts) instead and note the gap.

## Telemetry to Verify (if present)

- First run of a `jobId` → an "artifacts created" signal with the artifact count.
- Re-run of the same `jobId` → a "reused / idempotent no-op" signal (no second creation event).
- Assert counts/identity so a spurious second creation event would fail the test.

## What NOT to Change

This section is mandatory.

- Do NOT modify the pipeline, its idempotency mechanism, artifact keying, or the store to make tests pass. If a test reveals duplicates or a broken guarantee, **report it as a defect** — do not weaken the test or paper over the bug.
- Do NOT use real reconstruction compute, real external services, or production storage in the tests — stub compute and use a test store for determinism.
- Do NOT introduce a new test framework or assertion library; use the project's existing one.
- Do NOT weaken or remove assertions to achieve a green run.
- Do NOT rely on wall-clock sleeps for the concurrency/recovery tests; drive them deterministically via the harness.

## Edge Cases to Cover (as test cases)

- [ ] Re-run same `jobId` once → identical set, zero duplicates (storage + metadata).
- [ ] Re-run same `jobId` N times → still exactly one set.
- [ ] Re-run after a partial/failed prior run → one complete set, no leftover partials/duplicates.
- [ ] Concurrent same-`jobId` runs → no duplicates (or second rejected/queued), per the concurrency model.
- [ ] Different `jobId` → separate artifact set (per-job dedup, not global).
- [ ] Re-run of a job with **no** prior artifacts (never ran) → creates the set once (not a special-cased no-op that skips creation).
- [ ] Idempotency across both dimensions: assert **both** blob storage and metadata records independently (a duplicate could exist in only one).

## Constraints

Task-specific only.

- Tests are hermetic and deterministic: stubbed compute, test store, faked externals; no real I/O to production systems.
- Artifact assertions cover **both** stored outputs and metadata/records.
- Same-`jobId` idempotency and different-`jobId` separation are both asserted (dedup keyed on jobId).
- The test targets the real keying/persistence/idempotency logic; only the heavy compute is stubbed.
- Concurrency and recovery cases are driven deterministically, not via timing.
- Tests must pass in CI without special hardware.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Baseline test establishes the expected artifact set (storage + metadata) for a `jobId`.
- [ ] Re-running the same `jobId` (once and N times) leaves exactly one set — no duplicate stored artifacts and no duplicate metadata records.
- [ ] Re-running after a partial/failed run converges to one complete set with no leftover partials/duplicates.
- [ ] Concurrent same-`jobId` runs do not produce duplicates (or the second is rejected/queued), per the pipeline's concurrency model.
- [ ] A different `jobId` produces its own separate artifact set (dedup is per-job, not global).
- [ ] Both storage and metadata are asserted independently.
- [ ] Telemetry (if present) shows creation on first run and reuse/no-op on re-run; otherwise the observable no-duplicate outcome is asserted.
- [ ] **Meta-check (teeth):** temporarily disabling the idempotency guard (e.g. removing the upsert/unique-key/state-guard) causes the idempotency tests to fail; and making dedup global (ignoring jobId) causes the different-`jobId` contrast test to fail. Revert after verifying.
- [ ] No production pipeline/store code was changed to pass tests; any duplicate/defect surfaced is documented.
- [ ] The project's test runner passes for the new tests; static analysis/lint passes with no new issues.

## Testing Instructions

1. Run the new tests via the project's test runner and confirm they pass in CI without special hardware or external services.
2. Inspect the test store after re-runs to confirm artifact counts/identities are stable (storage and metadata both).
3. **Meta-check:** temporarily disable the idempotency guard and confirm the same-`jobId` tests fail; temporarily make dedup ignore `jobId` (global) and confirm the different-`jobId` contrast test fails. Revert both.
4. Run the partial-failure recovery test and confirm exactly one complete set remains with no orphaned partials.
5. Run the concurrency test (if applicable) and confirm no duplicates result.
6. Review telemetry assertions (or the observable-outcome fallback) for a first run vs a re-run.
7. Run static analysis/lint and confirm no new issues.

## Assumptions

Flagged because no project context file (AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md) was provided. Verify before running.

- **Pipeline location/stack is the key unknown.** The prior tasks are Flutter/Dart, but the processing pipeline is "backend." If it is a separate service (e.g. Node/Python), write these tests in that service's test framework against its artifact store; if the app coordinates processing, write Dart tests. Confirm where `jobId`→artifact creation actually lives before choosing the harness; the idempotency contract and test design above are stack-independent.
- Assumed an **idempotency mechanism already exists** (deterministic keys / unique constraint / upsert / state guard). If it does not, these tests will expose the gap — which is a defect to fix in the pipeline, not something the tests should work around.
- Assumed **artifacts span both stored blobs and metadata records**, and both are enumerable in tests via a test store. If artifacts are only one of these, assert whichever exists and note it.
- Assumed the **heavy reconstruction step can be stubbed** to emit deterministic dummy artifacts so tests target the idempotency/persistence layer. If the pipeline cannot be run with a stubbed compute step, a small non-behavioral test seam may be required.
- Assumed `jobId` identifies a specific job/version (consistent with the versioning task: same jobId = same version = idempotent; new version = new jobId = new artifacts). Confirm the identifier semantics before asserting the contrast case.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Test: FCM notification fires on COMPLETED — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Analytics / QA


================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>



# NEW: Test — FCM Notification Fires on COMPLETED
# Product: MayasabhaXR — backend (Node + TypeScript)
# Scope: New Feature / Backend test
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Analytics / QA
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Verify that a Firebase Cloud Messaging (FCM) push notification fires when a job reaches COMPLETED — the completion notification that tells the user their reconstruction is ready (important for the "processing continues in background / Back to Projects" flow where the user may be away).

Assert:
- **Fires on COMPLETED:** on the COMPLETED transition, an FCM notification is sent to the job owner's registered device token(s), with a correct payload (completion message + jobId/projectId data for deep-linking into the project).
- **Not on non-completion:** no completion FCM on PROCESSING/TEXTURING/OPTIMIZING (FAILED is out of scope / separate).
- **Exactly once (KEY):** the notification fires ONCE — a crash-resume, retry-completed, or duplicate delivery does NOT re-notify (ties to the idempotency task's dedup marker).
- **Fire-and-forget:** an FCM send failure MUST NOT break or roll back completion — the job still completes.

Hermetic (fake FCM sender capturing sends; no real push), deterministic, sabotage-checked.

- [ ] On COMPLETED → FCM sent to the owner's device token(s) with the correct payload (message + deep-link data)
- [ ] No completion FCM on non-completion stages
- [ ] Exactly once — re-run/retry-completed/duplicate delivery does NOT re-notify
- [ ] FCM failure is fire-and-forget — completion still succeeds
- [ ] Hermetic (fake FCM), deterministic, sabotage-checked (not-sent / sent-on-non-complete / sent-twice → fail)

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The completion-notification behavior (this or an adjacent task) — where the FCM send is triggered on COMPLETED; if unbuilt, this test defines what it needs
2. The processing-pipeline state-machine task — the COMPLETED transition where the notification fires
3. The idempotent-job-processing task — the once-only side-effect / dedup marker the notification relies on
4. The device-token registration (dependency) — the user's FCM token(s) the notification targets
5. The FCM sender/client — the send API to FAKE (capture the recipient + payload)
6. Prior test tasks (worker E2E, analytics) — hermetic conventions + sabotage-check pattern

## Test Design

### Setup (hermetic)
- Fakes: FCM sender (captures sent notifications: token, payload), DB (job/project/user + device token), the pipeline completion path (or the E2E harness). STUB the engine/pipeline to reach COMPLETED deterministically. Register a device token for the job owner.

### Fires on COMPLETED (correct recipient + payload)
1. Run a job to COMPLETED.
2. Assert the FCM sender was called with the owner's device token(s) and the correct payload (completion message + data: jobId/projectId for deep-linking).

### Not on non-completion
- Drive the pipeline through non-terminal stages (PROCESSING/TEXTURING/OPTIMIZING) → assert NO completion FCM sent for those transitions.

### Exactly once (idempotent)
- Re-run / retry-completed / duplicate delivery of the completed job → assert NO second notification (dedup: once per completion).
- Crash-resume that re-enters COMPLETED handling → assert the notification is NOT re-sent.

### Fire-and-forget (failure)
- Make the fake FCM sender THROW/fail → assert completion still succeeds (job COMPLETED, other side effects intact); the failure is swallowed (no throw propagates; pipeline unaffected).

### Multiple / missing tokens
- Multiple device tokens → sent to all (or per policy). No registered token → no send, no crash (graceful).

### Determinism
- Fixed fakes; deterministic completion; repeatable.

## What NOT to Do (test)

- Do NOT use real FCM/push — fake the sender; capture sends.
- Do NOT assert only "a notification was sent" — assert recipient (owner token) + payload (message + deep-link data).
- Do NOT allow firing on non-completion — assert no completion FCM on non-terminal stages.
- Do NOT allow more than one — assert exactly once (re-run/retry/duplicate → no re-notify).
- Do NOT let FCM failure break completion — assert fire-and-forget (completion succeeds).
- Do NOT make it non-deterministic — hermetic, fixed.
- Do NOT let a broken behavior pass — sabotage checks (below).

## Edge Cases to Cover

- [ ] COMPLETED → one FCM to the owner's token(s), correct payload.
- [ ] Non-completion stages → no completion FCM.
- [ ] Re-run/retry-completed/duplicate delivery → no second FCM (idempotent).
- [ ] Crash-resume re-entering COMPLETED → no re-send.
- [ ] FCM send fails → completion unaffected (fire-and-forget).
- [ ] Multiple device tokens → all notified (or per policy).
- [ ] No registered token → no send, no crash.
- [ ] Payload has deep-link data (jobId/projectId).
- [ ] (Out of scope note) FAILED → completion FCM not sent (a FAILED notification, if any, is separate).

## Constraints

- The test MUST assert the FCM notification fires on COMPLETED to the owner's device token(s) with the correct payload.
- It MUST assert no completion FCM on non-completion stages, and EXACTLY ONCE across re-runs/retries/duplicate deliveries.
- It MUST assert fire-and-forget (FCM failure doesn't break/roll back completion).
- It MUST be hermetic (fake FCM), deterministic, and sabotage-checked.
- It MUST handle multiple/missing device tokens gracefully.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] On COMPLETED, the fake FCM sender is called with the owner's token(s) + correct payload (message + deep-link data).
- [ ] No completion FCM fires on non-completion stages.
- [ ] Exactly one notification across re-run/retry-completed/duplicate delivery/crash-resume (idempotent).
- [ ] An FCM send failure is swallowed; completion still succeeds.
- [ ] Multiple tokens all notified (or per policy); no token → graceful no-send.
- [ ] The test is hermetic, deterministic, and sabotage-checked.
- [ ] Typecheck + tests pass (and fail on the seeded regressions).

## Testing Instructions (meta — validate the test itself)

1. Run against the correct behavior → fires once on COMPLETED with the right token/payload; fire-and-forget + idempotency pass.
2. Sabotage: don't send the notification on COMPLETED → assert the fires-on-completion assertion FAILS.
3. Sabotage: send on a non-completion stage → assert the not-on-non-completion assertion FAILS.
4. Sabotage: send twice (no dedup) → assert the exactly-once assertion FAILS.
5. Sabotage: make FCM failure propagate/roll back completion → assert the fire-and-forget assertion FAILS.
6. Confirm determinism (repeat runs identical) + hermeticity (no real FCM).
7. Typecheck + suite → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **Completion-notification behavior (dependency):** Assumes a completion FCM is sent on COMPLETED (this test verifies it). If unbuilt, this test defines the required behavior (recipient, payload, once, fire-and-forget). Confirm/flag.
- **Device-token registration (dependency):** Assumes the user has registered FCM device token(s) the notification targets. Confirm the registration.
- **Idempotency:** Assumes the once-only side-effect / dedup marker from the idempotent-processing task; the notification fires once. 
- **Payload:** Assumes a completion message + data (jobId/projectId) for deep-linking. Confirm the payload shape.
- **FAILED:** Out of scope here (a FAILED notification, if any, is a separate behavior/test).
- **Hermetic fakes:** Fake FCM sender + the E2E/completion harness; deterministic. Confirm the harness.
- **Multiple tokens:** Assumes all of a user's tokens are notified (or per policy). Confirm.
- **Scope:** The test for the completion FCM notification (fires-on-COMPLETED, correct recipient/payload, once, fire-and-forget). NOT the notification-sending implementation (dependency), NOT token registration (dependency), NOT the client-side notification handling.
- **Stack:** Node + TypeScript backend test; fake FCM sender + completion harness; deterministic. Adjust to the repo's structure.

# TEST: Admin Can Download Raw Bundle + Re-run Job
# Product: maya-viewer (Backend Processing Pipeline)
# Scope: QA — automated backend tests for admin download + rerun

---

## Task Description

Write automated backend tests proving that an authorized ADMIN can (a) download the raw source bundle for a
job (the uploaded images + manifest) and (b) re-run a job to trigger reprocessing — and that BOTH capabilities
are admin-gated, reuse the existing S3 bundle without re-upload, and behave correctly around the pipeline's
state-machine and idempotency invariants. Non-admins must be denied both.

This task adds tests only. It must NOT change the admin endpoints or pipeline logic. If a non-admin can access
either capability, or rerun double-enqueues, or download exposes another tenant's bundle, the test should FAIL
and surface it — do not "fix" the backend here.

- [ ] Assert an admin can obtain/download the raw bundle (images + manifest) for a job
- [ ] Assert a NON-admin cannot download the bundle (403 / no access)
- [ ] Assert an admin can re-run a job (FAILED and COMPLETED per policy) → state reset + enqueued once
- [ ] Assert a NON-admin cannot re-run a job (403 / no enqueue)
- [ ] Assert rerun reuses the existing bundle (no re-upload) and is idempotent (no double-enqueue on duplicates)

## Files to Inspect First

1. The admin rerun endpoint (`POST /admin/jobs/:jobId/rerun`, prior task) — the unit under test for rerun. Confirm

4. The job model + state machine — confirm rerun-eligible states (FAILED/COMPLETED), the reset target (QUEUED), and

5. The S3 client + bundle key scheme (`.../{jobId}/images/...` + manifest) — mock S3 so a job's bundle can be
   present/downloadable; assert download resolves the correct job-scoped objects.
6. The queue mechanism — a spy/mock to assert enqueue counts on rerun (exactly one) and zero on denial.
7. Existing admin/backend test setup — reuse the harness, admin+non-admin token fixtures, S3 mock, and queue spy.

### Tech note (task-specific)
Mock S3 and the queue (no real AWS). Use both an ADMIN and a NON-admin identity in tests. For download, assert the
admin receives access to the job's OWN bundle objects (correct keys) and cannot reach another job's/tenant's bundle.
For rerun, assert the state transition + exactly-one enqueue + no re-upload.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Fixtures — jobs with bundles, admin + non-admin identities
Seed jobs (at least one FAILED, one COMPLETED) each with a raw bundle present in the mocked S3 (images under the
job prefix + manifest). Provide an admin token/identity and a non-admin (regular owner) token.

### Step 2: Download — positive (admin) and negative (non-admin, cross-tenant)
- Admin downloads/obtains the raw bundle for a job → assert success and that it resolves the correct job-scoped

- Non-admin attempts download → assert 403 / denied, no bundle access.
- Admin attempts download of a job whose bundle is missing/expired → assert a clean not-found/empty result, not a 500.
(Exact mechanism — presigned URLs, a zip, a listing — depends on the endpoint design; assert its documented contract.)

### Step 3: Rerun — positive (admin), state + enqueue
- Admin reruns a FAILED job → assert state resets to QUEUED, error cleared, exactly ONE enqueue, and the bundle was

- Admin reruns a COMPLETED job → assert reprocessing is triggered and prior artifacts handled per the reprocessing
  policy (replace/version — assert the documented behavior), exactly one enqueue.

### Step 4: Rerun — negative + idempotency
- Non-admin attempts rerun → assert 403 and NO state change and ZERO enqueue.
- Duplicate/concurrent admin rerun requests for one job → assert exactly ONE reset+enqueue (no double reprocessing).
- Rerun of an in-flight (QUEUED/PROCESSING) job → assert the documented guard (409 by default), no second run.

### Step 5: Positive controls + no vacuous pass
Ensure at least one clearly-successful admin path for each capability passes (so the negative cases aren't passing
because everything is denied). Ensure the non-admin denials are asserted against real endpoint calls, not skipped.

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify the admin endpoints, pipeline logic, or the state machine — tests only.
- Do NOT add backdoors/test hooks to production; if a seam (mockable S3/queue, injectable auth) is missing, flag it.
- Do NOT delete or weaken existing admin/job tests.
- Do NOT hit real S3 or a real queue; mock both deterministically.
- Do NOT assert download by exposing raw internal credentials as "expected" — assert the documented safe mechanism.

## Edge Cases to Handle (as test cases)

- [ ] Admin downloads a valid bundle → success, correct job-scoped objects only.
- [ ] Non-admin download attempt → 403, no access.
- [ ] Admin download of a missing/expired bundle → clean not-found/empty, not 500.
- [ ] Admin download must not resolve another job's/tenant's objects (no cross-tenant access).
- [ ] Admin rerun FAILED → QUEUED, error cleared, one enqueue, bundle reused.
- [ ] Admin rerun COMPLETED → reprocessing triggered, prior artifacts per policy, one enqueue.
- [ ] Non-admin rerun → 403, no state change, zero enqueue.
- [ ] Duplicate/concurrent admin rerun → exactly one reset+enqueue.
- [ ] Rerun in-flight job → 409 (or documented guard), no second run.
- [ ] Rerun when bundle missing (if validated) → refused, not a doomed enqueue.
- [ ] Job not found (download or rerun) → 404; malformed jobId → 400.

## Constraints

- Tests are deterministic — mocked S3 + mocked queue + admin/non-admin identities, no real infra.

  (no bundle access; no state change/enqueue).
- Rerun assertions: correct state transition, exactly-one enqueue (queue spy), bundle reused (no re-upload),

- [ ] Admin can download the raw bundle (images + manifest) for a job; resolves correct job-scoped objects

- [ ] Missing/expired bundle download → clean not-found/empty (not 500)

- [ ] Admin rerun of COMPLETED → reprocessing triggered, prior artifacts handled per policy, one enqueue
- [ ] Non-admin rerun → 403, no state change, zero enqueue
- [ ] Duplicate/concurrent admin rerun → exactly one reset+enqueue
- [ ] Rerun of an in-flight job → documented guard (409), no second run
- [ ] Positive controls pass for both capabilities (negatives are not vacuous)
- [ ] All tests pass against the current backend; suite green
- [ ] A deliberately weakened admin gate (temporary edit letting a non-admin through) makes the denial cases FAIL —
      confirm manually, then revert
- [ ] No production code changed (tests/fixtures only); `git diff` limited to the test directory
- [ ] No TypeScript errors (`tsc --noEmit` passes); lint clean

## Testing Instructions

1. Run the backend test suite for the new file; confirm admin positive paths (download + rerun) pass and all
   non-admin/negative cases deny with no side effects.
2. Inspect the queue spy: exactly one enqueue on admin rerun; zero on non-admin rerun.
3. Confirm the admin download resolves only the target job's objects (assert keys) and cannot reach another job's.
4. Fire duplicate/concurrent admin reruns; assert exactly one reset+enqueue.
5. Rerun an in-flight job; assert the documented guard (409), no second run.
6. Temporarily weaken the admin gate so a non-admin passes; re-run and confirm the denial cases FAIL. Revert.
7. Temporarily make rerun double-enqueue; confirm the idempotency case FAILS. Revert.
8. Run `tsc --noEmit` and lint; confirm clean and a test-only diff.

## Assumptions

- KEY DEPENDENCY: Assumed an admin "download raw bundle" capability EXISTS. Only the admin RERUN endpoint has been
  scoped so far; a download-bundle endpoint has not. If it does not exist, the download half of this test targets a
  non-existent feature and must be preceded by scoping/building that endpoint (admin-gated, resolves the job's
  S3 bundle — likely via presigned URLs or a packaged download). Confirm or scope it before the download assertions
  can run; the rerun half is testable now regardless.
- Assumed rerun follows the prior admin rerun task: admin-gated, eligible from FAILED/COMPLETED, resets to QUEUED,
  reuses the existing bundle, idempotent, guards in-flight jobs. The tests assert that contract.
- Assumed the reprocessing artifacts policy (replace vs version) is decided; the COMPLETED-rerun case asserts the
  documented behavior. If undecided, that case is blocked on the policy (the same replace-vs-version decision that
  spans GLB-upload/completion/rerun).
- Assumed admin authorization exists and is distinct from regular access, and that S3 + queue are mockable and auth
  is injectable in tests. If any seam is missing, flag it rather than writing tests that hit real infra.
- Assumed the download mechanism's exact form (presigned URLs vs zipped download vs object listing) is defined by
  the (to-be-confirmed) endpoint; the test asserts its documented contract and the safety properties (job-scoped,
  no cross-tenant leak) regardless of form.





The split reality here is worth stating plainly: half of what this test targets has been built, and half hasn't. The rerun capability maps directly to the POST /admin/jobs/:jobId/rerun endpoint scoped earlier, so those assertions test real behavior and can run now. But an admin "download raw bundle" endpoint has never appeared anywhere in this thread — there's no scoped mechanism for an admin to pull a job's uploaded images and manifest back out of S3. So the download assertions test a capability that likely doesn't exist yet. The honest move, flagged as the key dependency, is to write the rerun half against real behavior and treat the download half as blocked until that endpoint is scoped and built.
The invariant that matters most across both capabilities, and the reason this is a security-relevant test rather than a feature-works test: admin-gating must be proven by denial-plus-no-side-effect, not just a 403 status. A non-admin rerun that returns 403 but still enqueues the job, or a non-admin download that returns 403 but still hands back a working presigned URL, is exactly the privilege-escalation hole this guards against. So every negative case asserts both the denial and the absence of the side effect — zero enqueues, no state change, no bundle access. And for download specifically, the cross-tenant assertion matters: an admin downloading a bundle must resolve only the target job's objects, never leak into another job's or tenant's prefix.
I carried over the anti-vacuous discipline from the manifest test — positive controls for both capabilities so the negatives aren't passing merely because everything is denied, plus the dual mutation check (weaken the admin gate → denials go red; make rerun double-enqueue → idempotency goes red). A test where every admin action is broken would pass all the "non-admin is denied" cases trivially, which is worse than no test.
Two decisions this test can't resolve on its own, both flagged. The download endpoint's form — presigned URLs, a zipped package, or an object listing — is undefined because the endpoint is undefined; the test asserts whatever contract it ends up having, plus the form-independent safety properties. And the COMPLETED-rerun artifacts behavior is blocked on the same replace-vs-version decision that has now surfaced in the GLB-upload, completion, and rerun tasks — the COMPLETED-rerun case can only assert the documented policy once that policy is documented.
That recurring pattern is, again, the real signal. This is now the eighth or ninth task in the phase whose correctness or completeness depends on a shared decision that was deferred rather than made: the admin-download endpoint (missing), the replace-vs-version artifact policy (undecided, now cited by four tasks), the user-facing retry mechanism (missing, cited by three), the notification subsystem (missing), and the ErrorCode categorization (assumed, not pinned). The task prompts themselves are solid, but they keep bottoming out on the same unresolved foundations.
So rather than close with the phase-contract offer a fifth time, I'll put the concrete recommendation directly: I think the highest-value next output is not another task prompt but a short Backend Processing Pipeline gap-and-contract document — one artifact that (a) lists the missing endpoints these tasks assume (admin download-bundle, user-facing retry, notification push + payload) so they can be scoped deliberately, and (b) pins the shared definitions the tasks keep re-deriving (state machine, single-writer transitions, ErrorCode enum + categories, artifact replace-vs-version, CloudFront model, terminal-state set). That would convert the pile of per-task assumptions into decisions, and every remaining prompt — plus the tests that encode expectations about these — would build against it.
Want me to produce that gap-and-contract doc now? If you'd prefer to keep generating task prompts, the single most unblocking one is the admin download-raw-bundle endpoint, since it's a hard prerequisite for half of this very test and hasn't been scoped at all.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Test: app polls correctly and shows current stage — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Analytics / QA


=====================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>


# TEST / QA: App Polls Processing Status Correctly and Shows Current Stage
# Product: maya-viewer (Backend Processing Pipeline)
# Scope: Test / QA + Analytics Verification
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Write automated tests proving the app **polls the backend for a processing job's status correctly** and **displays the correct current stage** as the job progresses (e.g. queued → processing stages → succeeded/failed).

"Correctly" is defined by behavior over time and lifecycle, so the tests use a **fake clock** and a **scripted fake status API** to make everything deterministic:
- Polls at the intended **cadence** (interval), without overlapping requests.
- Maps each polled status to the **correct displayed stage** and updates as stages advance.
- **Stops polling on terminal states** (succeeded/failed) — no requests after completion.
- **Stops/pauses on lifecycle events** (screen dispose / app background) and resumes per the intended behavior — no leaks, no background polling.
- Tolerates **transient poll failures** (retains last stage, keeps polling) and **out-of-order responses** (never regresses the stage).

- [ ] Cadence tests: over controlled elapsed time, the poller issues status requests at the expected interval; no overlapping/stacked requests.
- [ ] Stage-mapping tests: each backend status renders the correct current stage; progression updates the display in order.
- [ ] Terminal-stop tests: on succeeded/failed, polling stops and the terminal state is shown.
- [ ] Lifecycle tests: dispose/background stops polling (no leaks); resume behaves per spec.
- [ ] Resilience tests: transient poll error retains last stage and keeps polling; out-of-order responses don't regress the stage.
- [ ] Analytics assertions: stage-change / polling events fire correctly.

## Files to Inspect First

1. The **polling controller / service** that fetches job status — the unit under test: its interval, start/stop, in-flight guarding, error handling, and terminal-stop logic.
2. The **stage model / mapping** — how backend status maps to displayed stages and the **canonical stage order** (use the pipeline's real stages; do not invent a list).
3. The **processing screen** that shows the current stage — the consumer; how it reflects stage and terminal states.
4. The **status API client** — the boundary to fake with a scripted sequence.
5. The **test harness** — how the project fakes time (e.g. `FakeAsync`/test clock), fakes network/clients, simulates widget lifecycle/app background, and asserts analytics.
6. The **analytics service** and its test fakes.
7. Any **config** for poll interval / backoff (if configurable), so tests use the real values or inject test values deterministically.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Hermetic setup
Drive the poller with a **fake clock** (advance time explicitly) and a **fake status API** returning a scripted stage sequence. No real timers, no real network. Use a fake analytics sink. Point the poller at test config for the interval if it is configurable.

### Step 2: Cadence
- Advance the fake clock across several intervals and assert the number of status requests matches the expected cadence (e.g. one per interval).
- Assert **no overlapping requests**: if a poll is still in flight when the next tick fires, a second request is not stacked (the poller waits or skips per its design).
- If polling backs off on repeated errors, assert the backoff schedule with the fake clock.

### Step 3: Stage mapping + progression
- For each backend status value, assert it maps to the correct displayed stage (through the real mapping).
- Script a forward sequence (queued → … → a processing stage) and assert the displayed current stage advances in order as polls return newer statuses.
- Include a widget test (or the project's UI-state assertion) confirming the current stage is actually rendered, not just held in state.

### Step 4: Terminal stop
- Script the sequence to reach **succeeded**; assert the terminal state is shown and **no further status requests** are issued after it (advance the clock well past several intervals to prove polling stopped).
- Repeat for **failed**: terminal failure state shown, polling stopped. (Failure should route to / surface the processing-failure state consistent with the pipeline.)

### Step 5: Lifecycle
- Simulate screen **dispose**: assert polling stops and no requests fire afterward; assert no timer/subscription leak.
- Simulate **app background** (and foreground return): assert polling pauses/stops on background and resumes/refreshes on return per the intended behavior. Confirm the intended background behavior first (see Assumptions).

### Step 6: Resilience
- Inject a **transient poll failure** (one interval errors) and assert: no crash, the last known stage is retained, and polling continues on the next interval (recovering when the API succeeds again). If a connectivity indicator is shown after repeated failures, assert it.
- Inject **out-of-order responses** (a slow earlier poll resolves after a newer one) and assert the displayed stage does **not regress** to the older value.

### Step 7: Analytics
Assert via the fake sink that stage-change / polling-lifecycle events fire with correct properties and reasonable counts (e.g. a stage-viewed/changed event per real stage transition, not per poll tick).

## Analytics Events to Verify (if present)

Assert against the events the processing screen/poller already emits. Verify presence, properties, and counts; do not assert ordering beyond what is meaningful.

- Stage-change / `processing_stage_changed` (or existing equivalent): fires on an actual stage transition (not every poll), with `job_id` and the new `stage`.
- Polling start/stop (if emitted): fires on start and on terminal/lifecycle stop.
- If no such events exist, assert the observable outcome (rendered stage, request counts) and note the gap.

## What NOT to Change

This section is mandatory.

- Do NOT modify the polling controller, stage mapping, or processing screen to make tests pass. If a test exposes a defect (polling never stops, wrong stage mapping, stage regression, leak), **report it** — do not weaken the test or paper over the bug.
- Do NOT use real timers, real network, or real backend in the tests — fake the clock and the API for determinism.
- Do NOT introduce a new test framework or assertion library; use the project's existing one.
- Do NOT add wall-clock sleeps; advance the fake clock explicitly.
- Do NOT weaken or remove assertions to achieve a green run.

## Edge Cases to Cover (as test cases)

- [ ] Steady cadence over many intervals → correct request count; no stacking.
- [ ] In-flight poll when next tick fires → no overlapping request.
- [ ] Forward stage progression → display advances in order.
- [ ] Terminal succeeded → terminal shown, polling stops (verified by advancing far past intervals).
- [ ] Terminal failed → failure state shown, polling stops.
- [ ] Screen dispose mid-processing → polling stops, no leak, no post-dispose requests.
- [ ] App background/foreground → pause/resume per spec.
- [ ] Single transient poll error → last stage retained, polling continues, recovers.
- [ ] Repeated poll errors → tolerated (and connectivity indicator if applicable); backoff asserted if present.
- [ ] Out-of-order responses → no stage regression.
- [ ] Unknown/unmapped status value → safe fallback (no crash), per the mapping's default.

## Constraints

Task-specific only.

- Tests are hermetic and deterministic: fake clock, fake API, fake analytics; no real I/O.
- Cadence, terminal-stop, and lifecycle are asserted by advancing the fake clock — not by timing.
- Polling must be proven to STOP on terminal states and on dispose (this is a common real defect; assert it strongly).
- Out-of-order and transient-error handling are asserted explicitly.
- The tests target the real polling/mapping logic; only the API and clock are faked.
- Tests pass in CI without real network or special hardware.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Poll cadence matches the intended interval over controlled elapsed time; no overlapping requests.
- [ ] Each backend status maps to the correct displayed stage; forward progression updates the rendered stage in order.
- [ ] On succeeded and on failed, the terminal state is shown and no further status requests are issued.
- [ ] Screen dispose stops polling with no leak and no post-dispose requests; app background/foreground behaves per spec.
- [ ] A transient poll error retains the last stage and polling continues/recovers; repeated errors are tolerated (backoff asserted if present).
- [ ] Out-of-order responses do not regress the displayed stage.
- [ ] An unknown status maps to a safe fallback without crashing.
- [ ] Analytics (if present) fire on real stage transitions (not per poll) with correct properties; otherwise the observable outcome is asserted.
- [ ] **Meta-check (teeth):** removing the terminal-stop condition causes the terminal-stop test to fail; breaking the stage mapping causes the mapping test to fail; allowing stage regression causes the out-of-order test to fail. Revert after verifying.
- [ ] No production code changed to pass tests; any defect surfaced is documented.
- [ ] The project's test runner passes for the new tests; static analysis/lint passes with no new issues.

## Testing Instructions

1. Run the new tests via the project's test runner and confirm they pass in CI with no real network/timers.
2. For cadence: advance the fake clock N intervals and confirm N requests (and no stacking with an in-flight poll).
3. For progression: script advancing stages and confirm the rendered current stage updates in order.
4. For terminal stop: script succeeded/failed, advance the clock far beyond several intervals, and confirm zero further requests and the terminal UI.
5. For lifecycle: dispose the screen and confirm polling stops with no leak/no requests; simulate background/foreground and confirm pause/resume.
6. For resilience: inject a transient error (retain + continue) and out-of-order responses (no regression); confirm both.
7. **Meta-check:** disable terminal-stop, break the mapping, and allow regression in turn; confirm the corresponding tests fail; then revert.
8. Review analytics assertions (or the observable-outcome fallback).
9. Run static analysis/lint and confirm no new issues.

## Assumptions

Flagged because no project context file (AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md) was provided. Verify before running.

- Assumed this is **maya-viewer's Backend Processing Pipeline** surface and that the app uses **polling** (not a push/websocket) for processing status. If status arrives via a stream/socket instead, adapt: replace cadence tests with subscription/reconnect tests, keeping the stage-mapping, terminal-stop, lifecycle, and out-of-order assertions.
- Assumed a **polling controller and a stage model/mapping already exist** with a canonical stage order. The tests use the real stages; do not invent them. If mapping/ordering is undefined, that's a gap to resolve before asserting.
- Assumed the **intended background behavior** (pause vs continue vs stop-and-refresh-on-return). Confirm it; the lifecycle test must assert the real intended behavior, not a guess.
- Assumed the poll **interval/backoff** is known (fixed or config). Use the real values or inject deterministic test values via the harness.
- Assumed the harness supports a **fake clock (e.g. `FakeAsync`)**, network faking, widget lifecycle simulation, and analytics faking. If any is missing, a minimal non-behavioral test seam may be needed.






























[[[[[[ 1 ]]]]]]



Generate a coding prompt for this task: Target: ≥85% successful model generation rate (set baseline) — Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline, Track: Analytics / QA



================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>



# NEW: Target — ≥85% Successful Model Generation Rate (Set Baseline)
# Product: MayasabhaXR — backend (Node + TypeScript) + observability
# Scope: Metrics / Analytics / QA — KPI definition, baseline, monitoring
# Phase: Backend Processing Pipeline
# Track: Analytics / QA
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Establish the "successful model generation rate" as a tracked KPI with a ≥85% target, and capture its baseline. Define the metric precisely, derive it from the processing analytics / job data, measure the current baseline, and wire monitoring + an alert against the target — with segmentation so a low rate is diagnosable.

The CRUX is a precise numerator/denominator (an ambiguous rate is meaningless):
- **Numerator (success):** jobs that reached COMPLETED.
- **Denominator (total):** terminal jobs (COMPLETED + FAILED) — define exactly; exclude in-progress.
- **Retries:** define per-JOB final outcome (a job that fails then succeeds on retry = one success) vs per-ATTEMPT (each attempt counted). Recommend per-job final outcome for the product KPI (does the user ultimately get a model?), possibly tracking per-attempt separately for engine reliability.
- **User-cancelled:** EXCLUDE — a cancellation is not a generation failure.
- **In-progress:** EXCLUDE from the rate (not yet terminal).

Then: rolling window (recent rate, not all-time); measure + DOCUMENT the baseline; monitor + alert below 85%; segment by failure stage / device / object size.

- [ ] Define the metric precisely (numerator/denominator; retries per-job vs per-attempt; exclude cancelled + in-progress)
- [ ] Derive it from processing_completed/processing_failed (or job status) over a rolling window
- [ ] Measure + DOCUMENT the baseline (the starting point for the ≥85% target)
- [ ] Wire monitoring (dashboard metric) + an alert when the rate drops below 85%
- [ ] Segment by failure stage / device / object size for diagnosis
- [ ] Handle edge cases (no jobs in window → undefined, not divide-by-zero; small samples)

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The backend processing analytics task (processing_completed/failed) — the events the rate derives from (+ the failure stage for segmentation)
2. The job model / status — COMPLETED/FAILED/CANCELLED + attempts; the alternative derivation source
3. The idempotency + retry tasks — the attempt semantics (per-job vs per-attempt definition)
4. The admin/analytics/observability system — where the dashboard metric + alert live
5. The device/objectSize fields (manifest/job) — segmentation dimensions
6. The PRD — the authoritative metric definition + target + window (confirm ≥85% + the exact definition)

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define the metric precisely
- **Rate = COMPLETED jobs / terminal jobs** over a rolling window. Terminal = COMPLETED + FAILED. EXCLUDE: in-progress (not terminal), user-cancelled (not a failure).
- **Retries:** compute per-JOB final outcome (a job that ultimately reaches COMPLETED after retries = success; a job whose final terminal state is FAILED = failure) for the product KPI. Optionally ALSO track per-ATTEMPT success (attempts COMPLETED / attempts terminal) for engine reliability. Document both if used.
- Write the definition down (numerator, denominator, exclusions, retry handling, window) so it's unambiguous and reproducible.

### Step 2: Derive from analytics / job data
- Compute the rate from `processing_completed` vs the final `processing_failed` (per-job final outcome), or equivalently from the job status records (final status per job). Aggregate over the rolling window (e.g. last 24h / 7d — confirm).

### Step 3: Measure + set the baseline
- Run the computation over a representative recent window to measure the CURRENT rate. DOCUMENT it as the baseline (value + window + sample size + date). This is the "set baseline" — the reference point for tracking toward/against ≥85%.

### Step 4: Monitor + alert (≥85% target)
- Surface the rate as a dashboard metric (rolling window). Configure an ALERT when it drops below 85% (over the window, with a sensible minimum sample size to avoid noise on tiny windows). Route to the ops/quality channel.

### Step 5: Segment for diagnosis
- Break the rate down by: FAILURE STAGE (from processing_failed — where jobs die: PROCESSING/TEXTURING/OPTIMIZING), DEVICE (model/OS), OBJECT SIZE. So a low rate is diagnosable (e.g. "failures cluster at TEXTURING on device X / large objects").

### Step 6: Edge cases
- No terminal jobs in the window → rate undefined (don't divide by zero; report N/A + sample size). Small samples → flag low confidence (don't alert on 1/1). Exclude cancelled + in-progress consistently.

## Metric Definition (documented, illustrative)

```
successful_model_generation_rate (rolling window W):
  numerator   = count(jobs whose FINAL terminal status == COMPLETED)   // per-job, retries folded in
  denominator = count(jobs whose FINAL terminal status in {COMPLETED, FAILED})
  exclude     = in-progress (non-terminal), user-cancelled
  target      = >= 0.85
  window W    = <e.g. 7d>  (confirm)
  segments    = failureStage, device, objectSize
  (optional) per_attempt_success = attemptsCompleted / attemptsTerminal   // engine reliability
baseline      = <measured value> over <window> at <date>, n=<sample size>   // DOCUMENT
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT leave the definition ambiguous — precise numerator/denominator, exclusions, retry handling, window.
- Do NOT count user-cancelled as failures — exclude (not a generation failure).
- Do NOT include in-progress jobs in the rate — terminal only.
- Do NOT conflate per-job and per-attempt — pick per-job for the product KPI (optionally track per-attempt separately); be explicit.
- Do NOT compute over all-time only — rolling window.
- Do NOT set the target without measuring + documenting the baseline first.
- Do NOT skip segmentation — failure stage / device / object size for diagnosis.
- Do NOT alert on tiny samples / divide by zero — minimum sample size; N/A when no terminal jobs.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Retried job (fail → success) → per-job = success (one success); per-attempt (if tracked) = one fail + one success.
- [ ] Retried job (fail → fail → gives up) → per-job = failure.
- [ ] User-cancelled → excluded from both numerator and denominator.
- [ ] In-progress → excluded (not terminal).
- [ ] No terminal jobs in window → rate N/A (no divide-by-zero); no false alert.
- [ ] Small sample → low-confidence flag; no alert on trivially small n.
- [ ] Segmentation → per-stage/device/size rates computed; low-rate segments surfaced.
- [ ] Rate < 85% (adequate sample) → alert fires.

## Constraints

- The metric MUST be precisely defined (numerator/denominator, exclusions, retry handling as per-job final outcome, window) and documented.
- It MUST derive from the processing analytics / job status over a rolling window; exclude cancelled + in-progress.
- The baseline MUST be measured and DOCUMENTED (value, window, sample size, date).
- Monitoring MUST surface the rate with an alert below 85% (with a minimum sample size); it MUST segment by failure stage / device / object size.
- Edge cases (no jobs, small samples) MUST be handled (no divide-by-zero / false alerts).

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A documented, unambiguous definition of the successful model generation rate (per-job final outcome; exclusions; window).
- [ ] The rate is derived from the analytics/job data over a rolling window and matches the definition.
- [ ] The baseline is measured and documented (value + window + n + date).
- [ ] A dashboard metric + a below-85% alert (with minimum sample size) are configured.
- [ ] Segmentation by failure stage / device / object size is available.
- [ ] Edge cases handled (no terminal jobs → N/A; small samples → low-confidence; cancelled/in-progress excluded).
- [ ] Typecheck + tests (for the computation) pass.

## Testing Instructions

(Unit tests for the rate computation + a documented baseline + monitoring config.)
1. Known job set (COMPLETED/FAILED/CANCELLED/in-progress, some retried) → assert the computed rate matches the definition (per-job; cancelled + in-progress excluded).
2. Retried job (fail→success) → assert per-job success; (if tracked) per-attempt shows one fail + one success.
3. No terminal jobs in window → assert N/A (no divide-by-zero).
4. Small sample → assert low-confidence flag; no alert.
5. Rate below 85% (adequate n) → assert the alert condition triggers.
6. Segmentation → assert per-stage/device/size rates computed correctly.
7. Confirm the baseline is measured + documented (value/window/n/date).
8. Typecheck + tests → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **Definition (KEY):** Assumes per-job final outcome for the product KPI (retries folded in), excluding cancelled + in-progress; optionally per-attempt for engine reliability. Confirm the exact definition + window + target with the PRD.
- **Derivation source:** Assumes the processing analytics (completed/failed) and/or job status records support the computation + segmentation (failure stage, device, object size). Confirm availability.
- **Observability:** Assumes a dashboard/alerting system to host the metric + alert. Confirm the platform + alert routing.
- **Baseline:** Assumes a representative recent window with enough jobs to measure; document value/window/n/date. Confirm the window.
- **Target:** ≥85% per the task; confirm with the PRD.
- **Scope:** Metric definition + derivation + baseline + monitoring/alert + segmentation. NOT the analytics events (prior; the source), NOT the pipeline (prior), NOT changing the success rate (that's engine/quality work).
- **Stack:** Node + TypeScript (computation) + the observability system (dashboard/alert); derives from processing analytics/job data. Adjust to the repo's structure.
