# PHASE P7 — Backend Processing Pipeline — Master Build Prompt (All Tracks)
# Product: maya-viewer
# Type: Phase orchestration prompt (multi-track, multi-session)
# Priority: High

---

## How to use this prompt

This is a **phase-level orchestration prompt**, not a single executable task. Do **not** attempt the whole phase in one agent session — it spans multiple tracks and would over-scope a single run.

Use it to:
1. **Lock the shared contracts** in Section "Shared Foundations" first — every track depends on them, and settling them once prevents each task from re-guessing (the individual task prompts each flagged these as assumptions).
2. **Follow the Build Order** so tracks don't collide or build on undefined interfaces.
3. **Execute each work item via its dedicated task prompt** (referenced below). This document coordinates; the task prompts implement.

The agent's first action for this phase is the **architecture & contracts pass** (Build Order step 1) — building the pipeline before the contracts are settled is the primary risk here.

## Phase Goal

Turn an uploaded capture bundle into a processed, viewable result — with a job/version model, robust **idempotent** server-side processing, **live processing status** in the app, **non-destructive versioning**, clear **failure handling**, and **QA coverage** across the phase.

Entry point: a finalized upload bundle (from the Upload Pipeline phase) exists for a job.
Exit point: the user can view the processed result, see version history, watch live processing stages, recover from failures, and re-capture to create a new version — all provably idempotent and tested.

## Tracks in this phase

- **Backend / Processing Pipeline** — server-side job processing: consume the bundle, run stages, produce artifacts idempotently, expose status.
- **Flutter UI** — processing status/stage screen, processing-failed screen, job versioning + history.
- **Upload Engine (integration/handoff)** — job creation and bundle handoff into processing (bridges the prior phase to this one).
- **Analytics / QA** — idempotency, polling/stage, versioning-non-destructive, and failure-presentation tests, plus telemetry.

> Track list is inferred from the phase's known work items (versioning UI, idempotency test, polling test) plus the pipeline they imply. Confirm the actual P7 task inventory; add/remove tracks accordingly.

---

## Shared Foundations & Contracts (define/confirm FIRST — all tracks depend on these)

These are the single sources of truth. No track may redefine them locally. Resolving them here is the main purpose of this document.

### 1. Job & Version model
- A **project** has one or more **job versions**. Each version = one capture→upload→process cycle.
- **`jobId` semantics (critical, phase-wide):** the **same `jobId` = the same version** → re-running it is **idempotent** (no new artifacts). A **new version = a new `jobId`** → produces its **own** artifact set.
- Fields (confirm/extend against the real model): `jobId`, `projectId`, `versionNumber`, `createdAt`, `status` (queued/processing/succeeded/failed), `stage` (current stage), `isCurrent`, artifact references.

### 2. Processing stages
- A **canonical, ordered stage enum** (e.g. queued → preprocessing → reconstruction → texturing → finalizing → succeeded/failed — use the pipeline's real stages, do not invent).
- One definition shared by the **backend** (emits stages) and the **client stage mapping** (displays them). No divergent copies.

### 3. Status API contract
- How the client learns status: **poll vs push** (decide explicitly — this shapes the client status layer and its tests).
- Payload shape: `jobId`, `status`, current `stage`, optional progress, terminal marker, and on failure an **error category** (see Failure taxonomy).
- If polling: the expected **interval/backoff**; terminal states that stop polling.

### 4. Artifact contract
- What artifacts are produced (model/textures/thumbnails/derived files) and how they are **keyed by `jobId`** (deterministic keys/paths).
- Where stored: **blob storage + metadata records** (both dimensions matter for idempotency).
- How the viewer consumes them.

### 5. Idempotency contract
- Re-running a `jobId` yields **exactly one** artifact set — via deterministic keys, unique constraints/upsert, and/or a job-state guard. Safe under retry, resubmit, crash-recovery, and concurrency.

### 6. Failure taxonomy
- Processing error categories (transient vs terminal; e.g. transient-compute, input-invalid, resource/quota, unknown) → mapped to **user-facing presentation** on the client, mirroring the Upload phase's Screen 9F approach (friendly, actionable, no raw errors/PII).

### 7. Telemetry & analytics conventions
- Event naming and properties consistent across pipeline + client; **no PII**; categories aligned (stage changes, failures, idempotent no-ops) so backend telemetry and client analytics reconcile.

### 8. Non-functional constraints (phase-wide)
- Client: low-end Android safe, offline-tolerant reads (cached status/history), heavy work off the main isolate, no UI flooding.
- Backend: idempotent, concurrency-safe, no PII in artifacts/manifests/telemetry.

---

## Build Order & Dependencies

Execute roughly in this order; later steps depend on earlier contracts.

1. **Architecture & contracts pass (Backend/foundations)** — settle the pipeline stack and Sections 1–8 above. **Gate for everything else.** (Consider a dedicated architecture task before coding.)
2. **Backend processing pipeline** — consume the uploaded bundle, run the stages, produce **idempotent** artifacts (keyed by `jobId`), expose the **status API**. Depends on: 1.
3. **Upload→Processing handoff** — job/version creation on upload completion; hand the bundle to processing; ensure a new capture cycle maps to the correct `jobId`/version. Depends on: 1, 2.
4. **Client status layer + Processing status UI** — polling (or push) controller + **stage mapping** + the screen that shows the current stage (queued→stage→done). Depends on: 2, 3, Sections 2–3.
5. **Processing-failed UI** — the 9F-analog for processing failures (reason + retry/back), driven by the Failure taxonomy. Depends on: 3, 4, Section 6.
6. **Job versioning UI** — re-capture → new version (non-destructive), history in the overflow (⋮) menu, live per-version status. Depends on: 1, 3.  → **[Task prompt: "Job Versioning — Re-capture Creates a New Version"]**
7. **QA / Analytics** — the phase's test suite. Depends on all above.
   - Idempotency (same `jobId` → no duplicate artifacts). → **[Task prompt: "Re-run Same jobId → Idempotent"]**
   - Polling/stage (app polls correctly, shows current stage). → **[Task prompt: "App Polls Correctly and Shows Current Stage"]**
   - Versioning non-destructive (re-capture adds, never overwrites).
   - Failure presentation (categories → correct client UX, no raw leakage).

---

## Per-Track Work Items

Each item below is executed via its own focused task prompt (existing ones are named; the rest need a task prompt generated in the same style before coding).

### Backend / Processing Pipeline
- **Architecture & contracts** (step 1) — *needs task/architecture prompt.* Settle stack + Sections 1–8. This is the highest-leverage step; do not skip.
- **Pipeline execution + idempotent artifacts + status API** (step 2) — *needs task prompt.* Core deliverable of the phase; must satisfy the Idempotency and Artifact contracts.

### Upload Engine (integration/handoff)
- **Job/version creation + bundle handoff** (step 3) — *needs task prompt.* Bridges the Upload phase to processing; establishes `jobId`/version per cycle.

### Flutter UI
- **Processing status/stage screen** (step 4) — *needs task prompt.* Consumes the status layer; shows the live stage; handles terminal transitions to result/failure.
- **Processing-failed screen** (step 5) — *needs task prompt.* Failure-taxonomy → friendly reason + retry/back; no raw errors/PII.
- **Job versioning + history** (step 6) — **existing prompt: "Job Versioning — Re-capture Creates a New Version, History in Overflow Menu."** Non-destructive; history in ⋮ menu.

### Analytics / QA
- **Idempotency test** — **existing prompt: "Re-run Same jobId → Idempotent (No Duplicate Artifacts)."**
- **Polling/stage test** — **existing prompt: "App Polls Correctly and Shows Current Stage."**
- **Versioning-non-destructive test** — *needs task prompt.* Re-capture adds a version; prior versions/artifacts remain; no overwrite path.
- **Failure-presentation test** — *needs task prompt.* Each processing error category maps to the correct client UX; no raw leakage.

---

## Cross-Cutting "What NOT to Change / Violate" (phase-wide)

- Do NOT let any track redefine the shared contracts (Sections 1–8) locally — they are the single source of truth.
- Do NOT make versioning destructive — re-capture only ever adds a version; no overwrite/delete path in the UI.
- Do NOT break `jobId` idempotency — re-running a `jobId` must never duplicate artifacts (storage or metadata); a new version must still get its own set.
- Do NOT expose raw errors, stack traces, tokens, paths, or PII in UI, artifacts, manifests, or telemetry.
- Do NOT block the client's main isolate; tolerate offline for reads; stay low-end-Android safe.
- Do NOT poll after terminal states or after screen dispose (no leaks/battery drain).
- Do NOT change the prior phases' capture, gating, bundle, transport, or retry behavior beyond the defined handoff.

---

## Phase Definition of Done

- **End-to-end happy path:** an uploaded bundle is processed through all stages and the result is viewable; the client shows live stage progression and a clean completion.
- **Versioning:** re-capture creates a new version (non-destructive); history is visible in the ⋮ menu with correct current-version indication and live per-version status.
- **Idempotency:** re-running the same `jobId` (retry/resubmit/crash-recovery/concurrent) produces exactly one artifact set; a new version produces a separate set — both proven by tests.
- **Failure path:** a processing failure surfaces a friendly, actionable reason with retry/back; no raw leakage; retry re-attempts correctly.
- **Polling/status:** the client polls (or subscribes) correctly, shows the right stage, stops on terminal/dispose, and tolerates transient errors and out-of-order responses — proven by tests.
- **Each track's acceptance criteria** (from its task prompt) are met.
- **Integration coverage:** happy path + failure path + re-capture/new-version path exercised end-to-end.
- **Static analysis/lint and all new tests pass; any native changes build on Android and iOS.**

Flagged because no project context file (AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md) was provided.

- **Pipeline stack is the pivotal decision.** The processing pipeline is "backend"; prior phases are Flutter/Dart. Confirm whether it's a separate service (e.g. Node/Python) or app-coordinated — this determines where steps 1–3 are built and which test framework the QA track uses.
- **Poll vs push** for status — shapes step 4 and its tests.
- **What already exists vs net-new:** the individual task prompts assume a job/version model, stage enum, status API, artifact store, and idempotency mechanism exist. If any are missing, they are prerequisites within steps 1–2, not client concerns.
- **The full P7 task inventory** may include items not represented here (only the versioning UI and two QA tests were given explicitly). Reconcile this breakdown with the real backlog and generate the "*needs task prompt*" items in the same focused style before coding them.
- **Artifact scope** (which outputs, blobs vs metadata) and **stage set** are contract details to confirm in step 1; the QA prompts assert against whatever is defined there.

Before writing any pipeline code, run the **architecture & contracts pass** (Build Order step 1) to lock Sections 1–8, then generate focused task prompts for each "*needs task prompt*" item and execute them in Build Order. This document stays the reference the individual prompts point back to.

# NEW: Screen 11 — Model Ready (name, version chip, file size, date, status "Ready")
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client)
# Scope: New Feature / Flutter UI — model-ready metadata screen
# Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement
# Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer

---

## Task Description

Build Screen 11: the "Model Ready" screen shown when a reconstruction has completed. It displays the model's metadata — name, a version chip, file size, completion date, and a "Ready" status — and launches into the 3D preview / AR placement (the phase's next screens).

It READS the completed job's data (does not recompute/fabricate): name from the project, version from the job (a project accumulates versions via re-captures — the chip reflects which reconstruction this is, tied to the job history), file size + date from the artifact/quality-report metadata, and "Ready" as the user-friendly surfacing of COMPLETED.

- [ ] Display: model name, version chip, file size (human-readable), completion date, status "Ready"
- [ ] Read the data from the completed job/artifact (name, version, size, date, status) — consistent with the backend
- [ ] Version chip reflects THIS model's version (tied to the job history; a project has versions)
- [ ] Provide actions: View (3D preview) / Place in AR → the phase's next screens (no dead-end)
- [ ] Show metadata without downloading the full model (metadata is lightweight; model download is for preview)
- [ ] Handle loading + error states on the metadata fetch

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The project-completion-update task — `latestCompletedJobId` + `status=COMPLETED` (the completed job this screen shows)
2. The quality-report + artifact tasks — file sizes + artifact refs (the model file size + metadata)
3. The job-history endpoint — the version list (the chip's version; viewing a specific version)
4. The client status/download endpoints (dependencies) — the metadata source + the model download (for View → preview)
5. The projects hub (P1) — arriving here (a COMPLETED project → Model Ready); the project name
6. The app's theming/design tokens — the version chip + status indicator styling; the 3D-viewer/AR navigation targets

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Fetch model metadata
- Fetch the completed job's model metadata (name, version, file size, completion date, status) from the backend (status/history/download endpoints or the job record). Metadata is lightweight — do NOT download the full model to show this screen. Loading state while fetching; error state on failure (retry).

### Step 2: Display the fields
- **Model name:** the project/model name.
- **Version chip:** a chip showing this model's version (e.g. "v3" or the version label), reflecting which reconstruction this is (a project accumulates versions via re-captures). Tie to the job history.
- **File size:** human-readable (e.g. "24.3 MB"), from the artifact/quality-report size.
- **Date:** the completion date (formatted).
- **Status:** "Ready" (the user-friendly surfacing of COMPLETED).

### Step 3: Actions (no dead-end)
- Provide primary actions launching the phase's next screens: **View** (open the 3D preview) and **Place in AR** (AR placement). View may trigger the model download (artifact) — coordinate with the download/preview task. Don't leave the screen a dead-end.

### Step 4: Version context
- The chip reflects the version shown (default: the latest completed = `latestCompletedJobId`; or the version navigated to from history). Optionally allow viewing other versions (via history) — confirm scope; default to the current model's version.

### Step 5: States + consistency
- Loading / error / ready states. The displayed size/date/version MUST be consistent with the backend (quality report / job record) — read, don't recompute. Guard: only show "Ready" when the job is actually COMPLETED (shouldn't arrive otherwise, but reflect the actual status).

## Component / State Contract (Dart, illustrative)

```dart
class ModelReadyScreen {
  final String projectId; final String jobId; // the completed job/version
  // fetches: { modelName, version, fileSizeBytes, completedAt, status }
  // renders: name, version chip, human-readable size, date, "Ready" status
  final VoidCallback onView;      // -> 3D preview (may trigger model download)
  final VoidCallback onPlaceInAr; // -> AR placement
}
```
Rules: read backend metadata (don't fabricate/recompute); version chip tied to history; human-readable size; "Ready" = COMPLETED; actions to preview/AR; metadata without full-model download; loading/error states.

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT recompute/fabricate the metadata — display the backend's (name, version, size, date, status).
- Do NOT hardcode the version — from the job (a project has versions; the chip reflects this one, tied to history).
- Do NOT show "Ready" for a non-COMPLETED model — reflect the actual status.
- Do NOT download the full model just to show metadata — metadata is lightweight; the model download is for View/preview.
- Do NOT dead-end — provide View / Place in AR actions.
- Do NOT diverge size/date/version from the backend — consistent.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Latest version → chip shows the current version; metadata for `latestCompletedJobId`.
- [ ] Older version (from history) → chip + metadata for that version (if in scope).
- [ ] Large file size → formatted human-readable (MB/GB).
- [ ] Metadata fetch loading → loading state; fetch error → error + retry.
- [ ] View → triggers the model download (artifact) for the preview (coordinate with the download/preview task).
- [ ] Model not actually COMPLETED (shouldn't arrive) → reflect actual status; don't falsely show "Ready".
- [ ] Multiple versions → the chip (and optional version access via history); default to this version.
- [ ] Missing size/date (older/partial data) → graceful (omit/placeholder), not a crash.

## Constraints

- The screen MUST display name, version chip, file size (human-readable), date, and "Ready" status, READ from the completed job/artifact (no recomputation/fabrication).
- The version chip MUST reflect this model's version, tied to the job history.
- "Ready" MUST correspond to the actual COMPLETED status.
- Metadata MUST be shown without downloading the full model; the download is for View/preview.
- The screen MUST provide View / Place-in-AR actions (no dead-end) and handle loading/error.
- Displayed metadata MUST be consistent with the backend (quality report / job record).

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Screen 11 shows the model name, a version chip, human-readable file size, completion date, and a "Ready" status, read from the completed job/artifact.
- [ ] The version chip reflects the shown model's version (tied to history); metadata is consistent with the backend.
- [ ] View / Place-in-AR actions launch the phase's next screens (View may trigger the model download).
- [ ] Metadata displays without downloading the full model; loading/error states handled.
- [ ] "Ready" corresponds to the actual COMPLETED status; missing fields handled gracefully.
- [ ] `flutter analyze` passes; widget tests pass.

## Testing Instructions

(Widget tests with a fake metadata source.)
1. Completed job with known metadata → assert name, version chip, human-readable size, date, "Ready" rendered correctly.
2. Assert the version chip reflects the job's version (tie to history); metadata matches the backend.
3. Tap View → assert navigation to the 3D preview (and the model download triggered per the download/preview task).
4. Tap Place in AR → assert navigation to AR placement.
5. Metadata fetch loading → assert loading state; error → assert error + retry.
6. Large size → assert human-readable formatting; missing size/date → assert graceful.
7. Confirm metadata is NOT gated on a full-model download.
8. `flutter analyze` + widget tests → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **Metadata source (dependency):** Assumes the model metadata (name, version, size, date, status) is available via the status/history/download endpoints or the job record. Flag if the endpoint is unbuilt; this screen defines what it needs.
- **Version:** Assumes a project accumulates versions (re-captures) and the chip reflects the shown version (default `latestCompletedJobId`), tied to the job history. Confirm the version label.
- **File size:** Assumes the model file size comes from the artifact/quality-report metadata. Confirm the source.
- **Actions:** Assumes View → 3D preview (next task) and Place in AR → AR placement (later task); View may trigger the artifact download (separate). Confirm the navigation targets.
- **Arrival:** Assumes arriving from a COMPLETED project (hub) or from Screen 10 on Done.
- **Scope:** The Model Ready metadata/status screen + navigation. NOT the 3D preview/rendering (next task), NOT AR placement (later), NOT the model download (separate), NOT the backend metadata endpoint (dependency).
- **Stack:** Flutter/Dart screen; fetches model metadata; version chip + status; navigation to preview/AR. Adjust to the app's structure.





























[[[ 13 ]]]


Generate a coding prompt for this task: 3D viewer: flutter_3d_controller or model_viewer_plus plugin for GLB rendering — Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement, Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer

====================================================================================================>>>>




# NEW FEATURE: 3D Model Viewer — GLB Rendering (flutter_3d_controller or model_viewer_plus)
# Product: maya-viewer (Model Preview + AR Placement)
# Scope: New Feature (Flutter UI + 3D Viewer)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Render the processed **GLB** model (produced by the Backend Processing Pipeline) in an interactive in-app **3D viewer**, using either **`flutter_3d_controller`** or **`model_viewer_plus`**. Wrap the chosen plugin behind a small **abstraction** so the concrete renderer can be swapped later without rippling through the app — important because the sibling **AR Placement** task will likely use a dedicated AR plugin regardless of which viewer is chosen here.

Both candidate plugins render GLB by embedding Google's `<model-viewer>` web component in a **WebView**, so they share the same platform configuration and the same low-end-device caveats — this is not a native-vs-web decision. Choose based on the in-app control you need versus AR-handoff convenience, then hide it behind the abstraction.

- [ ] Add and configure the chosen plugin (verify the current version + setup on pub.dev at implementation time — these move).
- [ ] Build a `Model3DView` abstraction (interface + one implementation) that takes a GLB source and renders it, exposing camera controls, load progress, and error state.
- [ ] Load the pipeline's GLB (prefer a downloaded local file; URL as fallback) with loading, empty, and error states.
- [ ] Support basic interaction: orbit/rotate, zoom, reset camera (and optional auto-rotate).
- [ ] Apply the required Android/iOS platform configuration for the WebView-based renderer.
- [ ] Manage lifecycle/memory (single instance, dispose on leave) for low-end Android.
- [ ] Fire analytics for view open, model loaded, and load failure.

## Files to Inspect First

1. The **model/artifact reference** from the processing pipeline — how a completed job/version exposes its GLB (a URL, a storage ref, or a downloaded local path). This is the viewer's input.
2. The **screen that hosts model preview** (likely the result/version screen from the versioning task) — where the viewer mounts, and its overflow (⋮)/actions.
3. Any **existing WebView usage / platform config** — because both plugins use WebView, check for existing Android manifest / iOS Info.plist settings to avoid conflicts.
4. The **file/download utility** — to fetch the GLB to a local file for offline, CORS-free, low-end-friendly rendering.
5. The **theme / design tokens** and existing loading/error components.
6. The **analytics service**.
7. **pub.dev** for the current version, platform support, and setup steps of the chosen plugin (do this at implementation time; do not rely on remembered specifics).

## Plugin Selection (decide, then abstract)

Evaluate against the real requirements; both share WebView caveats (below), so weigh these differentiators:
- **`flutter_3d_controller`** — richer controller API (camera orbit/target, animations, textures/variants), `onProgress`/`onLoad`/`onError` callbacks, and a gesture-interceptor that fixes gesture issues on iOS/some Android. Stronger for an interactive in-app preview with explicit loading/error control. No built-in AR launch.
- **`model_viewer_plus`** — simpler widget with built-in AR handoff (`ar: true` → Scene Viewer on Android / Quick Look on iOS, graceful 3D fallback without ARCore). Fewer in-app control hooks.

**Recommended default:** `flutter_3d_controller` for this in-app preview task — its explicit load progress/error callbacks and camera control map directly to the required states, and AR will be handled by the dedicated AR Placement task rather than the viewer's built-in handoff. Confirm against current pub.dev status and the AR task's plan before committing; the abstraction makes reversing this cheap.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Add and configure the plugin
Add the chosen dependency at its current version (from pub.dev). Apply the WebView platform setup (see Platform Configuration). Keep all plugin-specific types confined to the single implementation file behind the abstraction.

### Step 2: Build the abstraction
Define a `Model3DView` widget + a small interface, e.g.:
```
Model3DView({
  required Model3DSource source,   // local file path preferred; URL fallback
  Model3DController? controller,    // resetCamera(), (optional) autoRotate toggle
  ValueChanged<double>? onProgress, // 0..1
  VoidCallback? onLoaded,
  ValueChanged<Object>? onError,
})
```
Implement it once with the chosen plugin; the rest of the app depends only on `Model3DView`/`Model3DSource`/`Model3DController`, never on the plugin's types. This is what makes the renderer swappable and keeps the AR task decoupled.

### Step 3: Source the GLB (prefer local file)
Resolve the model source from the pipeline artifact:
- **Preferred:** download the GLB to a local file (via the file utility) and render from the local path. This avoids WebView CORS issues, works offline once cached, and is gentler on low-end devices.
- **Fallback:** render from a URL — but then the model must be served with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (WebView CORS). Flag this dependency if the URL path is used.
Cache the downloaded file so re-opening the preview does not re-download.

### Step 4: States
Using the plugin's callbacks (or model-viewer's loading/poster):
- **Loading** → progress indicator driven by `onProgress`.
- **Loaded** → interactive model.
- **Error** (bad/failed GLB, unsupported, WebView too old) → friendly error state with a retry, no raw errors; distinguish "model failed to load" from "no model yet."
- **Empty** (no processed model available) → appropriate empty state.

### Step 5: Interaction
Enable orbit/rotate, zoom, and a **reset camera** control; optional auto-rotate (default off to save battery on low-end). Use the plugin's camera API where available (e.g. `flutter_3d_controller`'s camera orbit/target/reset). Ensure gestures work on iOS/Android (enable the gesture interceptor if using `flutter_3d_controller`).

### Step 6: Lifecycle & memory
WebView-backed rendering is memory-heavy on low-end Android:
- Mount a **single** viewer instance; do not stack multiple WebViews.
- **Dispose** the viewer/WebView when leaving the screen to release memory.
- Avoid rebuilding the WebView on unrelated state changes.

### Step 7: Analytics
Fire the events below.

## Platform Configuration (WebView-based renderer — required)

Apply per the chosen plugin's current docs; typically:
- **Android** `AndroidManifest.xml`: `android:usesCleartextTraffic="true"` (Android 9+ localhost proxy), and a `<queries>`/intent entry if launching Scene Viewer (only if using the viewer's AR). Confirm the localhost HTTP permission requirement.
- **iOS** `Info.plist`: `io.flutter.embedded_views_preview = YES`.
- **System WebView/browser**: an outdated Android System WebView/Chrome can render a blank screen or low quality — handle via the error/fallback state and note the minimum supported version. Verify current requirements on pub.dev.

## Analytics Events

Match existing conventions.

Event: `model_preview_opened` — `project_id`, `version` (or `job_id`).
Event: `model_preview_loaded` — `project_id`, `version`, `load_ms` (if available from progress/onLoad timing).
Event: `model_preview_load_failed` — `project_id`, `version`, `reason` (mapped: `bad_model` / `network` / `webview_unsupported` / `unknown`).

## What NOT to Change

This section is mandatory.

- Do NOT leak the concrete plugin's types outside the single implementation file — the app depends only on the `Model3DView` abstraction.
- Do NOT change the processing pipeline, the artifact/GLB output, or the versioning UI beyond consuming the model.
- Do NOT stack multiple WebView/viewer instances or leave them undisposed.
- Do NOT expose raw plugin/WebView errors to the user; map to friendly states.
- Do NOT hardcode the model source; resolve it from the pipeline artifact.
- Do NOT build AR placement here — that is the sibling task; keep the viewer AR-agnostic behind the abstraction.
- Do NOT introduce new styles outside the design system.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] GLB fails to load / invalid model → error state with retry; no crash; no raw error.
- [ ] No processed model available yet → empty state (distinct from load failure).
- [ ] Offline with a cached local GLB → renders from cache; offline without cache → clear message.
- [ ] Outdated System WebView/browser → blank/low-quality render handled via the error/fallback path, not a silent blank screen.
- [ ] Large/heavy GLB on low-end Android → renders without OOM where feasible; if the device can't handle it, fail gracefully rather than hard-crash.
- [ ] Rapid navigation in/out of the preview → viewer disposes cleanly; no leaked WebView; no double-mount.
- [ ] Re-open after first load → uses cached local file (no re-download).
- [ ] URL source without proper CORS headers → surfaces as a load failure with the CORS cause noted in logs (not to the user).

## Constraints

Task-specific only.

- The concrete plugin is isolated behind `Model3DView`; swapping it later must not touch call sites.
- Prefer local-file rendering (download-then-render) over remote URL for offline/CORS/perf.
- Single viewer instance; disposed on leave; memory-conscious on low-end Android.
- Loading/loaded/error/empty states are all handled; no silent blank screen.
- The viewer is AR-agnostic; AR is added by the sibling task through (or alongside) the abstraction.
- Plugin version and platform setup are verified against current pub.dev docs at implementation time.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] The processed GLB renders interactively in-app; orbit/rotate, zoom, and reset-camera work on Android and iOS.
- [ ] The renderer is accessed only through the `Model3DView` abstraction; no plugin types appear at call sites.
- [ ] The model loads from a downloaded local file (cached; no re-download on re-open); URL fallback works when CORS is configured.
- [ ] Loading (progress), loaded, error (with retry), and empty states all render correctly; no silent blank screen.
- [ ] Required Android/iOS WebView platform configuration is applied; an outdated WebView degrades to the error/fallback state, not a blank crash.
- [ ] A single viewer instance is used and disposed on leave; rapid in/out navigation leaks no WebView and does not double-mount.
- [ ] Heavy GLB on a low-end device renders where feasible or fails gracefully (no hard crash).
- [ ] `model_preview_opened`, `model_preview_loaded`, and `model_preview_load_failed` fire with correct properties.
- [ ] No raw plugin/WebView errors reach the user.
- [ ] `flutter analyze` passes; the app builds and runs on Android and iOS physical devices.
- [ ] Reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — pipeline/artifact/versioning untouched, no AR built here, abstraction boundary intact.

## Testing Instructions

1. On a physical Android and iOS device, open the preview for a processed model and confirm it renders and is interactive (orbit/zoom/reset).
2. Confirm the model loads from a local file; re-open and confirm no re-download (cache hit).
3. Toggle airplane mode with a cached model (renders) and without cache (clear message).
4. Feed an invalid/corrupt GLB and confirm the error state + retry, with no raw error shown.
5. Open with no processed model and confirm the empty state (distinct from failure).
6. Test on a device with an outdated System WebView (or simulate) and confirm the fallback/error path rather than a silent blank screen.
7. Navigate rapidly in and out of the preview and confirm the WebView disposes (no leak, no double-mount) — watch memory on a low-end device.
8. Load a heavy GLB on a low-end device and confirm graceful behavior.
9. If the URL fallback is used, test with and without proper CORS headers and confirm behavior.
10. Verify analytics. Run `flutter analyze` and device builds.

## Assumptions

Flagged because no project context file (AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md) was provided. Verify before running.

- Assumed this is **maya-viewer's Model Preview + AR Placement** phase and that the **Backend Processing Pipeline produces a GLB** artifact reachable from a completed job/version (URL or storage ref). Confirm how the GLB is exposed.
- **Plugin choice:** both candidates are `<model-viewer>`-in-WebView; the default recommendation is `flutter_3d_controller` for richer in-app control, but confirm current pub.dev status and align with the AR Placement task before committing. The abstraction keeps the decision reversible.
- Assumed a **file/download utility** exists to fetch the GLB locally. If not, add a minimal downloader; local-file rendering is preferred over remote URL.
- Assumed **AR is a separate sibling task** and will likely use a dedicated ARCore/ARKit plugin; this task keeps the viewer AR-agnostic. If instead the product wants to use the viewer's built-in AR handoff (Scene Viewer/Quick Look via `model_viewer_plus`), that shifts the plugin choice toward `model_viewer_plus` — confirm with the AR task.
- Assumed **low-end Android is in scope** (per prior phases), making local-file rendering, single-instance, and disposal important. Verify current WebView/system-browser minimums on pub.dev at implementation time.






























[[[[[[[ 12 ]]]]]]]


Generate a coding prompt for this task: Viewer gestures: one-finger rotate, two-finger pinch zoom, two-finger drag pan, double-tap reset — Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement, Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer


====================================================================================>>>>>>



# NEW: Viewer Gestures — One-Finger Rotate, Pinch Zoom, Two-Finger Pan, Double-Tap Reset
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, 3D viewer)
# Scope: New Feature / Flutter UI + 3D Viewer — gesture-driven orbit camera
# Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement
# Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Implement the 3D model viewer's gesture controls: one-finger drag ORBITS (rotates) the camera around the model, two-finger PINCH zooms, two-finger DRAG pans, and DOUBLE-TAP resets to the default framing. Gestures drive an orbit-camera controller; the 3D renderer applies the resulting camera state.

Structure it as: a PURE, testable orbit-camera controller (target, distance, azimuth/elevation, pan offset) + gesture input that updates it + the renderer that consumes it. Handle gesture arbitration (1 vs 2 pointers + double-tap) and sensible clamps.

- [ ] One-finger drag → orbit (azimuth/elevation), elevation clamped
- [ ] Two-finger pinch → zoom (distance/dolly), clamped min/max
- [ ] Two-finger drag → pan (target offset), bounded; simultaneous with pinch
- [ ] Double-tap → reset to default framing (from model bounds); distinct from tap/drag
- [ ] Gesture arbitration: 1 pointer = rotate; 2 pointers = pinch + pan together; double-tap = reset
- [ ] Pure camera controller (testable) + gesture input + renderer application (renderer interface flagged)

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The 3D viewer/renderer task (this phase) — the rendering approach (native view via platform channel, a Flutter 3D package, or custom GL) + the camera API the controller drives; the model bounds for framing
2. Screen 11 (Model Ready) — arrival into the viewer (View → the viewer with these gestures)
3. The app's gesture conventions — GestureDetector / RawGestureDetector / ScaleGestureRecognizer usage
4. Any existing camera/orbit utilities — to reuse/extend
5. The app's theming/interaction feel — sensitivity/animation conventions

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Orbit-camera controller (pure, testable)
- Model the camera as: `target` (model center), `distance` (zoom), `azimuth` + `elevation` (rotation), `panOffset` (target translation). Methods: `orbit(dAzimuth, dElevation)`, `zoom(scaleFactor)`, `pan(dx, dy)`, `reset(defaultFraming)`. Deterministic + unit-testable (no Flutter/render deps). The renderer derives the view/eye position from this state.

### Step 2: Gesture arbitration
- Use Flutter's scale gesture (ScaleGestureRecognizer / GestureDetector onScaleUpdate) which reports pointer count, scale, and focalPointDelta:
  - **1 pointer:** rotate — map focalPointDelta → `orbit(dAzimuth, dElevation)` (horizontal → azimuth, vertical → elevation), with sensitivity.
  - **2 pointers:** pinch + pan TOGETHER — `scale` delta → `zoom`; focalPointDelta → `pan`. Apply both in one update.
- **Double-tap:** a distinct recognizer → `reset()`. Ensure it doesn't conflict with single tap/drag (double-tap timing).
- Handle transitions (1↔2 pointers mid-gesture) gracefully (re-baseline the gesture reference on pointer-count change to avoid jumps).

### Step 3: Clamps + bounds
- **Elevation:** clamp (e.g. avoid going fully over the poles / under the model awkwardly) — or intentional full orbit; pick + document.
- **Zoom:** clamp min/max distance so the camera can't clip inside the model or recede to infinity (derive from model bounds).
- **Pan:** bound so the model stays reachable (don't let it pan entirely off-screen).

### Step 4: Reset to default framing
- `reset()` returns the camera to a default framing that nicely frames the model — computed from the model's bounding box (fit-to-view distance + centered target + a default angle). Optionally animate the reset (smooth) vs instant; pick + document.

### Step 5: Renderer application + lifecycle
- The renderer consumes the camera state each frame/update (apply to the 3D view's camera). Flag the renderer interface (this task provides camera state; the renderer applies it — coordinate with the viewer/rendering task). Gestures no-op until the model is loaded. Clean up gesture recognizers on dispose.

## API / Controller Contract (Dart, illustrative)

```dart
class OrbitCameraController {
  double azimuth, elevation, distance; Vector3 target, panOffset;
  void orbit(double dAz, double dEl);   // 1-finger; elevation clamped
  void zoom(double scaleFactor);        // pinch; distance clamped min/max
  void pan(double dx, double dy);       // 2-finger drag; bounded
  void reset(BoundingBox modelBounds);  // double-tap; fit-to-view framing
  CameraState get state;                // consumed by the renderer
}
// Gesture wiring: onScaleUpdate -> pointerCount==1 ? orbit : (zoom + pan); onDoubleTap -> reset
```
Rules: 1 pointer rotate, 2 pointers pinch+pan together, double-tap reset; pure testable controller; clamps (elevation/zoom/pan); reset = fit-to-view from bounds; renderer consumes state.

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT conflate one-finger and two-finger gestures — 1 pointer = rotate; 2 pointers = pinch + pan together.
- Do NOT let zoom clip inside the model or recede to infinity — clamp min/max (from bounds).
- Do NOT let pan lose the model off-screen — bound it.
- Do NOT let rotation flip awkwardly — clamp elevation (or intentional full orbit; documented).
- Do NOT couple the camera math to the renderer/gestures — a pure, testable controller + input + application.
- Do NOT make double-tap ambiguous with tap/drag — distinct recognizer + timing.
- Do NOT jump on 1↔2 pointer transitions — re-baseline the gesture reference.
- Do NOT process gestures before the model is loaded — no-op until ready.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] One-finger drag → orbit (azimuth/elevation), elevation clamped.
- [ ] Pinch → zoom, clamped at min (no clip-in) and max (no infinite recede).
- [ ] Two-finger drag → pan, bounded (model stays reachable).
- [ ] Simultaneous pinch + pan (fingers spreading AND translating) → both applied.
- [ ] Double-tap → reset to default framing (fit-to-view); distinct from single tap/drag.
- [ ] 1↔2 pointer transition mid-gesture → re-baseline, no jump.
- [ ] Very small / very large model → reset framing + zoom clamps adapt to model bounds.
- [ ] Rapid gestures → responsive, no jitter; (optional) momentum.
- [ ] Model not yet loaded → gestures no-op.
- [ ] Dispose → gesture recognizers cleaned up.

## Constraints

- Gestures MUST map: 1-finger → rotate (orbit); 2-finger pinch → zoom; 2-finger drag → pan (pinch+pan simultaneous); double-tap → reset.
- The orbit-camera controller MUST be pure/deterministic/unit-testable, separate from gesture input and the renderer.
- Clamps MUST apply: elevation (documented), zoom min/max (from bounds; no clip/infinity), pan bounds.
- Reset MUST return a fit-to-view default framing computed from the model bounds.
- Gesture arbitration MUST cleanly handle 1 vs 2 pointers, double-tap, and pointer-count transitions (no jumps).
- Gestures MUST no-op until the model is loaded; recognizers cleaned up on dispose.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] One-finger drag orbits (clamped elevation); pinch zooms (clamped); two-finger drag pans (bounded); pinch+pan work together.
- [ ] Double-tap resets to a fit-to-view default framing from the model bounds; distinct from tap/drag.
- [ ] The camera controller is pure and unit-tested (orbit/zoom/pan/reset + clamps) independent of the renderer.
- [ ] Gesture arbitration handles 1↔2 pointer transitions without jumps; gestures no-op until loaded.
- [ ] The renderer applies the camera state; recognizers are cleaned up on dispose.
- [ ] `flutter analyze` passes; unit + widget tests pass.

## Testing Instructions

1. Unit-test the controller: orbit → azimuth/elevation update + elevation clamp; zoom → distance clamp (min/max); pan → offset + bounds; reset → fit-to-view from a known bounding box.
2. One-finger drag (widget) → assert orbit; two-finger pinch → assert zoom; two-finger drag → assert pan; pinch+pan together → assert both.
3. Double-tap → assert reset to default framing; assert it doesn't fire on single tap/drag.
4. 1↔2 pointer transition mid-gesture → assert re-baseline, no jump.
5. Zoom to extremes → assert clamped (no clip-in / infinite recede); pan far → assert bounded.
6. Small vs large model → assert reset framing + clamps adapt to bounds.
7. Model not loaded → assert gestures no-op; dispose → assert recognizers cleaned up.
8. `flutter analyze` + tests → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **Renderer interface:** Assumes a 3D renderer (native view / Flutter 3D package / custom GL) that consumes camera state; this task provides the controller + gestures, the renderer applies. Confirm the rendering approach + camera API (some packages have built-in orbit controls to drive instead).
- **Model bounds:** Assumes the model's bounding box is available (for fit-to-view reset + zoom clamps). Confirm the source.
- **Gesture system:** Assumes Flutter's scale gesture (pointer count + scale + focalPointDelta) + a double-tap recognizer. Adjust if using raw pointers.
- **Clamps:** Elevation clamp vs full orbit, zoom min/max, pan bounds — defaults documented; confirm the desired feel.
- **Reset animation:** Instant vs animated — pick + document.
- **Scope:** The gesture-driven orbit camera (rotate/zoom/pan/reset) + controller + clamps. NOT the model loading/rendering (viewer/rendering task), NOT AR placement (later), NOT Screen 11 (prior).
- **Stack:** Flutter/Dart; gesture handling + a pure orbit-camera controller; renderer applies the state. Adjust to the app's structure.




























[[[[[[[[[ 11 ]]]]]]]]]


Generate a coding prompt for this task: Viewer loading skeleton + 'Loading preview...' state — Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement, Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer


=========================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>



# NEW FEATURE: Viewer Loading Skeleton + "Loading preview…" State
# Product: maya-viewer (Model Preview + AR Placement)
# Scope: New Feature (Flutter UI + 3D Viewer) — small, focused
# Priority: Medium-High

---

## Task Description

Add the **loading state** for the 3D model preview: a **skeleton placeholder** shaped like the viewer, with a **"Loading preview…"** label (and progress when available), shown while the GLB downloads and the renderer warms up. It transitions smoothly to the rendered model on success or to the error state on failure.

This consumes the existing `Model3DView` abstraction's load lifecycle (`onProgress` / `onLoaded` / `onError`) — it does not implement its own loading logic. Two qualities matter most: **no flicker** on fast loads (and a smooth fade-in of the model, not an abrupt pop), and **no infinite loading** — the state always exits to loaded or error.

- [ ] Build a skeleton placeholder matching the viewer's footprint, with a "Loading preview…" label.
- [ ] Show determinate progress when a real fraction is available (`onProgress`), otherwise an inexpensive indeterminate animation.
- [ ] Wire to the viewer lifecycle: show while loading, crossfade to the model on `onLoaded`, hand off to the error state on `onError`.
- [ ] Prevent flicker on fast loads (short delay before showing + minimum visible duration) and avoid a jarring pop-in.
- [ ] Handle slow loads so the state never spins forever with no escape.

## Files to Inspect First

1. The **`Model3DView` abstraction** (from the GLB viewer task) — its `onProgress`/`onLoaded`/`onError`; this loading state is driven entirely by them.
2. The **preview host screen** — where the viewer mounts, so the skeleton occupies the same area/footprint.
3. Existing **skeleton/shimmer/loading components** and **theme / design tokens** — reuse the app's loading pattern; don't invent a new one.
4. The **error and empty states** — this state hands off to error on failure (and must not be confused with "no model yet").
5. The **analytics service** — for optional slow-load telemetry.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Skeleton matching the viewer footprint
Build a placeholder that occupies the same size/shape as the viewer (same container/rounding), so there's no layout shift when the model replaces it. Use the app's existing skeleton/shimmer widget; keep the animation cheap (subtle) so it doesn't jank on low-end Android.

### Step 2: Label + progress
Show a **"Loading preview…"** label. If `onProgress` yields a real 0..1 fraction, show a determinate indicator; if not, use an indeterminate one. Do not fake a determinate bar when no real fraction exists.

### Step 3: Wire to the viewer lifecycle
- On mount / while loading → show the skeleton state.
- On `onLoaded` → **crossfade** from skeleton to the rendered model (a short fade), not an instant swap/pop.
- On `onError` → transition to the existing error state (with retry); do not remain in loading.

### Step 4: Anti-flicker
- Delay showing the skeleton very briefly (e.g. ~150–200ms) so an instant/cached load doesn't flash it.
- Once shown, keep it visible for a small minimum (e.g. ~300–400ms) to avoid a flash-then-vanish. Tune to the app's conventions.

### Step 5: Slow-load handling
Ensure the state always resolves: after a longer threshold with no `onLoaded`/`onError`, keep an informative message (e.g. "Still loading…") and rely on the viewer's timeout to eventually surface the error state. Never leave an indefinite spinner with no path out.

### Step 6: Accessibility
Announce the loading state (accessible label for "Loading preview…") so assistive tech conveys it; announce completion/failure via the resulting state.

### Step 7: Analytics (optional)
Optionally emit a slow-load signal for observability; otherwise rely on the viewer's `model_preview_loaded` / `model_preview_load_failed` events.

## Analytics Events (optional)

Event: `model_preview_slow_load` — fires if loading exceeds the slow-load threshold. Properties: `project_id`, `version`/`job_id`, `elapsed_ms`.

## What NOT to Change

This section is mandatory.

- Do NOT implement loading/download logic here — consume the `Model3DView` lifecycle callbacks.
- Do NOT change the viewer/plugin, the error state, or the empty state beyond showing this loading state and handing off.
- Do NOT add heavy shimmer/animation that janks on low-end Android.
- Do NOT allow an infinite loading state with no exit to loaded or error.
- Do NOT cause a layout shift or an abrupt pop when the model appears.
- Do NOT introduce new styles outside the design system.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Instant/cached load → skeleton does not flash (delay-before-show works).
- [ ] Fast load just over the delay → skeleton shows briefly but not sub-perceptibly (min-duration works).
- [ ] Normal load → determinate progress advances (if available), then crossfades to the model.
- [ ] No progress fraction available → indeterminate animation, still smooth.
- [ ] Load failure → error state (not stuck loading).
- [ ] Very slow load → informative "still loading" then eventual error via the viewer's timeout; never infinite.
- [ ] Navigate away mid-load → loading state disposes cleanly (no leaked animation/timer).
- [ ] Distinct from empty state → "loading" is not shown when there is simply no model to load.

## Constraints

Task-specific only.

- The skeleton matches the viewer footprint (no layout shift) and uses a cheap animation.
- Loading is driven solely by the viewer's `onProgress`/`onLoaded`/`onError`.
- Anti-flicker via delay-before-show + minimum-visible-duration; smooth crossfade to the model.
- Determinate progress only when a real fraction exists.
- The state always exits to loaded or error — no infinite loading.
- Accessible and low-end-friendly.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A skeleton matching the viewer footprint with a "Loading preview…" label shows while the model loads.
- [ ] Determinate progress appears when `onProgress` provides a fraction; otherwise a smooth indeterminate animation.
- [ ] On load success the model crossfades in with no layout shift or abrupt pop; on failure the error state appears.
- [ ] Fast/cached loads do not flash the skeleton; brief loads respect the minimum visible duration.
- [ ] Slow loads show an informative message and eventually resolve (to error via the viewer timeout) — never an infinite spinner.
- [ ] The loading state is announced for accessibility and disposes cleanly on navigation away.
- [ ] The animation does not jank on a low-end Android device.
- [ ] (If added) `model_preview_slow_load` fires past the threshold with correct properties.
- [ ] `flutter analyze` passes; builds/runs on Android and iOS.
- [ ] Reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — viewer/plugin, error/empty states, and the design system untouched; no infinite loading.

## Testing Instructions

1. Throttle the network so the GLB loads slowly; confirm the skeleton + "Loading preview…" and (if available) progress, then a smooth crossfade to the model.
2. Load a cached/instant model and confirm the skeleton does not flash.
3. Force a load failure and confirm transition to the error state (not stuck loading).
4. Simulate a very slow load and confirm the "still loading" message and eventual resolution via timeout — no infinite spinner.
5. Navigate away mid-load and confirm clean disposal (no leaked timer/animation).
6. Confirm no layout shift when the model replaces the skeleton.
7. On a low-end Android device, confirm the skeleton animation is smooth (no jank).
8. With a screen reader, confirm the loading state is announced.
9. Run `flutter analyze` and device builds.

## Assumptions

Flagged because no project context file (AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md) was provided. Verify before running.

- Assumed the **`Model3DView` abstraction from the GLB viewer task exists** and exposes `onProgress`/`onLoaded`/`onError`. If not yet built, this task depends on it; if the abstraction differs, wire to its actual load-lifecycle hooks.
- Assumed the app has an **existing skeleton/shimmer component and error/empty states** to reuse and hand off to. If no skeleton pattern exists, build a minimal one consistent with the design system.
- Assumed **`onProgress` may or may not** provide a reliable fraction depending on the chosen plugin; the state handles both determinate and indeterminate. Confirm what the plugin actually reports.
- Assumed a **viewer-level timeout** exists (or is added in the viewer task) to bound slow loads; this state relies on it to avoid infinite loading. If no timeout exists, add a bounded one here or coordinate with the viewer task.

# NEW FEATURE: "Share link" → Native Share Sheet + "Link copied" Toast

## Task Description

Add a **secondary "Share link" action** on the model preview screen that presents the **native OS share sheet** with the model's shareable link and shows a **"Link copied" toast** confirming the link was copied to the clipboard. The copy-to-clipboard + toast doubles as a reliable fallback so the user has the link even if they dismiss the share sheet or have no share targets.

The link shared/copied must be the **canonical public/share URL** provided by the backend — never an internal or authenticated URL containing tokens or PII. The toast must reflect the **actual** clipboard result (don't claim "copied" if it failed) and must not be obscured by the share sheet.

- [ ] Add a secondary "Share link" action (overflow ⋮ item or secondary button, per the screen's pattern).
- [ ] Resolve the model/version's shareable link from the backend/share source; guard when it isn't available.
- [ ] Copy the link to the clipboard and show a "Link copied" toast that reflects the real result.
- [ ] Present the native share sheet with the link.
- [ ] Handle unavailable link, share failure, and no share targets; guard double-tap.
- [ ] Fire analytics for the share action.

1. The **preview screen and its secondary actions / overflow (⋮) menu** — where "Share link" belongs.

3. The **existing share utility** (a `share_plus` wrapper or similar), the **clipboard utility**, and the **toast/snackbar component** — reuse these; verify the share package's current version on pub.dev at implementation time.
4. The **analytics service**.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Add the secondary action
Place "Share link" as a secondary action on the preview screen (overflow item or secondary button) using existing components. Enable it only when a shareable link is available (see Step 2).

### Step 2: Resolve the shareable link
Obtain the canonical public share URL for the current model/version from the backend/share source. If no link is available (e.g. the model isn't shareable yet / still processing / sharing disabled), the action is disabled or shows a brief explanation — never share/copy an empty or placeholder link. Verify the URL contains no auth tokens/PII (the source should already provide a clean public link; do not append credentials).

### Step 3: Copy + toast
Copy the link to the clipboard. Show a **"Link copied"** toast using the app's toast/snackbar. The toast must reflect the actual result — if the clipboard write fails, do not claim success (show nothing or an appropriate message). Sequence the toast so it is not hidden under the share sheet (e.g. show it in coordination with, or immediately around, presenting the sheet per the app's pattern).

### Step 4: Present the native share sheet
Present the OS share sheet (via the existing share utility / `share_plus`) with the link (and an optional subject/text). This is fire-and-present; do not block the UI awaiting the result. On platforms that report a share result, it may be used for analytics but is not required.

### Step 5: Failure & guard handling
- If presenting the share sheet fails or there are no share targets, the clipboard copy + toast still gives the user the link (graceful fallback).
- Guard against double-tap so only one share/copy invocation occurs per tap.

### Step 6: Analytics
Fire the events below.

- Do NOT change the viewer, loading, or error states beyond adding this action.
- Do NOT introduce new toast/button styles outside the design system.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Tertiary: 'Re-capture' → routes to Screen 4 (same project) — Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement, Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer


=================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>



# NEW: Tertiary "Re-capture" → Routes to Screen 4 (Same Project)
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client)
# Scope: New Feature / Flutter UI — re-capture entry affordance
# Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement
# Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Add a tertiary "Re-capture" action on the model viewer that routes to Screen 4 (the capture flow's start) for the SAME project, initiating a new capture that becomes a new VERSION. This is the path for a user unhappy with the reconstruction to shoot again — non-destructively (prior versions preserved).

Key behaviors:
- **Tertiary priority:** subtle/lower-emphasis vs the primary "AR Preview" (and View); not competing.
- **Same project:** carries the projectId — creates a new job/version UNDER the existing project; does NOT create a new project.
- **Prior versions preserved:** re-capture ADDS a version; the existing completed model stays in the job history. Non-destructive → no heavy confirm (optionally a light "starts a new version" inform).
- **Fresh capture session:** the new job starts the guided-capture flow anew (a new progression session); it does NOT reuse the prior job's capture data.

This ties to the versioning model: completion-update's `latestCompletedJobId`, the job history versions, and Screen 11's version chip — re-capture is how a project gains multiple versions.

- [ ] Tertiary "Re-capture" action on the viewer (subtle; not competing with AR Preview/View)
- [ ] Route to Screen 4 carrying the same projectId (same project, not a new one)
- [ ] Initiate a new job/version; preserve prior versions (history intact)
- [ ] Fresh guided-capture session (new progression session; no reuse of prior capture data)
- [ ] Non-destructive → no heavy confirm (optional light inform); clean back navigation
- [ ] Handle edges (abandon re-capture, prior job still processing)

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. Screen 4 (the capture flow's start — pre-capture/object-size/capture-start) — the navigation target; what it expects (a projectId)
2. The Model Ready (Screen 11) / viewer tasks — where this tertiary action lives (alongside AR Preview/View)
3. The project-completion-update + job-history tasks — the versioning model (latestCompletedJobId, versions); re-capture adds a version
4. The level progression controller — the guided-capture session (a new session for the new job)
5. The original job-creation flow (capture start / ingestion) — how a job is created for a capture; re-capture creates a new one consistently
6. The app's navigation + theming — tertiary-action styling; the Screen 4 route

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: The tertiary action
- Add a subtle "Re-capture" action on the model viewer (tertiary emphasis — e.g. a text button / overflow / secondary placement), clearly lower-priority than AR Preview (primary) and View.

### Step 2: Route to Screen 4 (same project)
- On tap → navigate to Screen 4 (capture start), CARRYING the projectId so the capture flow is scoped to the existing project. Do NOT create a new project. The new capture will produce a new job/version under this project.

### Step 3: New version, preserve prior
- The re-capture creates a NEW job/version under the project (consistent with how the original job was created — at capture start / ingestion). Prior versions (completed models) are PRESERVED in the job history; re-capture does not delete/overwrite them. On the new job's completion, it becomes the latest version (version chip increments; `latestCompletedJobId` updates).

### Step 4: Fresh capture session
- The new capture starts the guided-capture flow ANEW — a fresh progression-controller session for the new job (fresh per-level coverage). It does NOT reuse the prior job's capture data/photos (it's a re-capture — new photos).

### Step 5: Non-destructive UX + edges
- Non-destructive (adds a version) → no heavy destructive confirmation; optionally a light inform ("This starts a new version; your current model is kept"). Clean back navigation (cancel the re-capture → return to the viewer, prior version intact). Guard rapid taps. Handle: abandoning the re-capture (no new completed version; prior remains latest); a prior job still processing (per policy — allow/queue; confirm).

## API / Flow Contract (Dart, illustrative)

```dart
// tertiary action on the viewer:
void onRecapture() {
  // optional light inform, then:
  navigateToScreen4(projectId: currentProjectId); // same project -> new capture -> new job/version
}
// new job/version created via the capture-start/ingestion flow (consistent with the original);
// prior versions preserved in history; fresh progression session for the new job.
```
Rules: tertiary; same project (projectId carried); new version (prior preserved); fresh capture session; non-destructive (no heavy confirm); clean back nav.

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT make it primary/prominent — it's tertiary (subtle).
- Do NOT create a new project — same project (re-capture adds a version under it).
- Do NOT delete/overwrite prior versions — re-capture adds a version; history preserved.
- Do NOT reuse the prior job's capture data — fresh capture (new job, new session).
- Do NOT route to a generic capture start losing the project — carry the projectId.
- Do NOT add a heavy destructive confirmation — non-destructive; optional light inform only.
- Do NOT dead-end — clean back navigation to the viewer on cancel.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Re-capture → Screen 4 with the same projectId → fresh capture session.
- [ ] New job/version created under the project; prior versions preserved (history intact).
- [ ] New job completes → becomes latest version (chip increments; `latestCompletedJobId` updates).
- [ ] Abandon the re-capture (cancel/leave Screen 4) → no new completed version; prior remains latest.
- [ ] Prior job still processing when re-capturing → per policy (allow/queue a new one); confirm.
- [ ] Rapid/double tap → single navigation.
- [ ] Back from Screen 4 → clean return to the viewer (prior version intact).
- [ ] Fresh session → no leakage of the prior job's coverage/photos.

## Constraints

- The action MUST be tertiary (subtle), not competing with AR Preview/View.

## Task Description

Build Screen 11A: the AR preview foundation — a full-screen ARCore (Android) / ARKit (iOS) camera view with an initialized AR session (camera feed background + world tracking + plane-detection foundation). This is the base AR experience the "AR Preview" button navigates to; MODEL PLACEMENT (putting the reconstructed model into the scene) is a LATER task — this delivers the session + view, not the placement.

Key concerns:
- **Full-screen AR camera view:** the device camera feed rendered full-screen with AR tracking (ARCore/ARKit), via the AR plugin or a native platform-view/texture.
- **AR session lifecycle:** AR is resource-heavy — init/start on entry, PAUSE on background, RESUME on foreground, STOP/DISPOSE on leave (no camera/session leak, no battery drain).
- **Tracking + plane detection (foundation):** world tracking + plane detection running; surface tracking-state feedback (limited/normal, "move to detect surfaces").

- **Platform-correct:** ARCore (Android) / ARKit (iOS).

- [ ] Full-screen ARCore/ARKit camera view with an initialized AR session
- [ ] Session lifecycle: init/start, pause (background), resume (foreground), stop/dispose (leave) — no leak
- [ ] World tracking + plane detection running; tracking-state feedback + guidance
- [ ] Graceful runtime session-init/tracking failure (fall back to viewer, no crash)
- [ ] Platform-correct (ARCore/ARKit); full-screen view
- [ ] Placement / reticle / model-in-scene explicitly OUT of scope (next AR task)

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

## Implementation Instructions

- Render the AR camera view full-screen via the AR plugin / native AR platform-view. Initialize the AR session (ARCore on Android, ARKit on iOS) with world tracking + plane detection enabled. Platform-correct configuration.

### Step 2: Session lifecycle (resource management)
- Start/resume the session on screen entry (view ready). PAUSE the session when the app is backgrounded (WidgetsBindingObserver) and when leaving the screen; RESUME on foreground. STOP/DISPOSE on leave (release the camera + session). AR is resource-heavy — no leaks, no background camera/tracking, no battery drain.

### Step 3: Tracking + plane detection (foundation)
- Run world tracking + plane detection. Surface the TRACKING STATE: normal vs limited (poor lighting / fast motion / insufficient features) with guidance ("move your phone slowly to detect surfaces" / limited-tracking hint). Detected planes are the foundation for the LATER placement task (feedback like a subtle plane/reticle is optional here; full placement is next).

### Step 4: Graceful runtime failure
- Even with the entry gate, handle runtime failures: session init fails, AR becomes unavailable, tracking can't start, camera permission revoked mid-session → show a clear message and fall back to the viewer; do NOT crash or hang on a black screen.

### Step 5: Minimal overlay + navigation
- Minimal overlay UI: a close/back to return to the viewer (dispose the session), and tracking-state guidance. Placement controls (place, move, scale the model) are the LATER task. Full-screen camera; overlay is minimal.

## Component / Lifecycle Contract (Dart, illustrative)

```dart
class ArPreviewScreen extends StatefulWidget {
  final ModelRef model; // passed from the AR Preview button (for the LATER placement task)
}
// State: WidgetsBindingObserver
//  initState/view-ready -> arSession.start() (world tracking + plane detection)

//  dispose -> arSession.stop()/dispose()  (release camera + session)
//  tracking-state stream -> normal | limited(reason) -> guidance
//  init/tracking failure -> message + pop to viewer
```
Rules: full-screen ARCore/ARKit session; lifecycle (start/pause/resume/stop/dispose); tracking + plane detection + state feedback; graceful runtime failure; placement out of scope.

[[[[[[[ 5 ]]]]]]]


Generate a coding prompt for this task: AR: plane detection → surface found toast → Place / Move / Reset controls — Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement, Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer


=============================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>




# NEW FEATURE: AR Placement — Plane Detection → "Surface found" Toast → Place / Move / Reset
# Product: maya-viewer (Model Preview + AR Placement)
# Scope: New Feature (Flutter UI + 3D Viewer) — AR, device-only
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Add an **in-app AR placement** experience for the processed model: enter AR, detect a real-world surface (plane), show a **"Surface found"** toast when one is detected, and let the user **Place** the model on it, **Move** it, and **Reset**. This is true in-app AR (ARCore/ARKit), not the OS Scene-Viewer/Quick-Look handoff — it requires a plane-detection AR plugin.

Because AR needs capable hardware and permissions, the feature must be **optional and gracefully degradable**: unsupported devices and denied permissions get a clear fallback (the normal 3D preview), never a broken screen. AR is a **real-device** feature — emulators/simulators can't run it.

- [ ] Add an AR entry point from the preview screen, gated by an AR-support check and camera permission.
- [ ] Start an AR session with plane detection and show scanning guidance until a plane is found.
- [ ] On first plane detected → show a **"Surface found"** toast and enable **Place**.
- [ ] **Place** the model on the detected plane (anchored); **Move** it along the plane; **Reset** to remove/re-scan.
- [ ] Provide contextual controls (Place enabled after a surface is found; Move/Reset after placement) and handle tracking loss.
- [ ] Dispose the AR session on exit; keep it memory-conscious (single model node).
- [ ] Fire analytics across the AR flow.

## Files to Inspect First

1. The **model preview screen** and its actions — where the "View in AR" entry point belongs, and how it hands the model to AR.
2. The **model/GLB source** — prefer the viewer's cached **local GLB file**; the AR plugin loads GLB from a local file or URL.
3. Any **existing AR module / camera permission handling** — reuse if present; check for existing ARCore/ARKit setup in the platform projects.
4. The **theme / toast / button components** — for the "Surface found" toast and the Place/Move/Reset controls.
5. The **analytics service**.
6. **pub.dev / GitHub** for the current, **maintained** AR plugin variant and its setup — this ecosystem is community-maintained and forked; verify at implementation time.

## Plugin Selection (decide, then isolate behind an AR module)

- **Recommended:** `ar_flutter_plugin` (or its currently-maintained fork, e.g. an `ar_flutter_plugin_*` variant — verify on pub.dev). It bridges **ARKit (iOS)** and **ARCore (Android)** in one codebase and provides exactly what this task needs: plane detection, anchors, node placement, gesture-based move/rotate/scale, and GLB loading from a local file or URL, under an MIT license.
- **Caveat:** it's community-maintained; confirm the maintained variant supports the project's Flutter/OS versions before committing.
- **Fallback:** if no single cross-platform plugin is healthy enough, use `arcore_flutter_plugin` (Android) + `arkit_plugin` (iOS) behind a common interface — at the cost of two native paths.
- **Isolation:** encapsulate all AR-plugin usage behind an `ArPlacement` module/screen with a small interface (support-check, start/stop session, place/move/reset, event callbacks). The rest of the app depends on the module, not the plugin — so the plugin choice/fork is swappable and the AR code stays contained.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Support check + permission (gate the entry)
Before entering AR:
- Check **AR availability** (ARCore supported/installed on Android; ARKit-capable device on iOS). If unsupported → do not show/enable AR; offer the normal 3D preview with a brief explanation.
- Request **camera permission**. If denied → clear message with a path to settings; do not enter AR.

### Step 2: Start session with plane detection
Enter the AR screen and start the session with **plane detection** enabled (horizontal at minimum; add vertical if the product wants it). Show **scanning guidance** ("Move your phone slowly to find a surface") and a reticle/indicator if the plugin/design supports it. Keep the guidance visible until a plane is detected.

### Step 3: Surface found → toast + enable Place
On the **first plane detected**, show the **"Surface found"** toast (once — do not spam on every plane update) and transition the controls so **Place** becomes enabled. Update the reticle to indicate a placeable surface.

### Step 4: Place
On **Place** (tap the surface / reticle position), add the model as a node **anchored** to the plane at that location. Load the model from the local GLB (preferred) or URL. Enforce a **single** placed model (placing again either repositions or replaces — pick one and be consistent). After placement, enable **Move** and **Reset**.

### Step 5: Move
**Move** repositions the placed model along the detected plane (drag-to-move / tap-to-relocate via the plugin's gesture/translation support). Keep it anchored to a valid plane; do not allow it to drift off tracked surfaces. (Rotate/scale gestures may be enabled too if the plugin supports them and the design wants them — optional.)

### Step 6: Reset
**Reset** removes the placed model (and its anchor) and returns to the scanning state (guidance shown, Place disabled until a surface is found again). Reset must fully clean up nodes/anchors so repeated place/reset cycles don't leak or accumulate.

### Step 7: Tracking loss & exit
- On **tracking loss / session interruption**, show guidance to re-scan; recover gracefully when tracking resumes.
- On **exit**, stop and **dispose** the AR session/controller (a known crash source if not disposed) and release the camera; return to the preview.

### Step 8: Analytics
Fire the events below.

## Platform Configuration (required; verify against the chosen plugin's current docs)

- **Android:** `CAMERA` permission; ARCore declaration (`com.google.ar.core` metadata) and the `android.hardware.camera.ar` feature — mark AR **not required** if the app must still run on non-AR devices, and check availability at runtime; `minSdkVersion` per ARCore (24+). 
- **iOS:** `NSCameraUsageDescription`; enable the ARKit capability; set the minimum iOS version per the plugin/ARKit.
- Confirm exact requirements on pub.dev at implementation time (they vary by plugin/fork).

## Analytics Events

Match existing conventions.

- `ar_entered` — `project_id`, `version`/`job_id`.
- `ar_unsupported` — `project_id`, `reason` (`no_arcore` / `no_arkit` / `device`). (Fires when AR is attempted/gated off.)
- `ar_permission_denied` — `project_id`.
- `ar_surface_found` — `project_id` (first plane detected).
- `ar_model_placed` — `project_id`.
- `ar_model_moved` — `project_id` (coarse; not per-frame).
- `ar_reset` — `project_id`.
- `ar_exited` — `project_id`, `placed` (bool).

## What NOT to Change

This section is mandatory.

- Do NOT make AR mandatory — unsupported devices and denied permissions must fall back to the normal 3D preview, never a broken/blank screen.
- Do NOT leak the AR plugin's types outside the `ArPlacement` module.
- Do NOT change the processed GLB, the viewer, or the model generation.
- Do NOT leave the AR session/controller undisposed on exit (crash/memory risk); always dispose and release the camera.
- Do NOT allow multiple accumulating model nodes/anchors; enforce a single placed model and full cleanup on Reset.
- Do NOT expose raw plugin/AR errors to the user; map to friendly guidance.
- Do NOT introduce new toast/button styles outside the design system.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Device without ARCore/ARKit support → AR entry hidden/disabled with explanation; normal preview offered; `ar_unsupported`.
- [ ] ARCore not installed (Android) → prompt/guide per the plugin (install/update), or fall back gracefully.
- [ ] Camera permission denied → clear message + settings path; no AR entry; `ar_permission_denied`.
- [ ] No surface found yet → scanning guidance persists; Place stays disabled; no premature "surface found."
- [ ] Poor lighting / featureless surface → plane not found; guidance remains; no crash.
- [ ] Surface found toast → shown once, not repeated on every plane update.
- [ ] Tracking lost after placement → guidance to re-scan; model/anchor handled gracefully; recover on resume.
- [ ] Repeated Place/Reset cycles → no node/anchor leak or accumulation.
- [ ] Exit mid-session (back / app background) → session disposed, camera released, no crash; re-enter starts clean.
- [ ] Low-end but AR-capable device → single node, disposed session, acceptable performance; if it can't sustain AR, fail gracefully.
- [ ] GLB unavailable locally → fetch or fall back; do not enter AR with no model.

## Constraints

Task-specific only.

- AR is optional and device-gated; graceful fallback to the 3D preview when unsupported or permission-denied.
- All AR-plugin usage is isolated behind the `ArPlacement` module (swappable plugin/fork).
- Prefer the local GLB file for the AR model.
- Single placed model; full node/anchor cleanup on Reset; session disposed on exit.
- Controls are contextual: Place after a surface is found; Move/Reset after placement.
- "Surface found" toast fires once per detection transition, not per update.
- Verify the maintained plugin variant and platform setup on pub.dev at implementation time.
- Real-device feature — automated UI tests can't cover the AR session; unit-test the control/state logic behind the module interface.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] From the preview, an AR entry appears only on AR-capable devices with camera permission; unsupported/denied cases fall back to the 3D preview with a clear message.
- [ ] Entering AR starts a session with plane detection and shows scanning guidance until a plane is found.
- [ ] The first detected plane shows the "Surface found" toast once and enables Place.
- [ ] Place anchors the model on the plane; Move repositions it along the plane; Reset removes it and returns to scanning.
- [ ] Only one model is placed at a time; repeated place/reset cycles leave no leaked nodes/anchors.
- [ ] Tracking loss shows guidance and recovers gracefully; exiting disposes the session and releases the camera with no crash.
- [ ] The AR plugin is used only through the `ArPlacement` module; no plugin types leak to the app.
- [ ] No raw AR errors reach the user; all failure modes have friendly guidance.
- [ ] Analytics fire across the flow (`ar_entered` through `ar_exited`, plus unsupported/denied).
- [ ] Required Android/iOS AR + camera configuration is applied; the app still runs (non-AR) on unsupported devices.
- [ ] `flutter analyze` passes; the app builds and runs on physical Android and iOS devices; unit tests for the control/state logic pass.
- [ ] Reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — viewer/GLB untouched, AR optional, session disposed, single node, module boundary intact.

## Testing Instructions

AR requires **physical devices**; emulators/simulators cannot validate the session.

1. On an AR-capable Android (ARCore) and iOS (ARKit) device, open the model and enter AR; grant camera permission; confirm scanning guidance.

8. Induce tracking loss (cover camera / move fast) and confirm re-scan guidance and recovery.
9. Exit mid-session and background the app; confirm session disposal, camera release, and a clean re-entry.
10. On a low-end AR-capable device, confirm acceptable performance or graceful failure. Verify analytics. Run `flutter analyze`, unit tests, and device builds on both platforms.
