Generate a coding prompt for this task: AR: reticle pulse animation on surface found — Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement, Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer


================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>




# NEW: AR — Reticle Pulse Animation on Surface Found
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, AR)
# Scope: New Feature / Flutter UI + 3D Viewer — AR reticle + feedback animation
# Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement
# Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Add the AR reticle — the placement cursor rendered on a detected surface — with a PULSE animation when a surface is found. The reticle shows WHERE the model will be placed; the pulse is the "ready to place here" feedback the moment a placeable surface appears under the device's aim. This builds on Screen 11A's AR session (hit-test + plane detection) and is the precursor to placement (placing the model at the reticle is the NEXT task).

Key behaviors:
- **Reticle on surface (hit-test):** positioned via an AR hit-test (ray from screen center into the scene, intersecting a detected plane), ORIENTED to the surface, FOLLOWING the aim as the device moves.
- **States:** searching (no plane under the aim → reticle hidden / searching hint) → found (plane appears → reticle shown + pulse) → maintained (follows the aim) → lost (back to searching).
- **Pulse on found:** a scale/opacity pulse triggered on the FOUND transition (attention/"ready"), DEBOUNCED so rapid found/lost flicker doesn't spam it.
- **Approach:** a 3D reticle node in the AR scene (placed at the hit pose, oriented to the plane) is the robust default; a 2D projected overlay is an alternative — flag.

- [ ] Reticle positioned via AR hit-test on a detected plane; oriented to the surface; follows the aim
- [ ] States: searching (hidden) → found (shown + pulse) → maintained (follows) → lost (searching)
- [ ] Pulse animation on the found transition (scale/opacity), debounced against flicker
- [ ] Robust approach (3D node oriented to the plane vs 2D overlay) — flagged
- [ ] Leans on Screen 11A's session (hit-test/plane detection); precursor to placement (next task)
- [ ] Smooth, performant (no AR framerate hit); clean when the surface is lost/screen left

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. Screen 11A (AR session foundation) — the session, hit-test API, plane detection, tracking state this builds on
2. The AR plugin (ar_flutter_plugin/arcore+arkit, or native AR view) — hit-test + node/overlay APIs for the reticle
3. The (next) AR placement task — the reticle is its precursor (the model places at the reticle pose); align the reticle pose output
4. The app's animation conventions (AnimationController / implicit animations) — for the pulse (if a 2D overlay) or the AR renderer's animation (if a 3D node)
5. The app's theming — the reticle's visual style

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Reticle positioning (hit-test)
- Each frame/update, run an AR hit-test from the screen center (or the device aim) into the scene. If it intersects a detected plane → get the hit POSE (position + orientation). Position the reticle at that pose, ORIENTED to the plane (lie flat on the surface). The reticle follows the aim as the device moves.
- If no plane is hit → the reticle is hidden (searching state).

### Step 2: States + transitions
- **Searching:** no plane under the aim → reticle hidden (optionally a subtle "searching for a surface" hint).
- **Found:** a plane becomes hit (transition from searching → found) → show the reticle + play the PULSE.
- **Maintained:** while a plane is hit → the reticle follows the aim (no repeated pulse).
- **Lost:** plane no longer hit → back to searching (reticle hidden).

### Step 3: Pulse animation
- On the FOUND transition, play a pulse — e.g. the reticle scales up + fades (a ripple), or a quick scale bounce — to signal "ready to place." Define one-shot on found (attention) vs a gentle continuous pulse while placeable (choose + document; one-shot-on-found is the leaner default).
- DEBOUNCE: rapid found/lost transitions (jittery tracking at an edge) must NOT spam the pulse — only pulse on a stable found transition (e.g. a short hysteresis / minimum found duration).

### Step 4: Reticle approach
- Prefer a 3D reticle NODE in the AR scene (placed at the hit pose, oriented to the plane) — robust to perspective/orientation; the pulse animates the node's scale/opacity. Alternative: a 2D overlay widget positioned at the projected hit-point (simpler but less orientation-accurate). Flag the choice; align with the AR plugin's capabilities.

### Step 5: Precursor to placement + performance + cleanup
- The reticle pose is the placement point for the NEXT task (the model places at the reticle). Expose/prepare the current reticle pose for it. Keep it smooth/performant (no AR framerate hit — reuse the node, don't recreate per frame). Hide/clean up the reticle when the surface is lost or the screen is left.

## Component / Behavior Contract (Dart, illustrative)

```dart
enum ReticleState { searching, found }
class ArReticle {
  // per-frame: hitTest(screenCenter) -> plane pose? 
  //   pose != null && was searching -> state=found, playPulse() (debounced)
  //   pose != null -> position reticle at pose (oriented to plane), follow aim
  //   pose == null -> state=searching, hide reticle
  Pose? get currentPose; // placement point for the NEXT task
}
// pulse: scale/opacity animation on found transition; debounced against flicker
```
Rules: hit-test positioning (oriented, follows aim); searching→found→maintained→lost states; pulse on found (debounced); 3D-node-vs-2D-overlay flagged; precursor pose for placement; smooth + clean up.

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT build model placement here — the reticle + pulse is the precursor; placing the model is the next task.
- Do NOT show the reticle when no surface is under the aim — hidden/searching.
- Do NOT spam the pulse on jittery found/lost — debounce (stable found transition only).
- Do NOT misposition/mis-orient the reticle — use the hit-test pose, oriented to the plane, following the aim.
- Do NOT tank AR framerate — reuse the reticle node; don't recreate per frame; efficient animation.
- Do NOT leave the reticle when the surface is lost / the screen is left — hide/clean up.
- Do NOT hard-couple to an undecided rendering approach — flag 3D-node vs 2D-overlay.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] No surface under aim → reticle hidden (searching).
- [ ] Surface found (searching→found) → reticle shown + pulse (once, debounced).
- [ ] Surface maintained → reticle follows the aim, oriented to the plane; no repeated pulse.
- [ ] Surface lost → back to searching (reticle hidden).
- [ ] Rapid found/lost (edge jitter) → debounced; no pulse spam.
- [ ] Multiple planes → the aimed/nearest plane's pose.
- [ ] Reticle orientation → aligned to the plane normal (lies flat).
- [ ] Screen left / session paused → reticle cleaned up.
- [ ] Limited tracking (from 11A) → reticle hidden / searching until tracking recovers.

## Constraints

- The reticle MUST be positioned via the AR hit-test on a detected plane, oriented to the surface, following the device aim.
- It MUST implement searching→found→maintained→lost states; the pulse MUST play on the found transition and be debounced against flicker.
- The reticle approach (3D node vs 2D overlay) MUST be chosen deliberately (3D node preferred) and aligned with the AR plugin.
- The reticle pose MUST be available as the placement point for the next task.
- It MUST be smooth/performant (no AR framerate hit; reuse the node) and cleaned up on surface-lost / screen-left.
- It MUST build on Screen 11A's session (hit-test/plane detection); placement MUST be out of scope.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A pulse plays on the found transition, debounced so rapid found/lost doesn't spam it.
- [ ] States (searching/found/maintained/lost) behave correctly; the reticle cleans up on surface-lost / screen-left.
- [ ] The reticle pose is exposed as the placement point for the next task; placement is not implemented here.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: AR: haptic medium on Place, light on Reset — Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement, Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer


========================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>



# NEW FEATURE: AR Haptics — Medium Impact on Place, Light Impact on Reset
# Product: maya-viewer (Model Preview + AR Placement)
# Scope: New Feature (Flutter UI + 3D Viewer) — small, focused
# Priority: Low-Medium

---

## Task Description

Add tactile feedback to the AR placement controls: a **medium-impact** haptic when the model is **successfully placed**, and a **light-impact** haptic when it is **reset**. Feedback fires on the **successful completion** of each action — not on the raw button tap — so a failed Place (no valid surface) or a Reset with nothing placed produces no buzz.

Use Flutter's `HapticFeedback` (`mediumImpact()` for Place, `lightImpact()` for Reset), fire-and-forget (non-blocking), respecting the user's system/app haptic settings and degrading silently where haptics are unavailable.

- [ ] Fire a **medium-impact** haptic on a successful Place.
- [ ] Fire a **light-impact** haptic on a successful Reset.
- [ ] Trigger on action success, not on tap of a failed/no-op action.
- [ ] Respect system/app haptic settings; silent no-op where unsupported; non-blocking.

## Files to Inspect First

1. The **AR Place/Move/Reset handlers** (from the AR Placement task) — specifically the points where a Place is confirmed successful (model anchored) and a Reset actually clears the model. Haptics hook there.
2. Any **existing haptics helper / feedback utility** in the app — reuse it for consistency; if none, add a tiny wrapper.
3. Any **user setting for haptics/accessibility** (e.g. a reduce-haptics/feedback preference) — respect it if present.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Hook haptics to successful actions
- On a **successful Place** (model anchored to a plane) → `HapticFeedback.mediumImpact()`.
- On a **successful Reset** (model/anchor actually removed) → `HapticFeedback.lightImpact()`.
Do not fire on the button tap itself if the action does not succeed (e.g. Place attempted with no valid surface, or Reset with nothing placed).

### Step 2: Route through a small helper
Call haptics via the app's existing feedback helper, or add a tiny wrapper (e.g. `Haptics.placed()` / `Haptics.reset()`) so the impact-level mapping lives in one place. Fire-and-forget — do not `await` in a way that delays the AR action or UI.

### Step 3: Respect settings and degrade gracefully
Respect the system haptic setting and any in-app haptics/accessibility preference (skip when disabled). On devices without haptics, or where the platform doesn't distinguish medium vs light, the call is a silent no-op — never an error.

### Scope note
Only **Place (medium)** and **Reset (light)** are in scope per the task. Do **not** add haptics to Move or other controls unless separately requested.

## What NOT to Change

This section is mandatory.

- Do NOT change the AR placement/reset logic — only add feedback on their successful completion.
- Do NOT fire haptics on failed or no-op taps.
- Do NOT add haptics to Move or other actions beyond the spec.
- Do NOT block or delay the AR action awaiting haptics.
- Do NOT force haptics when the user/system has them disabled.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Place tapped with no valid surface (fails) → no medium haptic.
- [ ] Reset tapped with nothing placed (no-op) → no light haptic.
- [ ] System haptics disabled or unsupported device → silent no-op, no error.
- [ ] Platform can't distinguish medium vs light → best-available feedback, no crash.
- [ ] Rapid repeated Place/Reset → one haptic per successful action; no spamming.
- [ ] In-app reduce-haptics preference on (if present) → no haptics.

## Constraints

Task-specific only.

- Haptics fire on successful action completion, not raw taps.
- Mapping is exact: Place → medium impact, Reset → light impact.
- Non-blocking (fire-and-forget); never delays the action or UI.
- Respects system and any in-app haptic settings; silent where unsupported.
- Only Place and Reset are affected.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A successful Place produces a medium-impact haptic; a successful Reset produces a light-impact haptic.
- [ ] Failed Place and no-op Reset produce no haptic.
- [ ] Haptics respect system/app settings and are a silent no-op on unsupported devices (no error).
- [ ] Haptics never block or delay the AR action; no double-fire on rapid actions.
- [ ] Move and other controls are unaffected.
- [ ] `flutter analyze` passes; builds/runs on physical Android and iOS devices.
- [ ] Reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — AR logic untouched, no haptics on failures/other actions.

## Testing Instructions

Physical devices (haptics don't fire on emulators/simulators).

1. Successfully place the model and confirm a medium-impact haptic; reset and confirm a lighter haptic (distinct where the hardware allows). Test on Android and iOS.
2. Attempt Place with no valid surface and confirm no haptic; tap Reset with nothing placed and confirm no haptic.
3. Disable system haptics and confirm no haptic and no error/crash; if the app has a haptics preference, toggle it off and confirm the same.
4. Rapidly place/reset and confirm one haptic per successful action (no spam) and no UI delay.
5. Confirm Move produces no haptic (unchanged).
6. Run `flutter analyze` and device builds.

## Assumptions

Flagged because no project context file (AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md) was provided. Verify before running.

- Assumed the **AR Placement task exists** with identifiable success points for Place (anchored) and Reset (cleared) to hook. If those handlers don't expose a clear success signal, hook the haptic at the point the state actually transitions.
- Assumed **`HapticFeedback` (flutter/services)** is the intended mechanism (`mediumImpact`/`lightImpact`). If the app standardizes on a different haptics utility, route through that with the equivalent intensities.

- Assumed **only Place and Reset** get haptics per the task; Move is intentionally excluded.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: AR: screenshot button (optional, nice-to-have) — Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement, Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer


============================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>



# NEW: AR — Screenshot Button (Optional, Nice-to-Have)
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, AR)
# Scope: New Feature / Flutter UI + 3D Viewer — optional AR screenshot
# Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement
# Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer
# Priority: Low (optional / nice-to-have)

---

## Task Description

Add an optional screenshot button in the AR view that captures the AR scene — the real-world camera feed AND the rendered model composited together, as the user sees it — and saves/shares it. Explicitly a NICE-TO-HAVE: keep it simple and degrade gracefully if unsupported.

THE technical trap: capture the COMPOSITED AR frame (camera + rendered model) via the AR plugin's SNAPSHOT API — NOT a Flutter `RepaintBoundary`. AR views are native platform-views/textures; a Flutter widget capture will NOT include the native AR content (it'd grab an empty/transparent layer). Use the plugin's snapshot.

Also: saving to the gallery needs the media/storage permission (reuse P2; handle denial → share-only); exclude the UI chrome (reticle/buttons) for a clean AR shot; give capture feedback; hide/disable gracefully if the snapshot API is unavailable.

- [ ] Screenshot button in the AR overlay
- [ ] Capture the COMPOSITED AR frame (camera + model) via the AR plugin's snapshot API (NOT RepaintBoundary)
- [ ] Save to gallery (media/storage permission via P2; handle denial) and/or share (share sheet)
- [ ] Exclude UI chrome (reticle/buttons) — clean AR scene
- [ ] Capture feedback (shutter/flash + saved/shared confirmation)
- [ ] Graceful degradation if the snapshot API is unavailable (hide/disable; no crash)

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. Screen 11A (AR session) + the reticle/placement tasks — the AR view this overlays; the plugin snapshot API
2. The AR plugin (ar_flutter_plugin/arcore+arkit, or native AR view) — its snapshot/capture method (the composited-frame capture)
3. The P2 permission tasks (media/storage / photos) — the gallery-save permission flow (request/denial/settings)
4. A save/share utility (image_gallery_saver / gal, share_plus) — saving to the gallery + the share sheet
5. The app's theming — the button + capture-feedback styling

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: The button
- Add a screenshot/camera button to the AR overlay (unobtrusive; it's a nice-to-have). Enabled when the AR snapshot API is available; hidden/disabled if not.

### Step 2: Capture the composited AR frame (the trap)
- On tap, capture the COMPOSITED AR frame via the AR plugin's SNAPSHOT API (renders the camera feed + the AR-rendered model into an image). Do NOT use a Flutter `RepaintBoundary` — it won't capture the native AR platform-view content. The plugin snapshot typically excludes the Flutter overlay (so the reticle/buttons aren't in the shot) — confirm and ensure the UI chrome is excluded for a clean AR image.

### Step 3: Save / share
- **Save to gallery:** requires the media/storage (photos) permission — request via the P2 flow; on denial → messaging + fallback to share-only; on permanent denial → the P2 settings-prompt.
- **Share:** offer the share sheet (share_plus) with the captured image.
- Provide save and/or share per the desired UX (confirm).

### Step 4: Feedback
- Give capture feedback: a shutter/flash animation and a "saved"/"shared" confirmation (snackbar/toast). Keep it lightweight.

### Step 5: Graceful degradation + non-disruption
- If the AR plugin has no snapshot API (or it fails) → hide/disable the button; do NOT crash (nice-to-have). Capturing MUST NOT disrupt the AR session/tracking (capture the current frame without pausing/breaking AR). Guard rapid taps (debounce).

## Component / Flow Contract (Dart, illustrative)

```dart
// AR overlay screenshot button:
Future<void> onScreenshot() async {
  final image = await arSession.snapshot();      // plugin snapshot: composited camera + model (NOT RepaintBoundary)
  if (image == null) { /* unavailable -> disable/hide */ return; }
  if (await ensureMediaPermission()) { await saveToGallery(image); showSaved(); }
  else { await share(image); }                    // fallback: share-only
}
// button visible only if arSession.supportsSnapshot
```
Rules: composited capture via the plugin snapshot (not RepaintBoundary); save (P2 media permission) and/or share; exclude UI chrome; feedback; graceful degradation; non-disruptive; debounced.

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT capture only the Flutter overlay or only the camera — capture the COMPOSITED AR frame (camera + model) via the plugin snapshot.
- Do NOT use a Flutter `RepaintBoundary` for the AR platform-view — it won't capture native AR content.
- Do NOT save to the gallery without the media/storage permission — request via P2; handle denial (share-only).
- Do NOT include the UI chrome (reticle/buttons) in the shot — clean AR scene.
- Do NOT crash if the snapshot API is unavailable — hide/disable gracefully (nice-to-have).
- Do NOT disrupt the AR session on capture — capture the current frame without breaking tracking.
- Do NOT double-capture on rapid taps — debounce.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Capture success → composited frame saved (gallery) and/or shared; feedback shown.
- [ ] Snapshot API unavailable → button hidden/disabled; no crash.
- [ ] Media permission denied → share-only fallback + messaging; permanent denial → settings-prompt.
- [ ] Capture during limited tracking → captures the current frame (whatever's shown).
- [ ] Model not placed yet → captures the camera scene (allow) or gate until placed (confirm the UX).
- [ ] Rapid taps → debounced (single capture).
- [ ] Large image → handled (save/share without memory issues).
- [ ] iOS vs Android → platform-correct snapshot + gallery save.
- [ ] Capture must not pause/break the AR session.

## Constraints

- The capture MUST be the COMPOSITED AR frame (camera + model) via the AR plugin's snapshot API — NOT a Flutter `RepaintBoundary`.
- Saving to the gallery MUST use the media/storage permission (P2 flow; denial → share-only; permanent denial → settings).
- The shot MUST exclude the UI chrome (reticle/buttons) — clean AR scene.
- It MUST degrade gracefully if the snapshot API is unavailable (hide/disable; no crash) and MUST NOT disrupt the AR session.
- Capture MUST be debounced; feedback MUST be shown on success.
- As a nice-to-have, it MUST stay simple and never destabilize the AR experience.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] The screenshot button captures the composited AR frame (camera + model) via the plugin snapshot (not RepaintBoundary).
- [ ] The image saves to the gallery (with the media permission) and/or shares; denial falls back to share-only.
- [ ] The shot excludes UI chrome (clean AR scene); capture feedback is shown.
- [ ] If the snapshot API is unavailable, the button is hidden/disabled (no crash); capture doesn't disrupt AR.
- [ ] Rapid taps are debounced; iOS/Android platform-correct.
- [ ] `flutter analyze` passes; the app builds on both platforms; mockable parts tested.

## Testing Instructions

1. Tap screenshot with the model placed → assert the plugin snapshot is used (composited camera + model), NOT a RepaintBoundary; assert the UI chrome is excluded.
2. Media permission granted → assert saved to gallery + confirmation; denied → assert share-only fallback + messaging; permanent denial → settings-prompt.
3. Snapshot API unavailable (mock) → assert the button hidden/disabled, no crash.
4. Capture during limited tracking / before placement → assert it captures the current frame (or gates per the UX).
5. Rapid double-tap → assert single capture.
6. Assert the AR session isn't paused/broken by the capture.
7. iOS vs Android → assert platform-correct snapshot + save.
8. `flutter analyze` + build + tests → clean/pass.

## Assumptions

- **Nice-to-have:** Optional/low priority; keep it simple; degrade gracefully; never destabilize AR.
- **Plugin snapshot (KEY):** Assumes the AR plugin provides a snapshot API capturing the composited frame (camera + rendered model). If it doesn't, the feature degrades (hidden). Confirm the plugin's capability.
- **Composited capture:** Assumes the composited AR frame is wanted (camera + model), excluding the Flutter UI chrome. Confirm.
- **Save + share:** Assumes save-to-gallery (media/storage permission via P2) and/or share (share_plus). Confirm the desired combination.
- **Pre-placement capture:** Confirm whether a screenshot is allowed before the model is placed (camera-only scene) or gated until placement.
- **Scope:** The optional AR screenshot button (composited capture, save/share, feedback, graceful degradation). NOT the AR session (11A), NOT placement (adjacent), NOT the camera-permission internals (P2, reused; media permission for saving).
- **Stack:** Flutter/Dart + AR plugin snapshot API + P2 media permission + save/share utility. Adjust to the app's structure.






























[[[[[[[[ 1 ]]]]]]]]



Generate a coding prompt for this task: AR fallback: if device doesn't support AR → show 3D viewer only, hide AR button — Phase: Model Preview + AR Placement, Track: Flutter UI + 3D Viewer


==========================================================================================================>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>



# NEW FEATURE: AR Fallback — Unsupported Device → 3D Viewer Only, Hide AR Button
# Product: maya-viewer (Model Preview + AR Placement)
# Scope: New Feature (Flutter UI + 3D Viewer) — small, focused
# Priority: Medium-High

---

## Task Description

Detect whether the device supports AR and, when it does **not**, show the **3D viewer only** and **hide** the AR entry button. AR is an optional enhancement; the model preview must be fully usable on devices that can't run AR (a real slice of the low-end target). This task provides the **single authoritative AR-capability check** that the AR button — and any other AR affordance — consumes.

Two behaviors matter: the AR button is **hidden** (not merely disabled) when unsupported, and because the capability check is **asynchronous** (ARCore availability on Android is a query), the button must default to hidden until the check resolves so it never **flashes in and then disappears**.

- [ ] Add a centralized, async AR-capability check (ARCore on Android / ARKit on iOS) as the single source of truth.
- [ ] Show the 3D viewer regardless of AR support.
- [ ] Hide the AR button while the check is pending and when AR is unsupported; show it only once support is confirmed.
- [ ] Handle Android's "supported but not installed / transient" states distinctly from true "unsupported."
- [ ] Cache the result to avoid re-querying and flicker.
- [ ] Fire analytics on the capability outcome.

## Files to Inspect First

1. The **preview screen and the AR entry button** (from the AR Placement task) — where visibility is gated.
2. The **AR module / plugin's availability API** (from the AR task) — the ARCore/ARKit capability query to wrap; centralize it here so the AR task's entry consumes this check rather than re-deriving it.
3. The **3D viewer** — confirm it renders independently of AR (it must always be available).
4. The **analytics service**.

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Centralize the capability check
Add a single async capability check (e.g. `ArCapability.check() -> ArSupport`) returning a clear result:
- `supported` — ARCore supported+installed (Android) / ARKit-capable device (iOS).
- `notInstalled` — Android: ARCore supported but not installed / needs update (soft case).
- `unsupported` — device cannot do AR (no ARCore support; non-ARKit device; simulator/emulator).
- `checking` — result pending.
Wrap the AR plugin's availability API; on Android handle the transient availability state by re-querying until resolved. This is the one place AR support is determined.

### Step 2: Always show the 3D viewer
Render the 3D viewer unconditionally — it does not depend on AR support. No AR-related state blocks or hides the preview.

### Step 3: Gate the AR button (default hidden)
Drive AR button visibility from the capability result:
- `checking` → hidden (do not show then hide — avoid flicker).
- `unsupported` → hidden. No error/empty UI for the absence of AR; it is simply not offered.
- `supported` → shown.
- `notInstalled` → per the product decision (see Assumptions): either hide (treat like unsupported) or show with an install/update prompt. Default to hide unless the product wants the install path.

### Step 4: Cache the result
Cache the capability result for the session (or longer if appropriate) so it isn't re-queried on every rebuild/navigation and the button doesn't flicker. Re-check only when it makes sense (e.g. returning from settings after an ARCore install, if the install path is used).

### Step 5: Analytics
Fire the event below once per resolved check.

## Analytics Events

Match existing conventions.

Event: `ar_capability_resolved`
Trigger: when the capability check resolves.
Properties:
  - `result`: string — `"supported"` / `"not_installed"` / `"unsupported"`
  - `platform`: string — `"android"` / `"ios"`

## What NOT to Change

This section is mandatory.

- Do NOT make the 3D viewer depend on AR support — it always renders.
- Do NOT disable the AR button when unsupported; **hide** it.
- Do NOT show the AR button before the capability check resolves (no flash-then-hide).
- Do NOT scatter capability checks — the AR button and AR entry consume this single check.
- Do NOT change the AR placement logic; this only gates entry visibility.
- Do NOT show an error/empty state for the absence of AR; it's simply not offered.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Unsupported device (no ARCore / non-ARKit / simulator) → AR button hidden, 3D viewer fully works.
- [ ] Capability check pending → AR button hidden until resolved (no flicker).
- [ ] Android ARCore transient availability → re-query until a definitive result.
- [ ] Android ARCore supported-but-not-installed → per decision (hide or install-prompt); default hide.
- [ ] Rapid navigation / rebuilds → cached result reused; no repeated queries or flashing button.
- [ ] Capability API error/timeout → treat as unsupported (fail safe: hide the button), no crash.
- [ ] Returning after installing ARCore (if the install path is used) → re-check and reveal the button.

## Constraints

Task-specific only.

- One authoritative, async-safe, cached capability check; all AR affordances consume it.
- AR button hidden while checking and when unsupported; shown only when confirmed supported.
- The 3D viewer is always available, independent of AR.
- No flicker (button never appears before the check resolves).
- Absence of AR is silent — no error UI.
- Fail safe: an errored/timed-out check hides the AR button.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] On an unsupported device, the AR button is hidden and the 3D viewer works fully.
- [ ] The AR button appears only after the capability check confirms support — it never flashes in then disappears.
- [ ] The capability check is centralized, async-safe, and cached; the AR entry (from the AR task) consumes it rather than re-deriving support.
- [ ] Android transient/not-installed states are handled distinctly from true unsupported, per the chosen decision.
- [ ] A capability check error/timeout fails safe (button hidden), no crash.
- [ ] No error/empty UI appears for the absence of AR.
- [ ] `ar_capability_resolved` fires once with the correct `result`/`platform`.
- [ ] `flutter analyze` passes; builds/runs on Android and iOS (including an unsupported target such as the iOS simulator).
- [ ] Reviewed against "What NOT to Change" — viewer independent of AR, button hidden (not disabled), single check, AR logic untouched.

## Testing Instructions

1. On an **unsupported** target (iOS simulator, or a non-ARCore Android device/emulator) open the preview: confirm no AR button and a fully working 3D viewer.
2. On a **supported** device: confirm the AR button appears only after the check resolves (watch for any flash-then-hide during load) and that AR entry works.
3. On Android with **ARCore not installed**: confirm the chosen behavior (hidden by default, or an install prompt if that path is enabled).
4. Navigate in/out of the preview repeatedly and confirm the cached result is reused (no repeated queries, no flickering button).
5. Force the capability API to error/timeout and confirm the button is hidden (fail safe) with no crash.
6. If the install path is used, install ARCore and return; confirm the button reveals on re-check.
7. Verify `ar_capability_resolved` fires with correct properties. Run `flutter analyze` and device builds.

## Assumptions

Flagged because no project context file (AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md) was provided. Verify before running.

- Assumed the **AR Placement task exists** with an AR button/entry to gate and a plugin availability API to wrap. This task centralizes that check so the AR entry consumes it; if the AR task already has an inline check, replace it with this single source of truth.
- **not-installed decision:** the task says "doesn't support AR → hide." Assumed genuine unsupported hides the button. For Android **supported-but-not-installed**, confirm whether to also hide (default) or show an install/update prompt — this is a softer case than true unsupported.
- Assumed the 3D viewer is **fully independent of AR** and always renders. Confirm no shared state couples them.
- Assumed capability can be determined via the AR plugin's availability API (ARCore `checkAvailability` semantics on Android, device/OS capability on iOS). Verify the exact API for the chosen plugin/fork.
- Assumed **failing safe = hiding** the AR button (better to omit AR than to offer a broken entry). Confirm this is the desired bias.
